### Offer

While an assistant LEFT turn is streaming (the in-flight
`POST .../messages` SSE, including Regenerates and widget /
approve / connect replies), offer **Stop** (Grok Bot feel).
Chrome: a **12px / 12pt** muted **Stop** chip at the bottom
of the chat column (above **Jump to latest** if both show).
Hide Stop when idle. Composer stays focused. Do not put Stop
in the Send slot.

### Abort

Click/tap **Stop** is a **client abort** of that in-flight
stream/request (`AbortController` / SSE disconnect). Stop
appending tokens. Do **not** add a cancel HTTP route (no
`POST /cancel`). OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**.

### Partial

Keep the partial assistant LEFT text that already arrived.
Stop appending tokens. The partial LEFT stays as **completed**
(v0.48 multi-bubble split applies). Do not wipe the bubble.
Do not drop the optimistic user-RIGHT from that Send. Do not
GET-replace the transcript after Stop (a mid-stream GET would
miss the unpersisted partial).

### After stop

Composer is usable again (`busy` / `isSending` clear) and
stays focused. Hide Stop when idle. No auto-restart of that
turn. A later Send is a new turn.

### Stick / multi-bubbles / optimistic Send

v0.47 stick-to-bottom stands. v0.48 completed LEFT
`kind=message` multi-bubbles stand (a stopped partial is
treated as completed for split / Copy / Speak / Regenerates).
v0.49 optimistic user-RIGHT on Send stands (Stop is not a
4xx/5xx; do not restore the composer or show Couldn't send.).

## 70. v0.64 — per-chat composer drafts (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Never reintroduce `computerPane.ts`.
Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched. Clients never speak MCP
or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL unchanged.

### Composer lock

Unsent composer text is kept **per conversation**, keyed by
agentId + threadId (`null` thread = the top-level timeline).
In memory for the app session only. No new storage.

On loadConversation — selecting a chat, Jump, closing a
thread, New agent — save the current text under the old key
and load the new key's text, or empty when that chat has
none. Caret moves to the end. Focus behavior is unchanged
(desktop still focuses where it already did; iOS still does
not force the software keyboard up).

Successful Send clears **that** conversation's draft. A failed
Send puts the text back on the conversation it was typed in,
not whichever chat is open now. Attachments still clear on
switch, as before. Dictation and Speak cancel the same way
they do today.

chips; the text now returns to its own chat). v0.50 Stop
stands. v0.51 waiting ··· stands. v0.52 streaming caret
stands. v0.53 Esc=Stop stands. v0.54 same-turn 6px / 6pt gap
stands. v0.55 Send muted while generating stands (the

## 71. v0.65 — bubble timestamps on hover / long-press (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged.

### Timestamp lock

Each chat bubble — user-right and each assistant LEFT
`kind=message` bubble, including each short multi-bubble —
shows that message's existing `createdAt`. Tool / widget /
approve / connect / handoff cards are not bubbles.

Local time. Today is `h:mm AM` (hour 1–12, no leading zero;
minutes always two digits; `AM` / `PM`). Any other local
calendar day prefixes the date: `Oct 8, 3:04 PM`. A
different year includes the year: `Oct 8, 2025, 3:04 PM`.
Missing or unparseable `createdAt` draws nothing.

Hide the label while that bubble is still streaming, so it
never sits on the caret. After complete / Stop, the time
can show.

Desktop: a 12px muted label, `position: absolute` (no
layout shift), `pointer-events: none` so it does not take
clicks from the bubble text, Copy, Regenerate, or the
caret. Opacity 0 until the bubble is hovered or
keyboard-focused (`:hover` and `:focus-visible`), then a
120ms opacity fade. Reduce Motion on: `transition: none`
(the label still appears, with no fade). Copy / Speak /
Regenerate stay on their row under the last bubble.

iOS: long-press context menu. The time is a
non-actionable section header (`Text`, not a button)
beside Copy / Speak / Regenerate when those controls
already show. The inline action row stays. A user bubble
with none of those actions still reveals the time as a
disabled, non-actionable row. Streaming passes no stamp,
so the menu does not show a time. No custom fade — the
system menu presents it.

### Prior slices

v0.47 stick-to-bottom stands. v0.48 completed LEFT

## 73. v0.67 — day separators (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second

Jump to latest is already on main: the desktop chip and the
iOS `ScrollViewReader` snap, freeze when the reader scrolls
up, and re-arm at the bottom. This slice does not change
that. The closest missing chat-fluency piece is a day line
so a reader who has scrolled up can see which day they are
in without hovering each bubble.

### Day line lock

Above the first painted transcript row of each local
calendar day, show one muted **12px / 12pt** line:

- same local day as now: **Today**
- the previous local day: **Yesterday**
- 2 through 6 local days ago: the weekday (`Wednesday`)
- any other day this year: `MMM d` (`Oct 2`)
- a different year: `MMM d, yyyy` (`Oct 8, 2025`)

The first dated row in the transcript gets a line, including
when that day is today. Later rows on that same local day
do not repeat it. A missing or unparseable `createdAt` is
skipped and does not move the boundary. A collapsed tool
line is not painted, so it does not start a day; the next
painted row of that day gets the line instead.

The line is in the transcript flow, centered, full width of
the column. It is not a bubble, not a button, and not a
toast. It stays visible while that bubble is streaming.
Hover timestamps (v0.65) are unchanged.

No animation. Reduce Motion does not add a fade.

Desktop: a paragraph with `role="heading"` and
`aria-level={2}`. The accessible name is the day text
(`Today`, `Yesterday`, the weekday, or the date).

iOS: the same text, secondary color, header trait, above
that row inside the existing transcript stack.

## 74. v0.68 — recall the last sent message (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged.

Up-arrow in the composer already moves the `@` mention menu
and the `/` skill menu. It does not recall a sent message.
This slice adds that, for the open transcript only (the
current agent 1:1, or the open channel timeline / thread).

### Recall lock

When the composer text is empty (length 0) and IME is not
composing, Up-arrow with no modifier chord copies the user's
most recent sent `kind=message` that has text into the
composer. The text is an editable draft. The caret is at
the end. Attachment-only user rows (empty content), tool
lines, and assistant bubbles are skipped. If there is no
such message, Up-arrow does nothing.

Do not recall when the composer already has text, including
whitespace, or while IME composition is active. Mention and
skill menus still consume ArrowUp while they are open.

The recall stays armed until:

- Escape restores the composer to empty (caret at the start).
  While generating, Esc still Stop (v0.53) and does not clear
  the draft. IME, dictation cancel, an open mention or skill
  menu, takeover, and a pending widget / approve / connect
  card still own Escape. Jump (v0.60) runs only when this
  Escape did not clear a recall.
- Clearing the field (the text becomes empty) ends the
  recall. Edits that leave text stay armed, so a later
  Escape still restores empty.
- Send, or switching chats, ends the recall. A recalled
  string that is still in the field is an ordinary per-chat
  draft (v0.64) after a switch. A failed Send restores text
  the same way it does today and does not leave the recall
  armed.

### iOS

No hardware arrow is assumed. Long-press the user's own
most recent bubble: the v0.65 timestamp menu stays (the
time is still a non-actionable header or disabled row,
beside Copy / Speak / Regenerate when those already show),
and gains **Edit as new message**. Choosing it puts that
bubble's text into the composer, caret at the end, and
focuses the field. Older user bubbles do not grow this
item.

Hardware Up-arrow, via `UIKeyCommand` and the composer's
key press, uses the same empty-composer / IME-idle rule as
desktop. It does not move the caret once the field has text.

## 75. v0.69 — per-chat drafts persist (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged.

v0.64 stores unsent composer text per agentId + threadId
(`null` thread = top-level timeline) and restores it when
that chat is opened again. That map lived only in memory, so
relaunch dropped every draft. On iPad the sidebar list writes
`selectedAgentID` before the chat adopts the new id, so the
open text could be saved under the chat being entered.

### Draft lock

The composer owner is the chat the text was typed in, not
whichever id the sidebar has already selected. Leaving a
chat saves the current text under the old key and loads the
new key's text, or empty when that chat has none. The caret
moves to the end. Focus behavior is unchanged.

The map is written locally on each edit, switch, Send, and
failed Send. Desktop uses `localStorage` key
`snorlax.chatDrafts`. iOS uses `UserDefaults` under the same
key. A relaunch reads that blob and shows the draft for the
chat being opened. Corrupt or non-object blobs are ignored.
Empty text removes that chat's slot. Successful Send clears
that conversation's slot. A failed Send writes the text back
onto the conversation it was typed in.

### Recall

v0.68 Up-arrow (and iOS Edit as new message) still runs only
when the composer length is 0 and IME is idle. A restored
draft is non-empty, so recall does not replace it. Switching
chats disarms recall. Escape still clears an armed recall
and still Stops while generating. A recalled string left in
the field is an ordinary persisted draft after a switch or
a relaunch.

## 76. v0.70 — unread chat dot (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged.

Channel handoff already paints a 6px accent dot on a channel
row, in memory, when a 1:1 turn reports back. That dot is
unchanged. This slice marks any roster row when an assistant
reply finishes off that chat.

### When it marks

A finished reply is a non-empty assistant LEFT `kind=message`
(text or an attachment). User bubbles, `kind=tool` /
`widget` / `approve` / `connect`, and an empty assistant
reply do not mark. Stop aborts the stream and does not mark.

The mark is taken from the in-flight stream's `message.done`
for the chat that Send (or Regenerate, or a card reply)
started, including when the user has since switched away.
Mark when that chat is not the one on screen, or when the
app/window is not focused. A focused view of that same chat
does not mark.

Desktop: the active chat is on screen. The window is focused
when it has focus and the document is visible. iPad: the
detail selection is on screen. iPhone: the pushed chat is on
screen; the list (empty navigation path) has no open chat, so
a reply that finishes there marks the chat it belongs to.

### Clearing and persistence

Opening a chat clears its reply dot. Becoming focused while
that chat is already on screen clears it too, because the
reply is in front of you. Deleting a chat drops its id.
Handoff unread is not cleared by focus and is not written to
this set.

The ids persist locally. Desktop uses `localStorage` key
`snorlax.chatUnread` (a JSON array of chat ids). iOS uses
`UserDefaults` under the same key. A relaunch shows the dots
that were still unread. Corrupt or non-array blobs are
ignored. The roster dot is the existing 6px / 6pt accent
circle with the accessible name Unread. One dot if handoff
unread and reply unread both apply.

## 77. v0.71 — in-chat find (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged.

Find looks only at messages already loaded in the open chat.
It does not fetch older history and it does not add a route.

### Desktop

Cmd-F or Ctrl-F, with a chat open and no settings sheet,
profile sheet, or computer takeover in front, shows a small
find bar above the transcript. The field is labeled Find in
chat. Matches in the loaded transcript highlight. Enter and
the down button move to the next match. Shift-Enter and the
up button move to the previous. The movement wraps. The bar
shows **n of m** (1-based). No matches reads **0 of 0**. Esc
closes the bar and clears the highlights. While the bar is
open, that Esc does not Stop, Jump, or clear a recalled
draft. Mention / skill menus, takeover, and dictation cancel
still own Esc. A later Esc uses the existing Stop / recall /
Jump rules. Switching chats closes the bar. Desktop does not
hide non-matching messages.

### iOS

The chat toolbar magnifying glass opens the same search.
User, assistant, and handoff messages whose text does not
contain the query are hidden. Tool lines, widgets, approve
cards, and connect cards stay. Matches highlight. Enter and
the down button move forward. Shift-Enter and the up button
move back. The count is **n of m**. Esc closes and clears
highlights, and does not Stop or Jump. An empty result reads
**No matches**.

### What is searched

Case-insensitive substring match over painted transcript
text: user bubbles, assistant bubbles, handoff ask text, and
painted tool lines (including a live tool line that is on
screen). A collapsed tool line is not painted, so it is not
in the set until it is expanded. Widget, approve, and
connect chrome is not searched. A whitespace-only query
matches nothing. The count and the highlights use that same
loaded text.

## 78. v0.72 — quick chat switcher (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce

The switcher looks only at agents and channels already in
the roster. It does not fetch and it does not add a route.

### Desktop

Cmd-K or Ctrl-K, with no settings sheet, profile sheet,
computer takeover, create-channel sheet, skill / plugin /
routine sheet, delete confirm, or dictation cancel in
front, shows a small overlay. The field is labeled Switch
chat and the placeholder is Search chats. An empty query
lists every chat in roster order, with the open chat
highlighted. Typing fuzzy-filters by name: case-insensitive
subsequence, consecutive hits and word starts rank higher,
ties keep roster order. Arrow Down and Arrow Up move the
highlight and wrap. Enter switches to the highlighted chat
and closes the overlay. Enter on the chat that is already
open just closes. Esc closes without switching. While the
overlay is open, that Esc does not Stop, Jump, close find,
or clear a recalled draft. Mention / skill menus, takeover,
and dictation cancel still own Esc when the overlay is
closed. A later Esc uses the existing find / Stop / recall /
Jump rules. No matches reads **No chats**.

### iOS

The chat list has a pull-down search field, placeholder
Search chats, accessibility label Switch chat. The same
fuzzy filter narrows the rows. An empty query shows the
full list. No matches reads **No chats**. Choosing a row
opens that chat the same way as before. Create-channel
member picking still sees the full roster.

## 79. v0.73 — keyboard shortcuts help (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged.

The overlay and the sheet only name shortcuts and gestures
that already have handlers. They do not add a send path, a
find path, or a switch path.

### Desktop

Cmd-/ or Ctrl-/ (not Shift, not Alt, not both modifiers)
opens a small dialog titled Keyboard shortcuts. `?`, and
Shift-/ when the event key is still `/`, opens it when
focus is not in an input, textarea, select, or
contentEditable. A text field still types the question
mark. The chord works from the composer and does not insert
`/`. Opening is skipped while computer takeover, Settings,
the info pane, create-channel, a skill / plugin / routine
sheet, a delete confirm, or dictation cancel is in front.
Pressing the chord or `?` again closes it.

The rows are the handlers already on main:

- Enter sends. Shift Enter stays a newline.
- Esc stops while generating, closes find, closes the
  switcher, closes mention / skill menus, clears a recalled
  draft, jumps to latest when frozen, or cancels dictation.
- Up-arrow in an empty composer recalls the last user message.
- Cmd/Ctrl-F finds in the open chat.
- Cmd/Ctrl-K opens the quick switcher.
- Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-I opens info.
- Cmd/Ctrl-V pastes an image or file.
- While find is open, Enter and Shift-Enter move between matches.
- While the switcher is open, Up / Down / Enter move and open.
- While a mention or skill menu is open, Up / Down / Enter / Tab
  move and insert.

Esc while the dialog is open closes it in the capture phase
and does not Stop, Jump, close find, close the switcher, or
clear a recall. IME keeps Esc. A click on the backdrop
closes it; a click inside the dialog does not. A later Esc
uses the existing switcher / find / Stop / recall / Jump
rules.

### iOS

The chat toolbar has a control labeled Gestures & shortcuts.
It presents a sheet. Gestures: long-press a message for
Copy, Speak, Regenerate, or the time; long-press your own
latest message for Edit as new message; long-press an
approve command to copy it. Hardware keyboard: Return
sends, Shift-Return is a newline, Esc stops / closes find /
clears a recall / jumps, Up-arrow recalls when the composer
is empty, Cmd-F finds, and Return / Shift-Return in the
find field move between matches. Those keys are the existing
`UIKeyCommand` Escape and Up-arrow, the find
`.keyboardShortcut`, and the composer Return presses. iOS
has no Cmd-K chord; the chat-list search stays the switcher.
Done dismisses the sheet.

## 80. v0.74 — per-chat reading place (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged.

Assistant Copy and Regenerate already exist (v0.31). This
slice does not add a message Copy or Retry control.

The place is session memory. It is not written to
localStorage or UserDefaults. A relaunch opens at the latest
messages.

### Desktop

Leaving a chat records whether the transcript was within
~64px of the bottom, the same slack as stick-to-bottom. Near
the bottom, the saved anchor is empty. Further up, the anchor
is the topmost visible transcript row (a message, a handoff,
or the day line above that message). Coming back: a missing
place, a near-bottom place, or an anchor that is no longer in
the transcript opens at the latest messages and stays armed.
A parked anchor scrolls so that row meets the top of the
transcript, disarms stick, and does not follow the stream.
If the tail message id changed while the chat was away, Jump
to latest shows. Send, Regenerate, and Jump still snap to the
bottom and re-arm. Find closes when the chat changes, before
the place is restored. A switch that is still loading does
not overwrite another chat's place.

### iOS

The same rule. The chat publishes the top visible message
and whether the scroll is within ~64pt of the bottom. A
switch locks publishing until that transcript is restored, so
the outgoing scroll is not saved as the incoming chat.
Coming back scrolls the parked message to the top, or to the
bottom when the place was near the bottom or the anchor is
gone. A new tail shows Jump to latest. Send, Regenerate, and
Jump still bump stick and snap to the bottom. Find closes
when the open chat or thread changes.

light the composer. Esc while the overlay is showing clears
it and does not Stop or Jump. Nothing is sent. Send stays
muted while generating.

## 82. v0.76 — New messages divider (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged.

The line is client state. It rides the v0.70 unread condition
and the transcript you already have. It does not change
stick-to-bottom, Jump, or the v0.74 reading place.

### Desktop

Each chat, including a channel thread, stores the id of the
last message you viewed while that chat was open and the
window was focused (`localStorage` key `snorlax.chatLastSeen`).
Opening the chat, or focusing the window again, looks for the
first painted LEFT assistant message after that id and draws
one thin muted rule labelled **New messages** immediately
above it. The rule is 12px `--text-muted`, with a 1px
`--border` line on each side. It is computed once and stays
on that message while the chat remains open, including while
a reply you are watching streams in. Switching away and
coming back with nothing newer clears it. Send clears it.
A first visit, with no stored id, draws no line and records
the tail. User-RIGHT bubbles, collapsed tool lines, and
tool / widget / approve / connect rows do not anchor it.
When a day separator applies to the same message, the day
line is first, then this rule. No animation.

### iOS

The same rule in the chat transcript, persisted in
UserDefaults under `snorlax.chatLastSeen`. The chat list is
not an open chat. Becoming active again recomputes the line
from the frozen id. Send clears it. A day separator stays
above the rule. The row is static.

### Prior slices

v0.74 per-chat reading place stands. v0.75 drag files onto

## 83. v0.77 — unread count outside the chat list (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.

The count is the v0.70 unread-dot set (chats with a finished
assistant reply the reader has not opened). Channel handoff
unread stays its own in-memory dot and does not add. No new
storage key. Empty ids do not count.

### Desktop

When the count is one or more, the window title is
`(2) Snorlax-Bot` (the number is the count). The macOS dock
and the Linux taskbar badge show that same number where
Tauri `set_badge_count` supports it. Windows has no count
badge in that API; the title still shows the number. When
nothing is unread, the title is `Snorlax-Bot` and the badge
is cleared. The shell command is best-effort for the badge.
A browser preview still sets `document.title`.

### iOS

The chat list title (iPhone stack and iPad sidebar) uses the
same string. The open chat keeps the agent's name. The app
icon badge is set to that count, or cleared at zero, only
when notification authorization is already `.authorized` and
the badge setting is enabled. A status of not-determined
would show a blocking prompt, and provisional authorization
does not enable the badge, so the app does not call
`requestAuthorization`. If permission is missing, the list
title still shows the count and the icon is left alone.

stands.

## 84. v0.78 — retry a failed send (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged.

A Send that fails before any reply stays in the transcript.
That covers a network error, the runtime being down, an HTTP
4xx/5xx on the message post, and an SSE `error` that arrives
before any assistant token, tool, or card. inference_unavailable
is that kind of error: the wire body is the message text, so
any pre-reply error keeps the bubble. Stop / Esc abort is not
a failed send. The partial LEFT reply stays as it does today.

The user bubble is muted (opacity 0.55, no fade). Under it, a
12px muted line reads **Couldn't send · Retry**, with **Remove**
beside it. The line is in normal flow under that bubble only.
Reduce Motion sets no transition. Nothing retries on a timer.

Retry uses the existing send path for that same chat: the same
text, the same attachment ids, and the same mentions. It reuses
the local bubble id so a second bubble does not appear. While
a turn is in flight, Retry is disabled. On success the bubble
reconciles to the server row and the reply streams as usual.
Remove drops the local bubble. A user row the runtime already
saved before the error stays hidden for this session, so the
transcript does not show that row and the muted bubble together.
There is no delete-message route; the hide is not persisted.

iOS: tap Retry, or long-press the bubble for Retry / Remove.
The Gestures & shortcuts sheet names that long-press.

Switching chats keeps the failed record for the session and
paints the bubble back onto the loaded transcript. It is not
written into the composer. v0.64 still keeps each chat's unsent
draft across a switch, and Send still clears that draft.

### Prior slices

v0.49 optimistic user-RIGHT bubble stands. v0.64 per-chat
drafts stand, except a failed Send no longer restores its text
into the composer.

## 85. v0.84 — composer auto-grows with typed text (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.

Typing or pasting text that wraps or contains a newline grows
the composer with that text. The cap is about 8 lines,
**160px** on desktop and **160pt** on iOS (inside 160–200).
Past the cap the field scrolls inside instead of growing.
Deleting text shrinks the field. Clearing it returns the
empty single-line height the composer already uses. A single
line that still fits does not change that height.

Pending attachment chips stay above the field. The row stays

Jump, per-chat drafts (including the persisted copy), and
Up-arrow recall stay. A recalled or restored draft sizes the
field to that text. Nothing in this slice animates the
height: Reduce Motion or not, the change is instant.

## 88. v0.87 — Backspace removes the last attachment chip (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged.

User-RIGHT Copy already sits on a settled user bubble (v0.86).
This cut does not add another Copy control, paste path, or
day line. Pending chips still come from the paperclip, drop,
and clipboard paste (v0.25 / v0.30 / v0.75). The chip X still
removes that chip on click or tap.

When the composer text is empty and focus is in the field,
plain Backspace (desktop) or Delete (iOS) removes the last
pending chip: the rightmost, most recently added. One chip
per keypress, including key repeat. It does not clear the
rest. It does not send. Focus stays in the composer.

Any text stays with the field, including a single space or
newline. The key that deletes the last character does not
also remove a chip. The next press, once the field is empty,
removes one chip. IME marked text keeps Backspace. Shift,
Alt, Cmd, and Ctrl chords do not remove a chip.

No chips and an empty field does nothing. It does not
navigate away, clear a per-chat draft, or send. A draft that
still has text is edited by Backspace as usual.

iOS hardware Delete is `keyboardDeleteOrBackspace`. The
on-screen keyboard Delete key uses the same
`deleteBackward` path, so it matches. Forward delete is not
this key. Takeover still owns the keyboard. Send stays
muted while generating; this key still only removes a chip
and does not send.

## 89. v0.88 — Unread-only filter (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second

A compact All / Unread control sits under the desktop sidebar
title and above the iOS chat list. All is the default and
shows every chat. Unread shows only chats that currently
paint the v0.70 6px accent dot. That is a reply-unread chat,
or a channel whose handoff unread shares that same dot. A
chat with neither is hidden. An empty Unread list shows a
muted **No unread chats**.

Opening a chat still clears its unread the way v0.70 does.
Once the dot is gone, that row leaves the Unread list. The
transcript stays open. Switching back to All shows every
chat again, including ones with no dot.

The choice persists across a restart. Desktop stores
`all` or `unread` in localStorage under
`snorlax.chatListFilter`. iOS stores the same key in
UserDefaults. Missing or unknown values stay All.

Relative time and the last-message preview stay on each
row that is still listed. The v0.77 unread count outside
the list still counts the v0.70 reply-unread set only.
The dots themselves do not move or change size. Cmd-K /
Ctrl-K still lists every chat. On iOS, the name search
narrows whichever set All or Unread is showing. When
Unread is empty, the empty state is **No unread chats**
even if a name query is typed.

attachment chip stands.

## 90. v0.89 — Pin a chat to the top (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged.

Right-click a sidebar chat or agent row, or use the small
hover control on that row, to **Pin** or **Unpin**. Pinned
rows sit in a **Pinned** group at the top of the sidebar, in
the order they were pinned. A subtle 12px pin glyph stays on
a pinned row. Unpinned rows keep the roster order below that
group. An empty pin list shows no group header.

iOS: a leading swipe and a long-press on the chat list offer
the same **Pin** / **Unpin**. Pinned rows use the same group
and a pin glyph. The trailing Delete swipe stays.

Pins persist across a restart. Desktop stores the ordered id
list in localStorage under `snorlax.chatPins`. iOS stores the
same key in UserDefaults. Missing or corrupt values are an
empty list. Deleting a chat drops its pin. A chat that leaves
the roster on a successful refresh drops its pin too. Opening
a chat does not unpin it.

Pinned rows still show the v0.70 unread accent dot, the
v0.80 last-message preview, and the v0.83 relative time. All
/ Unread still applies to the whole list. Unread shows only
chats that currently paint the accent dot, and any of those
that are pinned stay in the pinned group. An empty Unread
list still reads **No unread chats**.

Cmd-K / Ctrl-K still lists every chat. On iOS, the name
search still narrows the All or Unread set, including chats
that are not pinned. Matches that are pinned stay in the
Pinned group.

## 91. v0.90 — Mark a chat unread (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.

unchanged.

Right-click a sidebar chat or agent row. A read row offers
**Mark as unread**. A row that already shows the v0.70
unread accent dot offers **Mark as read**. The dot is the
one a finished reply paints, and the one a channel handoff
shares.

Mark as unread writes that chat into the existing unread
set (`snorlax.chatUnread`). The same 6px accent dot appears
on the row. The v0.77 count outside the chat list includes
it (desktop window title and dock or taskbar badge; iOS
chat-list title and the icon badge when permission is
already granted). The row shows under the v0.88 Unread
filter. Mark as read removes it from that set. If the dot
was a channel handoff unread, Mark as read clears that
in-memory handoff too, so the dot leaves the row. A handoff
dot alone does not add to the outside count; clearing it
does not change that count.

Opening the chat still clears the unread, the same way a
reply-unread already clears. Coming back to a focused
window while that chat is on screen clears it too. No new
storage key. Desktop local storage and iOS UserDefaults
keep the set across a restart.

iOS: a trailing swipe and a long-press on the chat list
offer the same **Mark as unread** / **Mark as read**. The
leading Pin swipe stays. Delete stays on the trailing swipe.

Pinned rows stay pinned. Pin order does not change. A
pinned chat you mark unread stays in the Pinned group, and
under Unread it stays in that group. Last-message preview
and relative time stay on the row.

the chat list stands. v0.88 Unread-only filter stands.

## 92. v0.91 — Draft hint in the chat list (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.

A chat-list row other than the one currently open, whose
top-level composer still holds unsent text from the v0.64 /
v0.69 per-chat drafts, replaces the v0.80 last-message
preview with that draft. The line starts with a muted-accent
**Draft:** prefix, then the draft text. The text is one
line: whitespace collapses, and it truncates the same way
the last-message preview does (about 120 characters, then
the row ellipsizes). Whitespace-only drafts do not count,
so the row keeps the last-message preview. An empty draft,
including after Send clears it, does the same.

The draft is the null-thread slot for that chat: the
conversation the row opens. A thread draft does not replace
the channel row, the same rule as the v0.80 preview. No new
storage key. Desktop reads `snorlax.chatDrafts`. iOS reads
the same key in UserDefaults.

The open chat's row keeps its normal last-message preview
even while that composer has unsent text. On desktop the
open row is the selected chat. On iPad it is the selected
chat. On iPhone it is the chat on the navigation stack; the
list itself has no open chat, so a draft you just left
shows the hint.

The v0.83 relative time stays beside the line and still
follows the latest message. The v0.70 unread dot, the v0.88
Unread filter, v0.89 pins, and v0.90 Mark as unread / Mark
as read stay. A draft does not change which rows are unread
or pinned.

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second

Alt+Up / Alt+Down opens the previous or next chat. On macOS
that chord is Option+Up / Option+Down. The order is the
sidebar's current visible order. The v0.89 Pinned group is
first, in the order those chats were pinned. Unpinned rows
keep their roster order below. The v0.88 All / Unread filter
applies before that grouping. All walks every chat. Unread
walks only chats that currently paint the v0.70 accent dot.
The name search on iOS, and Cmd/Ctrl-K, do not change this
order. The quick switcher still lists every chat.

There is no wrap. On the first visible row, Alt+Up stays
there. On the last visible row, Alt+Down stays there. If the
open chat is not in the visible list (a read chat while
Unread is selected), the chord does not jump to another row.

The chord does not fire while an IME composition is active.
Plain Up-arrow still recalls the last sent message when the
composer is empty (v0.68). Mention and skill menus still own
plain Up / Down. In-chat find keeps Enter / Shift-Enter and
its up / down controls; while find is open this chord does
not switch chats. While the quick switcher is open, its own
arrows still move the highlight. Shortcuts help, takeover,
settings, and the image lightbox keep the keyboard too.

Opening the neighbor is the same as clicking or tapping that
row. Unread clears. The v0.69 draft for that chat comes
back. The v0.74 reading place comes back. The composer is
focused. A handoff thread closes, the same way the row opens
the top-level chat.

Desktop listens on the window. iOS uses a hardware-keyboard
`UIKeyCommand` in the chat composer (Option+Up / Option+Down)
and the same chord when the chat view has a hardware key
and the composer is not first responder. The v0.73 shortcuts
overlay lists **Alt ↑ / Alt ↓** as **Previous or next chat**.
The iOS Gestures & shortcuts sheet lists **Option ↑ / Option ↓**
with the same action.

## 94. v0.93 — Sidebar generating indicator (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged.

While an assistant reply the client started is in flight,
that chat's row in the sidebar, and the same row on the iOS
chat list, shows a small muted **`···`**. The dots use the
same visual language as the v0.51 waiting dots: three middle
dots, a pulse when Reduce Motion is off, and static muted
dots when Reduce Motion is on (v0.58). They sit in the v0.83
relative-time slot, in place of that time, beside the
preview. The time returns when the reply settles.

The v0.80 preview may read a muted **Typing…** for that
whole reply. Typing… is display-only. It does not replace
the stored last-message line. When the reply settles, the
preview is the last message again, or a draft hint when
that rule applies.

The window is the whole client stream. It starts when this
client sends (Send, Retry, or Regenerate) or continues a
widget, approve, or connect card, and it ends when that
stream completes, is Stopped, or errors, including an empty
reply. It is not only the pre-first-token waiting line. The
in-chat waiting `···` and the streaming caret stay as they
are. Switching chats does not move the indicator. It stays
on the chat whose stream the client started. Only that
in-memory client state is used. There is no new request to
ask the runtime who is generating.

Preview precedence, highest first. Draft: still wins the
preview line for a non-open chat with a visible draft.

1. A chat other than the one open, with a visible top-level
   composer draft (v0.91), shows **Draft:** and that draft.
   This wins over Typing…, including while that chat is
   generating. The `···` still replace the time.
2. Otherwise, a chat with an in-flight reply shows muted
   Typing…. That includes the open chat.
3. Otherwise the last-message preview.

Whitespace-only drafts still do not count. The open chat
does not show Draft:. A thread draft still does not replace
the channel row. The draft is still the null-thread slot,
the conversation the row opens.

When the reply finishes, the dots and Typing… clear. If
that chat is not open then, or the window is not focused
on it, the existing v0.70 unread dot takes over as it does
today. The indicator does not itself mark unread, and it
does not clear an unread mark. A row can show the unread
dot and the generating dots together, for example after
Mark as unread while the reply is still in flight. Opening
the chat still clears unread.

Pins (v0.89) keep the indicator on the pinned row, in the
Pinned group. The Unread filter (v0.88) still lists only
chats that paint the unread dot. Generating is not a second
unread. A pinned unread chat that is also generating stays
in the Pinned group under Unread, with both marks. Mark
unread, relative time, and the draft hint stay. No new
storage key.

iPhone's chat list is not an open chat, so a reply still
in flight after you go back to the list shows the indicator
there. iPad uses the selected chat as the open chat, the
same way the draft hint does.

## 95. v0.94 — Quote selection into the composer (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged.

Selecting text inside a user-RIGHT or assistant-LEFT
`kind=message` bubble offers a small muted **Quote** control.
Desktop places that control near the selection. iOS includes
Quote on the long-press menu when there is a selection, and
on the text-selection menu. Activating Quote inserts
greater-than lines (`> ` on each line of the selection) into
the composer, puts the caret on the blank line after the
quote so the reply is not part of the quote, and focuses the
composer. It does not send.

An empty or whitespace-only selection does nothing. Quoting
does not clear an existing draft. When the composer already
has text, the quote is appended after one blank line. A
whitespace-only composer counts as empty. The quote is the
selected text only, not the whole bubble.

Tool lines, widget cards, approve cards, connect cards, and
file chips are not quotable. A LEFT bubble that is still
streaming is not quotable; only completed bubbles (including
a Stop partial once it is completed) are. Reduce Motion adds
no animation: the control appears and disappears in place.

Pins, unread, the draft hint, relative time, and the Unread
filter stay. No new storage key beyond the existing per-chat
draft.

## 96. v0.95 — Rename a chat from the sidebar (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged.

Right-click a sidebar chat or agent row, or use the small
hover control beside the pin, to choose **Rename**. The row
name becomes an inline field filled with the current display
name. Enter commits. Blur commits. Escape cancels and
restores the previous name. A blank or whitespace-only draft
is rejected and the old name stays. A name longer than 80
characters is rejected the same way. Leading and trailing
whitespace are trimmed. iOS offers Rename on long-press and
on a leading swipe. The row name becomes an inline field
with the same rules: Return or leaving the field commits,
Escape cancels.

The stored value is a local display name for that chat id,
not a change to the agent or channel record. Desktop uses
`localStorage` key `snorlax.chatNames`. iOS uses UserDefaults
under the same key. A missing or corrupt blob is an empty
map. Deleting a chat drops its entry. There is no new HTTP
route. The existing identity PATCH is unchanged: the identity
pane still edits the server name. When that save changes the
server name, the local overlay for that chat is dropped so
the saved name shows.

The sidebar row, the open chat's header, the composer
placeholder, the quick switcher, and the iOS chat-list search
use the display name. Transcript speaker labels stay on the
server name. Pins, the unread dot, the Draft: preview,
generating ···, relative time, the All / Unread filter, mark
unread, and Alt+Up/Down stay on the chat id, so a rename does
not reorder the list or clear those marks. Reduce Motion adds
no animation.

stands. v0.89 pin a chat to the top stands. v0.90 mark a
chat unread stands. v0.91 draft hint in the chat list stands.
v0.92 keyboard previous/next chat stands. v0.93 sidebar
generating indicator stands. v0.94 quote selection into the

## 97. v0.96 — Archive a chat from the sidebar (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged.

Right-click a sidebar chat or agent row. The menu that
already offers Rename, Pin / Unpin, and Mark as unread /
Mark as read also offers **Archive**. The chat leaves the
main sidebar list immediately. A small muted **Archived (n)**
control at the bottom of the sidebar, above the account row,
expands to the archived chats. There is no animation.
**Unarchive** on that row's menu, and the hover control in
the pin slot, puts the chat back. If it was pinned, it
returns to the Pinned group in pin order. Otherwise it
returns to roster order. Pin and Unpin are hidden while the
chat is archived. The pin itself is not cleared.

If the archived chat is the one open, the following chat in
the current Alt+Up/Down order opens. That order is the
Pinned group first, then the rest, after the All / Unread
filter, with archived chats already left out. There is no
wrap. The last visible row, or an open chat that is not in
that order, becomes the empty state. Archiving a different
chat leaves the open chat alone.

iOS offers Archive on a trailing swipe and on long-press.
An **Archived (n)** row on the chat list opens an Archived
list. That list offers Unarchive on a leading swipe and on
long-press, and hides Pin. A Chats control returns to the
main list. The same selection rule applies when the open
chat is archived.

The set persists in desktop `localStorage` and iOS
UserDefaults under `snorlax.chatArchives`. A missing or
corrupt blob is an empty list. Deleting a chat drops its
entry. There is no new HTTP route.

A finished assistant reply in an archived chat does not
unarchive it. It still marks that chat unread when the chat
is not the one on screen, the same v0.70 rule, so the count
outside the list includes it. The Unread filter does not
show archived chats. The empty Unread state ignores archived
unread. Alt+Up / Alt+Down and iOS Option+Up / Option+Down
skip archived chats. Cmd/Ctrl-K and the iOS name search
still find them, and opening one from there leaves it
archived. Rename, the unread dot, Draft: preview, generating
···, and relative time stay available on an archived row.
Reduce Motion adds no animation.

draft hint in the chat list stands. v0.92 keyboard

## 99. v0.99 — focus the composer (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged. v0.98 is a separate open PR (per-agent model
provider) and is not this cut.

When the open chat is showing and focus is not in a text
field, typing a printable character moves focus to the
composer. The character is appended to that chat's draft,
after any text already there, and the caret sits after it.
The draft stays the v0.64 / v0.69 per-chat draft. Nothing
is sent.

Cmd/Ctrl-L does the focus and inserts nothing. `/` is not
that chord: it is printable, so it lands in the draft and
can open skill autocomplete. `?` outside a text field still
opens the v0.73 shortcuts overlay and is not inserted.
Cmd/Ctrl-L does not collide with Cmd/Ctrl-F, Cmd/Ctrl-K,
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-I, Cmd/Ctrl-V, or Cmd/Ctrl-/. Shift and Alt
on L stay with the browser. If the composer already has
focus, Cmd/Ctrl-L is swallowed and the caret stays put.

These keys are not captured: Cmd, Ctrl, and Alt chords,
Escape, the arrow keys, Tab, Enter, and IME composition.
Up-arrow on an empty composer still recalls (v0.68). Esc
still Stops while generating (v0.53) and Jumps when frozen
(v0.60). Alt+Up / Alt+Down still switches chats (v0.92).
In-chat find keeps Cmd/Ctrl-F, Enter / Shift-Enter, and its
up / down controls.

The key is also left alone while find is open, while the
quick switcher is open, while the shortcuts list is open,
while a chat is being renamed, while the image lightbox is
open, while the sidebar context menu is open, and while
focus is inside a widget, approve, or connect card. Takeover,
settings, and the other modal sheets keep the keyboard too.
A visible card does not block typing when focus is on the
transcript.

iOS uses the same focus action from a hardware keyboard:
⌘L or ⌃L focuses the composer, puts the caret at the end of
the draft, and does not insert or send. Plain letters are
not captured, so the software keyboard is not forced up.
The Gestures & shortcuts sheet lists **⌘L** as **Focus the
composer**. The desktop shortcuts overlay lists **Cmd/Ctrl L**
with the same action.

Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-C copies the latest completed assistant LEFT
`kind=message` in the open chat. The clipboard text is that
message's `content`, the same markdown source the Copy button
copies, including when the bubble is split into short
multi-bubbles. A reply that is still streaming is skipped, so
the previous completed reply is copied when one exists. If
the only assistant text is still streaming, or the latest
completed reply is empty or whitespace, nothing is copied and
the clipboard stays as it was. Tool, widget, approve, and
connect rows are not replies. User bubbles are not replies.

The muted Copied label from v0.61 sits beside that message's
Copy for 1.2s. The Copy label stays. There is no toast. The
composer may stay focused, and the draft is not edited or
sent. Plain Cmd/Ctrl-C still copies a selection.

The chord does not fire while IME composition is active, while
find is open, while the quick switcher is open, while the
shortcuts list is open, while a chat is being renamed, while
the image lightbox is open, while the sidebar context menu is
open, or while focus is inside a widget, approve, or connect
card. Takeover, settings, and the other modal sheets keep the
keyboard too. Alt on the chord stays with the browser. It does
not collide with Cmd/Ctrl-C, Cmd/Ctrl-L, Cmd/Ctrl-F,
Cmd/Ctrl-K, Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-I, Cmd/Ctrl-V, or Cmd/Ctrl-/.

iOS uses the same copy from a hardware keyboard: ⌘⇧C or ⌃⇧C.
The Gestures & shortcuts sheet lists **⌘⇧C** as **Copy the
last reply**. The desktop shortcuts overlay lists
**Cmd/Ctrl Shift C** with the same action.

## 102. v1.02 — mute a chat from the sidebar (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged. v0.98 is a separate open PR (per-agent model
provider) and is not this cut.

Right-click a sidebar chat row, or the hover control on that
row, offers **Mute** or **Unmute**. iOS offers the same labels
on a long-press and on a swipe. There is no keyboard shortcut,
so the v0.73 shortcuts overlay and the iOS Gestures & shortcuts
sheet do not gain a row.

A muted chat still receives messages. Opening it is the same
as opening any other chat: the transcript loads, the draft and
reading place come back, and the composer can send. The row
shows a subtle muted glyph (`Muted`) so the chat is
recognizable in the list, including the archived list.

While it is muted it does not paint the v0.70 unread accent
dot. Muting clears the dot it already had, including a channel
handoff dot. A finished assistant reply does not mark it, so
the v0.77 window title and badge do not grow. The v0.88 Unread
filter skips it; if nothing else is unread, the existing **No
unread chats** empty state shows. Unmute does not invent a
dot. Mark as unread stays in the menu. On a muted chat it does
not paint a dot or change the badge.

The set persists (`snorlax.chatMutes`, desktop local storage
and iOS UserDefaults). Deleting a chat drops its mute. Pins,
rename, archive, Draft: preview, generating ···, relative
time, All / Unread, Alt+Up/Down, and section headers stay.
Archived chats can be muted. A new message leaves an archived
chat archived.

## 103. v1.03 — transcript keyboard scroll (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23 and #24 untouched.
Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX / runtime URL
unchanged. v0.98 is a separate open PR (per-agent model
provider) and is not this cut. Nothing new is persisted.

When the open chat is showing and focus is on the transcript,
or focus is not trapped in a text field or overlay, the
keyboard scrolls the messages already loaded.

PageUp and PageDown move about one viewport up or down, the
same step a scrollable pane uses, and stop at the ends of the
loaded transcript. Holding the key repeats. Cmd/Ctrl-Home
jumps to the oldest loaded messages (the top). Cmd/Ctrl-End
jumps to the latest messages (the bottom) by the existing
Jump to latest path: the transcript snaps to the bottom,
stick-to-bottom re-arms, and the Jump chip dismisses the same
way a click on that chip does, including returning focus to
the composer when that path already does. A PageDown that
lands inside the ~64px stick slack re-arms through the
existing scroll check and does not move composer focus.

These keys are not taken while typing in the composer, the
find bar, the rename field, or the quick switcher, while the
shortcuts overlay or the image lightbox is open, or while
focus is inside a widget, approve, or connect card. IME
composition keeps the key. Shift and Alt stay with the
browser, so Shift-PageUp still selects. Bare Home and End on
desktop stay with the browser. Up-arrow on an empty composer
still recalls (v0.68). Esc still Stops while generating
(v0.53) and Jumps when frozen (v0.60). Alt+Up / Alt+Down
still switches chats (v0.92). Cmd/Ctrl-L still focuses the
composer (v0.99). Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-C still copies the last
reply (v1.00). In-chat find keeps Cmd/Ctrl-F and its own
stepping keys.

The scroll assigns the transcript offset directly. There is
no smooth-scroll animation, so Reduce Motion has nothing
further to disable.

iOS uses the same scroll from a hardware keyboard when the
composer and the find field are not first responder. Page Up
and Page Down match. ⌘↑ / ⌃↑ jump to the oldest loaded
messages and ⌘↓ / ⌃↓ jump to the latest, which is the Home /
End equivalent on that keyboard. Home and End themselves do
the same when they are not delivered to a text field. ⌘↓
uses the Jump chip path, including composer focus when a
hardware keyboard is attached. The Gestures & shortcuts sheet
lists **Page Up / Page Down** as **Scroll the transcript**
and **⌘↑ / ⌘↓** as **Oldest or latest messages**. The desktop
shortcuts overlay lists **Page Up / Page Down** and
**Cmd/Ctrl Home / End** with those actions.

Cmd/Ctrl-N starts a new chat. The chord runs the existing
New agent path: the same path as the sidebar Create control's

in progress is not interrupted and nothing is created.

sidebar context menu, and a focused widget, approve, or
connect card keep the key even when focus is not in their
field. Mention and skill menus keep it. Signed out, the
sidebar Create control is disabled, and the chord does
nothing. Nothing is sent.

Cmd/Ctrl-L still focuses the composer. Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-C still

scroll. Esc still Stops, closes, or Jumps. Up-arrow on an
empty composer still recalls.

iOS hardware ⌘N and ⌃N call that same create path, including

## 105. v1.05 — jump to a numbered chat (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second

untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged. v0.98 is a separate open PR (per-agent
model provider) and is not this cut. Nothing new is persisted.

Cmd/Ctrl-1 through Cmd/Ctrl-8 opens that chat in the sidebar's
current visible order. The order is the one Alt+Up / Alt+Down
already walks: the Pinned group first, in pin order, then the
unpinned rows. v0.97 section headers are not targets. The
v0.88 All / Unread filter applies to that main roster. Archived
chats are excluded unless the archived list is what is showing.
On desktop the archive section stays under the main roster, so
an expanded archive list is appended after those rows. On iOS
the Archived screen replaces the main list, so the order is
only the archived rows. The iOS name-search field does not
change the order, the same way Option+Up / Option+Down ignores
it. The Unread filter is not re-applied to the archived rows;
that list shows the archived chats it already paints.

Cmd/Ctrl-9 opens the last chat in that visible order. That is
the Slack and browser convention: 9 means last, not a literal
ninth row. With nine chats, the ninth and the last are the
same row. With fewer than nine, 1 through the count open those
rows, a higher number through 8 does nothing, and 9 still
opens the last row. With more than nine, 1 through 8 open
those rows and 9 opens the last, skipping the rows in between.
An empty list does nothing for every number. A key repeat does
not jump again.

The chord is Cmd-1..Cmd-9 or Ctrl-1..Ctrl-9, not both, and not
with Shift or Alt. It still works while the composer is
focused. The platform does nothing useful with that chord in
the field, and the digit is not inserted. Opening the chat is
the same path as clicking or tapping its sidebar row: unread
clears, that chat's draft and reading place come back, and
focus lands in the composer. A draft in the chat being left
stays with that chat. IME composition keeps the key.

Find, the quick switcher, the shortcuts list, rename, the
lightbox, a sidebar context menu, and a focused widget,
approve, or connect card keep the key. A modal or overlay
text field keeps it: find, rename, the switcher query,
settings, profile, the channel dialog, and any text field
that is not the composer. Mention and skill menus keep it.
Signed-out windows and the other modal sheets keep it.
Nothing is sent.

Cmd/Ctrl-K still opens the switcher. Cmd/Ctrl-F still finds.
Cmd/Ctrl-L still focuses the composer. Cmd/Ctrl-N still
starts a new chat. Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-C still copies the last
reply. Cmd/Ctrl-/ still opens shortcuts. Alt+Up / Alt+Down
still walks the main roster. PageUp / PageDown and
Cmd/Ctrl-Home / Cmd/Ctrl-End still scroll. Esc still Stops,
closes, or Jumps. Up-arrow on an empty composer still recalls.

iOS hardware ⌘1–⌘9 and ⌃1–⌃9 call that same open path,
including composer focus. ⌘9 opens the last chat. The
composer text view offers the chord and skips it while
marked text is composing. The chat list offers it on the
order that list is showing, and skips it while search,
rename, or the channel sheet is using the keyboard. The
Gestures & shortcuts sheet lists **⌘1-⌘9** as **Open a chat
(9 is last)**. The desktop shortcuts overlay lists
**Cmd/Ctrl 1-9** with that action.

## 110. v1.10 — mark the open chat unread from the keyboard (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP

model provider) and is not this cut. Nothing new is persisted

Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-U marks the currently open chat through the
v0.90 sidebar Mark as unread / Mark as read action. The chord
is Cmd-Shift-U or Ctrl-Shift-U, not both modifiers, and not
with Alt. Plain U still types. Cmd/Ctrl-U without Shift is
not this chord. The chord still matches a key repeat so the
letter is not inserted, and that repeat does not apply the
action again. Holding the chord cannot flap Mark as unread /
Mark as read.

The action is that sidebar control. A read chat is written
into the existing unread set (`snorlax.chatUnread`, desktop
local storage and iOS UserDefaults). That paints the same
accent unread dot, counts toward the unread count outside
the chat list, and shows the chat under the Unread filter.
A chat that already shows the dot is marked read, including
a channel handoff dot, which is what that same menu item
does. Opening the chat still clears it. A muted chat follows
the sidebar: Mark as unread does not paint a dot or change
the badge, and a finished assistant reply still does not
mark a muted chat. Pins, rename, archive, Draft: preview,
generating ···, relative time, All / Unread, and section
headers stay.

The chord still works while the composer is focused. The
letter is not inserted. IME composition keeps the key.
Find, the quick switcher, the shortcuts list, rename, the
lightbox, a sidebar context menu, and a focused widget,
approve, or connect card keep the key. A modal or overlay
text field keeps it: find, rename, the switcher query,
settings, profile, the channel dialog, and any text field
that is not the composer. Mention and skill menus keep it.
Signed-out windows with no open chat do nothing. Nothing
is sent.

Esc still Stops, closes, or Jumps. Up-arrow on an empty
composer still recalls. Cmd/Ctrl-K still opens the switcher.
Cmd/Ctrl-F still finds. Cmd/Ctrl-L still focuses the
composer. Cmd/Ctrl-N still starts a new chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-C still copies the last reply.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-A still archives the open chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-Backspace still clears the draft.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-P still pins or unpins the open chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-M still mutes or unmutes the open chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-/ still opens shortcuts. Cmd/Ctrl-1 through
Cmd/Ctrl-9 still jump to a numbered chat. Alt+Up /
Alt+Down still walks the visible roster. PageUp / PageDown
and Cmd/Ctrl-Home / Cmd/Ctrl-End still scroll.

iOS hardware ⌘⇧U and ⌃⇧U call that same action, including
while the composer is focused. The composer text view
offers the chord, skips it while marked text is composing,
and ignores a repeat of the held key. The chat screen
skips it while find, the shortcuts sheet, the lightbox,
rename, search, or a card text field is using the keyboard,
and when the chat is the empty state. A repeat there is
swallowed and does not flap. The Gestures & shortcuts sheet
lists **⌘⇧U** as **Mark as unread or read**. The desktop
shortcuts overlay lists **Cmd/Ctrl Shift U** with that
action.

The chord opens the same confirm the sidebar Delete… menu
uses. The copy is `Delete {name}? This removes the agent and
its chat.` (channel: `the channel and its chat`). Enter or
Delete confirms. On a Mac keyboard the Delete key is
Backspace; the forward Delete key confirms too. Esc or
Cancel aborts, and focus returns to the composer on cancel.
A key repeat never confirms, so holding Enter, Delete, or
the chord cannot delete the chat that opens next. Only on
confirm does the existing removal run. That is the existing
DELETE for the roster row, then the local cleanup: the row
leaves the roster, channel member lists drop that id, and
the pin, mute, archive, display name, and unread sets drop
it. The sidebar Delete… menu still uses that same confirm.
A removal already in flight does not start a second one.

After the open chat is gone, the next chat in the
Alt+Up/Down visible order opens: the Pinned group first,
section headers ignored, the All / Unread filter applied,
archived chats excluded. The following row opens. The last
visible row, or an open chat that is not in that order,
becomes the empty state. Sidebar Delete of some other row
still uses the existing roster fallback.

The chord still works while the composer is focused. The
letter is not inserted. IME composition keeps the key.

shortcuts overlay lists **Cmd/Ctrl Shift H** with that

PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged. Nothing new is persisted. The history
lives in memory for that window and is gone when the window
closes.

Cmd/Ctrl-[ opens the previously opened chat. Cmd/Ctrl-]
opens the next one again. The chord is Cmd-[ or Ctrl-[,
and Cmd-] or Ctrl-], not both modifiers, and not with Shift
or Alt. Plain [ and ] still type. The chord still matches a
key repeat so the bracket is not inserted. That repeat does
not step again.

The history is a small in-memory list of chat opens for
this window. Opening a chat pushes. That includes the
sidebar row, the quick switcher, Cmd/Ctrl-1 through
Cmd/Ctrl-9, next unread, previous unread, Alt+Up /
Alt+Down, a new agent or channel, and the chat that opens
after archive or delete. Opening the chat that is already
current does not push again and does not drop the forward
entries. Going back and then opening a different chat
truncates those forward entries. Back and forward themselves
move the cursor. They do not push.

Deleted chats are skipped in both directions. If every
candidate that way is gone, the chord is a no-op and the
cursor stays. Archived chats stay valid targets because
they are still on the roster. Opening one is the same as
opening any other chat: its draft and reading place come
back and the composer is focused. Either end of the history
is a no-op. Leaving the last chat for the empty state parks
the cursor just past that chat, so Back can return to it,
including when that chat is archived.

The chord still works while the composer is focused. The
bracket is not inserted. It also works when no chat is
open, so Back can return from the empty state. IME
composition keeps the key. Find, the quick switcher, the
shortcuts overlay, rename, the lightbox, a sidebar context
menu, and a focused widget, approve, or connect card keep
the key. A modal or overlay text field keeps it: find, the
rename field, the switcher query, settings, profile, the
channel dialog, and any text field that is not the composer.
Mention and skill menus keep it. Nothing is sent.

Esc still Stops, closes, or Jumps. Up-arrow on an empty
composer still recalls. Cmd/Ctrl-K still opens the
switcher. Cmd/Ctrl-F still finds. Cmd/Ctrl-L still focuses
the composer. Cmd/Ctrl-N still starts a new chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-C still copies the last reply.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-A still archives the open chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-B still unarchives the open chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-Backspace still clears the draft.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-P still pins or unpins the open chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-M still mutes or unmutes the open chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-U still marks the open chat unread or read.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-R still starts rename. Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-D
still asks to delete. Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-E still copies the
transcript. Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-G still collapses or expands
tool traces. Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-F still toggles All / Unread.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-H still opens or closes Archived chats.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-J still opens the next unread chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-Y still regenerates the last reply.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-S still focuses the chat list.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-O still marks every chat read.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-K still opens the previous unread chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-/ still opens shortcuts. Cmd/Ctrl-1 through
Cmd/Ctrl-9 still jump to a numbered chat. Alt+Up /
Alt+Down still walks the visible roster. PageUp / PageDown
and Cmd/Ctrl-Home / Cmd/Ctrl-End still scroll.

iOS hardware ⌘[ and ⌃[ step back. ⌘] and ⌃] step forward,
including while the composer is focused and from the chat
list. The composer text view offers the chords, skips them
while marked text is composing, and ignores a repeat of the
held key. The chat screen skips the chord while find, the
shortcuts sheet, the lightbox, rename, search, or a card
text field is using the keyboard. The Gestures & shortcuts
sheet lists **⌘[ / ⌘]** as **Back or forward chat**. The
desktop shortcuts overlay lists **Cmd/Ctrl [ / ]** with that
action.

### Prior slices

v0.73 keyboard shortcuts help stands. v0.92 keyboard
previous/next chat stands. v1.05 jump to a numbered chat
stands. v1.17 and v1.21 next and previous unread stand.
v1.22 unarchive the open chat from the keyboard stands.

Out of scope: VNC / full Box GUI, extra channel types, public
store, coding-agent, new surfaces, a new HTTP route, bumping
OpenAPI past 0.18.0, a second PR, persisting the history,
a back button in the chrome.
`workspaceTree.ts` (never reintroduce `computerPane.ts`)
stands.

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second

untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /

Cmd/Ctrl-= and Cmd/Ctrl-+ make the chat transcript text one
step larger. Cmd/Ctrl-- makes it one step smaller.
Cmd/Ctrl-0 resets to 100%. The steps are 85%, 92%, 100%,
110%, 120%, and 130%. The ends stay clamped. Shift is
allowed on = / + and on - because + is the shifted = key.
Cmd/Ctrl-0 does not take Shift, so Cmd/Ctrl-1 through
Cmd/Ctrl-9 still open a chat. The chord still matches a
key repeat so the character is not inserted. That repeat
does not step again.

Message bubble text, code blocks, and tool traces share
the scale. That includes user bubbles, assistant markdown,
headings, inline code, fenced code, math fallback, and
tool-trace lines. Padding, images, max widths, and chrome
outside the transcript stay put. A long message still
collapses at about 14 lines of the scaled body. The choice
persists in local storage (`snorlax.transcriptTextScale`).
Missing or unknown values stay at 100%.

The chord still works while the composer is focused. The
character is not inserted. It also works when no chat is
open, because the preference belongs to the transcript.
IME composition keeps the key. Find, the quick switcher,
the shortcuts overlay, rename, the lightbox, a sidebar
context menu, and a focused widget, approve, or connect
card keep the key. A modal or overlay text field keeps it.
Mention and skill menus keep it. Nothing is sent.

Esc still Stops, closes, or Jumps. Up-arrow on an empty
composer still recalls. Cmd/Ctrl-K still opens the
switcher. Cmd/Ctrl-F still finds. Cmd/Ctrl-L still focuses
the composer. Cmd/Ctrl-N still starts a new chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-C still copies the last reply.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-A still archives the open chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-B still unarchives the open chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-Backspace still clears the draft.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-P still pins or unpins the open chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-M still mutes or unmutes the open chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-U still marks the open chat unread or read.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-R still starts rename. Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-D
still asks to delete. Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-E still copies the
transcript. Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-G still collapses or expands
tool traces. Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-F still toggles All / Unread.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-H still opens or closes Archived chats.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-J still opens the next unread chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-Y still regenerates the last reply.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-S still focuses the chat list.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-O still marks every chat read.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-K still opens the previous unread chat.
Cmd/Ctrl-/ still opens shortcuts. Cmd/Ctrl-[ and Cmd/Ctrl-]
still step through recently opened chats. Alt+Up /
Alt+Down still walks the visible roster. PageUp / PageDown
and Cmd/Ctrl-Home / Cmd/Ctrl-End still scroll.

iOS hardware ⌘= and ⌃= (and ⌘+ / ⌃+) step larger. ⌘- and
⌃- step smaller. ⌘0 and ⌃0 reset, including while the
composer is focused and from the chat list. Dynamic Type
is the base: the percent multiplies the body-scaled point
size, so a larger content size stays larger. 100% at the
default content size is the authored size. The choice
persists in UserDefaults under the same key. The composer
text view offers the chords, skips them while marked text
is composing, and ignores a repeat of the held key. The
chat screen skips the chord while find, the shortcuts
sheet, the lightbox, rename, search, or a card text field
is using the keyboard. Gestures & shortcuts lists
**⌘= / ⌘+ / ⌘- / ⌘0** as **Larger, smaller, or default
text** and has Smaller, Larger, and Default controls.
The desktop shortcuts overlay lists **Cmd/Ctrl = / + / - / 0**
with that action.

stand. v1.23 keyboard back and forward stands.

## 125. v1.25 — jump between your sent messages from the keyboard (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-Up scrolls to the previous user-RIGHT
`kind=message` in the open chat's loaded transcript.
Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-Down scrolls to the next one. The target is
the sent message relative to the current viewport: the
closest one whose top is above the reading line, or the
closest one below it. The reading line is the viewport top
plus the sticky day header, so the bubble lands just under
that pin (v0.85). A long sent message you have scrolled
into counts as previous, so the first press brings its top
back. Either end stays clamped and does not wrap. The
target bubble gets a brief subtle outline for 1.2s. Reduce
Motion keeps the outline and skips the fade. Focus stays
where it was.

Assistant messages, tool lines, widgets, approve cards, and
connect cards are not targets. Collapsed long messages
(v0.81) stay collapsed. The jump uses the laid-out tops, so
a collapsed neighbor does not change which sent message is
next. Stick-to-bottom and the Jump chip follow the same
path as Page Up / Page Down: landing inside the bottom
slack re-arms, and scrolling up disarms. Per-chat reading
place (v0.74) captures the new offset. Nothing is sent.

The chord still works from an empty composer and does not
move the caret. When the composer already has text,
including spaces, Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-Up and Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-Down
keep selecting text. A key repeat still matches so the
browser does not scroll, and that repeat does not jump
again. IME composition keeps the key. Find, the quick
switcher, the shortcuts overlay, rename, the lightbox, a
sidebar context menu, and a focused widget, approve, or
connect card keep the key. A modal or overlay text field
keeps it. Mention and skill menus keep it. Nothing happens
when no chat is open or the loaded transcript has no sent
messages in that direction.

Up-arrow on an empty composer still recalls. Alt+Up /
Alt+Down still walk the visible roster. Cmd/Ctrl-Up on iOS
still jumps to the oldest loaded messages, because this
chord requires Shift. PageUp / PageDown and Cmd/Ctrl-Home /
Cmd/Ctrl-End still scroll. Cmd/Ctrl-= / Cmd/Ctrl-- /
Cmd/Ctrl-0 still change the transcript text size.
Cmd/Ctrl-K still opens the switcher. Cmd/Ctrl-F still
finds. Cmd/Ctrl-L still focuses the composer. Cmd/Ctrl-N
still starts a new chat. Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-C still copies the
last reply. The other Cmd/Ctrl-Shift letter chords stay.
Cmd/Ctrl-/ still opens shortcuts. Cmd/Ctrl-[ and Cmd/Ctrl-]
still step through recently opened chats. Esc still Stops,
closes, or Jumps.

iOS hardware ⌘⇧↑ and ⌃⇧↑ jump to the previous sent message.
⌘⇧↓ and ⌃⇧↓ jump to the next one, including while the
composer is focused and empty. The composer text view
offers those chords only when the field is empty, skips
them while marked text is composing, and ignores a repeat
of the held key. When the field has text, the arrows keep
selecting. The chat screen handles the same chord when
focus is not in the composer, and skips it while find, the
shortcuts sheet, the lightbox, rename, search, or a card
text field is using the keyboard. A sent message that is
not laid out yet still scrolls into view. Gestures &
shortcuts lists **⌘⇧↑ / ⌘⇧↓** as **Previous or next message
you sent**. The desktop shortcuts overlay lists
**Cmd/Ctrl Shift ↑ / ↓** with that action.

## 126. v1.26 — quote a transcript selection from the keyboard (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second

Transcript keyboard scroll already shipped as v1.03
(Page Up / Page Down and Cmd/Ctrl-Home / Cmd/Ctrl-End).
This cut is the next chat-fluency gap versus Grok Bot:
select text in a chat and press Cmd/Ctrl-L to quote it
into the next message. Snorlax already shows a Quote
control for that selection (v0.94) and uses Cmd/Ctrl-L to
focus the composer (v0.99). The chord did not quote.

Cmd/Ctrl-L, when the selection sits in one user-RIGHT or
completed assistant-LEFT `kind=message`, inserts that
selection as markdown `>` lines through the existing quote
path. The caret sits on the blank line after the quote.
The composer is focused. Nothing is sent. An existing
draft is kept and the quote is appended after one blank
line. A whitespace-only draft counts as empty. An empty
or whitespace-only selection does nothing, and Cmd/Ctrl-L
then only focuses the composer, as v0.99 already does.

A selection in the composer is not quoted. Cmd/Ctrl-L
while that field is focused still swallows the key and
leaves the caret where it is. A key repeat is not the
chord, so a hold does not quote again. IME composition
keeps the key. Find, the quick switcher, the shortcuts
overlay, rename, the lightbox, a sidebar context menu,
and a focused widget, approve, or connect card keep the
key. A modal or overlay text field keeps it. Mention and
skill menus keep it. Takeover, settings, and profile keep
it.

Tool lines, widgets, approve cards, connect cards, file
chips, and a streaming LEFT bubble stay unquotable. Both
ends of the selection still have to sit in the same
message. Stick-to-bottom, the Jump chip, per-chat reading
place, the sticky day header, and collapsed long messages
stay as they are. Quoting does not scroll the transcript
and does not expand a collapsed bubble. Reduce Motion
adds no animation. The Quote control stays.

Cmd/Ctrl-K still opens the switcher. Cmd/Ctrl-F still
finds. Cmd/Ctrl-N still starts a new chat. Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-C
still copies the last reply. The other chords stay.
Up-arrow on an empty composer still recalls. Page Up /
Page Down and Cmd/Ctrl-Home / Cmd/Ctrl-End still scroll.
Esc still Stops, closes, or Jumps.

iOS hardware ⌘L and ⌃L quote a transcript selection the
same way, including from the message text view, and then
focus the composer. A repeat of the hold does not quote
again. The composer text view still swallows ⌘L without
inserting and without quoting its own selection. Marked
text keeps the key. Find, the shortcuts sheet, rename,
the lightbox, settings, and a card text field keep it.
Gestures & shortcuts lists **⌘L** as **Quote the
selection**, beside the existing focus row. The desktop
shortcuts overlay lists **Cmd/Ctrl L** with that action.

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
