The New messages divider already shipped as v0.76: a thin
muted line above the first assistant reply that arrived
since you last viewed that chat, computed once on open,
cleared when you come back with nothing new or when you
Send. This cut does not add a second divider. The next
chat-fluency gap versus Grok Bot is the reply quote that
jumps. Snorlax already inserts a markdown quote (v0.94)
and quotes a selection from the keyboard (v1.26). A sent
quote was inert text. Grok Bot shows that quoted text and
jumps to the replied message when you activate it.

Quoting a user-RIGHT or completed assistant-LEFT
`kind=message` remembers that message id with the open
chat's draft. The memory is local: desktop `localStorage`
key `snorlax.quoteJumps`, iOS `UserDefaults` under the
same key. Send keeps a link for each quote block that is
still in the sent text, keyed by that text so the
optimistic bubble and the server echo share it. Clearing
the draft drops the pending link. A sent message that no
longer contains the quote does not keep it. Tool lines,
cards, and a streaming LEFT bubble stay unquotable.

In the open transcript, each matching quote block on a
user message is a thin control labelled with the quoted
text. Activating it scrolls that source message under the
sticky day header and outlines it for 1.2s. Reduce Motion
keeps the outline and skips the fade. Nothing else
animates. The jump uses the same stick-to-bottom and
reading-place path as Page Up. Collapsed long messages
stay collapsed. Find keeps the full message text so a
match stays highlighted. Muted chats and the New messages
divider stay as they are.

Cmd/Ctrl-' (⌘' / ⌃' on iOS) jumps to the source of the
quote nearest the reading line. The latest quote in that
message is the source. The composer may keep its text.
The apostrophe is not inserted. A key repeat is swallowed
and does not jump again. IME composition keeps the key.
Find, the quick switcher, the shortcuts overlay, rename,
the lightbox, a sidebar context menu, and a focused
widget, approve, or connect card keep the key. A modal or
overlay text field keeps it. Mention and skill menus keep
it. Nothing happens when no chat is open or no quote in
the loaded transcript has a source that is still loaded.

Cmd/Ctrl-L still quotes a selection or focuses the
composer. Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-Up / Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-Down still
jump between messages you sent. The other chords stay.
Up-arrow on an empty composer still recalls. Page Up /
Page Down and Cmd/Ctrl-Home / Cmd/Ctrl-End still scroll.
Esc still Stops, closes, or Jumps.

iOS hardware ⌘' and ⌃' jump the same way, including while
the composer is focused and already has text. A repeat of
the hold does not jump again. Marked text keeps the key.
Gestures & shortcuts lists **⌘'** as **Jump to the quoted
message**, and lists a tap on a sent quote. The desktop

## 128. v1.28 — queue a follow-up while a reply is generating (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Sending while a reply the client started is still streaming
was blocked. Grok Bot lets you type and press Enter while
the agent is working. The message waits as a muted Queued
item just above the composer. This cut does that, on the
existing send path.

While that reply is in flight, Enter (not Shift+Enter, not
while IME text is composing) or the Send button takes the
composer text, clears the composer, and shows it as a muted
**Queued** row. The text stays editable. A small × removes
that row. Pending attachment chips are not queued; Enter
and Send leave them in the composer and do not send. An
empty field does not queue.

Several follow-ups are separate rows, oldest first. They
are not combined into one message. When the in-flight reply
settles — it finished, it was empty, it errored, or Stop /
Esc aborted it — the oldest row is sent through the same
send path as a normal message. Each later row waits until
that reply settles, so they go out one per completed reply.
Stop or Esc still aborts the current reply, and the next
queued row then goes out immediately.

The queue is keyed by agent and thread, the same key as a
composer draft. Switching chats shows that chat's rows and
leaves the other chat's rows waiting. The queue lives for
the app session. Relaunch drops it. A quote in a queued
message keeps its jump to the source message. Quoting into
the composer after that does not steal the queued link.

If the queued send fails, that chat's queue is dropped and
nothing retries on its own. The failed text is put back in
that chat's composer. Rows that were still waiting are
included after a blank line, then any draft that was
already in the composer, so they are not discarded. The
failed row does not stay as a Retry bubble.

Esc still Stops, closes, or Jumps. Up-arrow on an empty
composer still recalls. Cmd/Ctrl-L still quotes or focuses
the composer. Cmd/Ctrl-' still jumps to a quoted message.
The other chords stay. No new shortcut, so the shortcuts
overlay and the iOS Gestures & shortcuts sheet do not gain
a row. Reduce Motion adds no animation to the Queued row.

iOS shows the same rows above the composer. Hardware
Return while a reply is in flight queues. Shift+Return
stays a newline. The Send button queues when the field has
text and no pending chips. Marked text keeps Return.

### Prior slices

v0.55 Send muted while generating stands for the in-flight
turn itself: a second message does not interrupt it. v0.53
Esc = Stop stands. v0.59 focus after Stop stands. v0.64
and v0.69 per-chat drafts stand. v0.78 Retry a failed send
stands for a send the user started from the composer.
v1.27 quote jump stands. v0.47 stick-to-bottom stands.

Out of scope: VNC / full Box GUI, extra channel types, public
store, coding-agent, new surfaces, a new HTTP route, bumping
OpenAPI past 0.18.0, a second PR, editing a sent message,
queueing attachment chips.
`workspaceTree.ts` (never reintroduce `computerPane.ts`)
stands.

OpenAPI **0.18.0** (not bumped).

Long lines in a completed assistant LEFT `kind=message`
code fence scroll horizontally. Grok Bot lets you wrap
those lines. This cut does that, on the existing fence.

A 12px muted **Wrap** button sits in the fence header,
immediately beside Copy. It is a real button (desktop tab

another fence in the same message or in another chat. It

scroll. The button is hidden while that message is still
streaming, the same gate as Copy. A rendered mermaid

toggle. Math blocks are unchanged. User-right text is
unchanged. Copy still copies the fence body only — no
fences, no language tag — whether or not the lines wrap.

No animation. The label swap and the reflow are instant,
including when Reduce Motion is off. Reduce Motion does
not add a fade. No new shortcut, so the shortcuts list and
the iOS Gestures & shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.
Esc, Up-arrow recall, and the other chords stay.

`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

v0.74 already keeps a per-chat reading place for the
session. Leaving a chat records whether the transcript was
within ~64px / ~64pt of the bottom. Near the bottom, coming
back opens at the latest messages and stays armed.
Further up, the anchor is the topmost visible row, and
coming back scrolls that row's top to the viewport top.
That drops a reader who was partway through the row at the
top of it. Grok Bot lands on the same line.

v1.30 stores one more number with that anchor: the fraction
of the row that sat above the viewport. 0 still aligns the
row top. Coming back measures the row again and scrolls so
the same fraction is above the fold. New messages change
the transcript's height, and v1.24 text size changes the
row's height; the fraction is taken from the row that is
on screen at restore, not from a raw scroll offset. The
assign is instant. There is no animation, including when
Reduce Motion is off.

An anchor that is no longer loaded opens at the latest
messages. A missing place does too. Within the stick slack,
the saved fraction is dropped and the chat stays pinned to
the stream, so the Jump chip logic is unchanged. A new tail
while the chat was away shows Jump to latest and the v0.76
New messages divider, and does not yank the parked row.

In-chat find, a jump to a quoted message, a jump to one of
your sent messages, and Cmd/Ctrl-Home/End still scroll to
their target. A targeted scroll claimed after the switch
was scheduled wins: Home opens at the oldest loaded
messages, End / Send / Regenerate / Jump open at the
latest, and a message target is left where that jump put
it. Send, Regenerate, and Jump still snap to the bottom
and re-arm.

The place lives in memory for the app session. It is not
written to localStorage or UserDefaults. A relaunch opens
at the latest messages. Nothing is persisted.

### Prior slices

v0.47 stick-to-bottom stands. v0.71 in-chat find stands.
v0.74 per-chat reading place stands. v0.76 New messages
divider stands. v1.03 transcript keyboard scroll stands.
v1.24 transcript text size stands. v1.25 jump between your
sent messages stands. v1.27 jump from a sent quote stands.
v1.29 soft-wrap on code blocks stands.

across relaunch.

## 131. v1.31 — markdown tables in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` text can include a
GitHub-style pipe table (a header row, a delimiter row of
hyphens, then body rows). On a completed reply that table
renders as a table. While the reply is still streaming the
LEFT bubble stays plain text, the same as today.

A table wider than the bubble scrolls horizontally inside
its own container. Cells do not wrap, so columns are not
squashed. The scroll container is bounded by the bubble, so
the table does not spill into the transcript or push the
composer. A narrow table uses the same chrome.

A 12px muted **Copy** button sits at the top-right of the
table, the same family as the v0.66 code-block control. It
is a real button (desktop tab order; iOS can activate it).
Accessible name `Copy table`. Click or tap copies that
table's markdown source — the header, delimiter, and rows
as written — then the label reads **Copied** for 1.5s and
reverts to **Copy**. Accessible name while confirming is
`Copied`. The surrounding prose is not copied. The clipboard
is markdown source, not tab-separated text, so a paste back
into the composer round-trips and matches the other copy
controls, which copy source.

The control is hidden while that message is still streaming.
A fence that happens to contain pipes stays a code fence.
User-right messages stay plain text. Tool lines, widgets,
approve cards, and connect cards are not tables.

No animation. The label swap is instant, including when
Reduce Motion is off. Reduce Motion does not add a fade.
No new shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS
Gestures & shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

### Prior slices

v0.11 assistant markdown stands. v0.66 Copy → Copied stands.
v1.29 code-block wrap stands. v1.30 reading offset stands.
Streaming LEFT bubbles stay plain text.

GFM blockquote and a GFM task list. On a completed reply a
line that begins with `>` renders as a quote: muted text,
indented, with a muted left rule. A nested line that begins
with `>>` is a quote inside that quote, with its own rule
and indent. `- [ ]` and `- [x]` (also `*` / `+`, and `[X]`)
render as read-only checkbox glyphs, not as the characters
`[ ]` or `[x]`. A checked item is muted. The glyphs are not
controls: they do not toggle, and they are not in tab order.

While the reply is still streaming, the LEFT bubble stays
as it renders today. Copy on the message, Copy on a code
block, and Copy on a table still copy markdown source,
including the `>` markers and the `- [ ]` markers. A fence
or a `$$` block that contains those characters stays a
fence or math. User-right messages stay plain text. Tool
lines, widgets, approve cards, and connect cards are not
quotes or task lists.

## 133. v1.33 — headings, strikethrough, and thematic breaks in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second

Completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` text can include ATX
headings, GFM strikethrough, and thematic breaks. On a
completed reply:

- `#` through `######` render as headings. Level 1 is 16px /
  16pt semibold. Levels 2–6 are 14px / 14pt semibold. The
  `#` characters are not shown. Desktop already did this.
  iOS applies the same sizes on the text view.
- `~~text~~` renders struck through. The tildes are not
  shown. Desktop does this because its markdown renderer
  does not include the GFM strikethrough extension. Inline
  code and fenced code keep the tildes.
- A line that is only a thematic break (`---`, `***`,
  `___`, including spaces between the marks, up to three
  leading spaces) renders as a thin muted rule across the
  bubble. A solid dash underline under a paragraph stays a
  setext heading, not a rule.

While the reply is still streaming, the LEFT bubble stays
as it renders today. Copy on the message, Copy on a code
block, and Copy on a table still copy markdown source,
including the `#` marks, the `~~` marks, and the rule
line. A fence or a `$$` block that contains those
characters stays a fence or math. User-right messages stay
plain text. Tool lines, widgets, approve cards, and
connect cards are not headings, strikes, or rules.

No animation. Reduce Motion does not add a fade. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

or a user RIGHT `kind=message` shows its destination before

until blur. A pointer click does not pin the pill after
the pointer leaves. A URL longer than 64 characters
truncates in the middle with an ellipsis, keeping the head
and the tail. The pill stays in the left half of the column
so it does not cover Jump to latest or Stop. The accessible name is the full URL. A click
still prevents in-app navigation and opens the system
browser, the same path as today. The pill does not take
pointer events, so Jump to latest and Stop stay clickable.

actions are **Open Link** and **Copy Link**. Open Link uses
the same open path as a tap. Copy Link writes the
destination URL to the pasteboard, not the visible label.
A normal tap still opens. Long-press on text that is not a
link keeps the existing bubble menu (time, Copy, Quote).

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, links stay as they
are today: a finished URL in the plain bubble can still be
opened, and it does not raise the status pill or this
context menu. Tool lines, widgets, approve cards, and

## 136. v1.36 — ordered list start numbers and nested lists in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` renders GFM /
CommonMark lists the way Grok Bot does.

An ordered list keeps the number on its first marker.
`3. three` / `4. four` renders as 3 then 4. After an
interrupting paragraph or a fenced code block, the next
ordered list keeps its own start, so `4. beta` stays 4
and does not restart at 1. A `)` delimiter (`9) nine`) is
the same rule; the painted marker is still `9.`.

A nested list is an indented `-`, `+`, `*`, or `1.` under
a list item, at least two levels. It renders indented,
with its own marker, instead of being flattened into the
parent line or shown as literal marker text. Bullets use
a disc, then a circle, then a square. Ordered markers stay
numbers. A list item can contain inline markdown, and a
deeper list is just another list in that item.

Desktop: react-markdown on main already emitted
`<ol start="N">` and nested `<ul>` / `<ol>`. This cut
forwards `start` on the `ol` element and sets the nested
marker types in CSS (`disc` / `circle` / `square`,
`decimal` for ordered lists) so a later style cannot drop
them. Padding stays 1.2em.

iOS: the reply is drawn in a text view. That view does not
paint Swift list presentation markers, so a list was a
plain paragraph and an ordered list could not keep its
start. Completed lists are now rows: the marker sits
beside the item, and the item body is markdown again, so
a nested list indents under its parent marker. The first
marker's number is the start; later items in that same
list count up from it.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays as
it renders today. Fences and `$$` blocks that contain list
markers stay fences and math. Copy on the message, Copy on
a code block, and Copy on a table still copy markdown
source. Tool lines, widgets, approve cards, and connect
cards are unchanged.

No animation. Reduce Motion does not add one. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

### Prior slices

v1.35 link destinations stand. v1.32 task lists and
blockquotes stand. v0.11 assistant markdown stands.
Streaming LEFT bubbles stay as they are. Copy still copies
markdown source.

Out of scope: VNC / full Box GUI, extra channel types, public
store, coding-agent, new surfaces, a new HTTP route, bumping
OpenAPI past 0.18.0, a second PR.

## 137. v1.37 — syntax highlighting in completed assistant code blocks (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` fenced code block
colors the code when the fence's info string names a
recognized language. The v0.11 label still shows that same
token (`python`, `ts`) and still shows nothing when the
fence is unlabeled. This cut does not add a second label.

Recognized languages, case-insensitive, are the first token
of the info string:

- python (`py`)
- javascript (`js`, `jsx`) and typescript (`ts`, `tsx`)
- json
- bash (`sh`, `shell`, `zsh`)
- swift
- go (`golang`)
- rust (`rs`)
- yaml (`yml`)
- html (`htm`)
- css
- sql
- diff

Keywords, strings, comments, and numbers take a muted color.
Other characters stay the fence's normal text color. The
colors follow appearance. On a dark fence they are a muted
periwinkle, sage, gray, and tan (`#9aa8e8`, `#8fbf9a`,
`#8b8d94`, `#c4a574`). On a light fence they are darker
(`#3d4f99`, `#2f6b45`, `#6b6d75`, `#8a5a22`). iOS uses the
same pairs and swaps them with the system appearance. They
stay readable on the elevated fence background.

An unlabeled fence stays monochrome. A language outside that
set stays monochrome. `mermaid` stays a diagram (v0.45); a
failed diagram falls back to the plain fence. `$$` math
(v0.46) is unchanged. Inline code stays the accent chip.

Highlighting runs in the client. The tokenizer ships in the
repo (`desktop/src/codeHighlight.ts`,
`ios/SnorlaxBot/CodeHighlight.swift`). Nothing is fetched.
There is no new HTTP route.

A diff fence colors a header line (`diff`, `index`, `---`,
`+++`, `@@`) as a comment, an added line as a string, and a
removed line as a keyword. An html fence colors a tag name
as a keyword and an attribute quote as a string. A `tsx` /
`jsx` fence colors a tag the same way, and leaves a generic
`Array<string>` as a type, not a tag.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays as
it renders today. Copy on the message still copies markdown
source. Copy on the code block still copies the raw fence
body — no color markup, no language tag — whether or not
the lines are highlighted. Wrap / Unwrap and the language
label keep working. In-chat find still marks matches inside
the fence, on top of the token color.

No animation. The colors are static, including when Reduce
Motion is off. Reduce Motion does not add a fade. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

mermaid diagrams stand. v0.46 TeX math stands. v0.66

## 138. v1.38 — GFM footnotes in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` that contains a
GFM footnote renders it. A reference is `[^label]`, where
the label is letters, digits, `_`, or `-` (`[^1]`,
`[^note]`). It paints as a small superscript of the
footnote's number. The matching definition is an own line
`[^label]: note text`, optionally continued by indented
lines. Definitions are removed from the body and shown once,
at the end of the message, as a muted numbered list titled
Footnotes. The number follows the first reference in the
message. Another reference to that same label uses the same
number.

On desktop, the marker is a button. Activating it scrolls
the note into view and focuses it. Each note has a back-link
that scrolls back to the first marker for that number and
focuses it. The scroll is instant. On iOS, tapping the
marker presents the note in a popover. A long-press on the
marker does not open a browser and does not show the link
destination preview. http and https links still open the way
they did, and a long-press on those links still shows the
destination.

A reference with no definition stays the characters
`[^label]`. A definition with no reference stays in the
body as written. A second definition for a label that
already matched stays in the body as written. References
and definitions inside a fenced code block, an inline code
span, or TeX (`$$` or `\( \)`) stay literal. A blank line
before a definition does not leave the definition as its
own bubble; the list is part of the message.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays as
it renders today, including the footnote characters.
Copy on the message still copies markdown source. Copy on a
code block and Copy on a table still copy their source.
In-chat find still searches the reply, including the note
text.

No animation. The marker, the list, and the popover contents
do not fade, including when Reduce Motion is off. Reduce
Motion does not add a fade. No new shortcut, so the
shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures & shortcuts sheet do
not gain a row.

strikethrough, and thematic breaks stand. v1.34 www
autolinks stand. v1.35 link destinations stand. v1.36
ordered lists stand. v1.37 syntax highlighting stands.
Streaming LEFT bubbles stay as they are. Copy still copies
markdown source.

## 139. v1.39 — CommonMark reference-style links in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` that contains a
CommonMark reference link renders it as an inline link.
Three forms resolve against a link reference definition
anywhere in that same message:

- Full: `[text][ref]`
- Collapsed: `[ref][]`
- Shortcut: `[ref]`

A definition is an own line `[ref]: https://example.com`,
optionally with a title in double quotes, single quotes, or
parentheses (`"Optional title"`). Angle-bracket destinations
(`<https://example.com>`) are accepted. The destination or
the title may sit on the following line. Labels match
case-insensitively after leading and trailing whitespace is
trimmed and internal whitespace is collapsed to one space.
The first definition for a label wins. Later definitions for
that label are not shown either.

The resolved link uses the same styling as an inline link
written `[text](url)`. On desktop, hover and keyboard focus
show the v1.35 destination preview. On iOS, a long-press
shows Open Link and Copy Link for that same destination.
Only `http` and `https` open. `javascript:`, `file:`, and
any other scheme stay plain text, the way an inline link
with that destination already does. A reference image
`![alt][ref]` resolves to an inline image and keeps the
existing image rendering.

Definition lines are removed from the body. They do not
become their own bubble. An unresolved reference stays the
characters that were written. A definition inside a fenced
code block, an inline code span, or TeX (`$$` or `\( \)`)
is not a definition, and a reference in those regions stays
literal.

`[^1]` and `[^note]` stay footnotes. A footnote definition
`[^1]: note text` is not a link definition. A task-list
marker (`- [ ]`, `- [x]`, `- [X]`) stays a task marker even
when a link label with that letter exists. Pipe tables keep
their rows. An existing inline link `[text](url)` is left
pointing at `url`.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays as
it renders today, including the reference characters.
Copy on the message still copies markdown source. Copy on a
code block and Copy on a table still copy their source.
In-chat find still searches the reply.

No animation. No new shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the
iOS Gestures & shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

### Prior slices

v0.11 assistant markdown stands. v1.31 tables stand.
v1.32 blockquotes and task lists stand. v1.33 headings,
strikethrough, and thematic breaks stand. v1.34 www
autolinks stand. v1.35 link destinations stand. v1.36
ordered lists stand. v1.37 syntax highlighting stands.
v1.38 GFM footnotes stand. Streaming LEFT bubbles stay as
they are. Copy still copies markdown source.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` honors CommonMark

A backslash before an ASCII punctuation character renders
that character and does not let it start markdown syntax.
`\*not bold\*`, `5 \> 3`, `\# not a heading`, `\- not a
list`, and `1\. not ordered` are ordinary text. A
backslash before any other character, including a newline
that is a hard line break, stays a backslash.

Named character references from the common HTML5 set
decode: `amp`, `lt`, `gt`, `quot`, `apos`, `nbsp`, `copy`,
`reg`, `trade`, `hellip`, `mdash`, `ndash`, `lsquo`,
`rsquo`, `ldquo`, `rdquo`, `times`, `divide`, `deg`,
`plusmn`, `middot`, `bull`, `euro`, `pound`, `yen`,
`cent`, `sect`, `para`, `larr`, `rarr`, `uarr`, `darr`,
and the check marks `check` and `checkmark`. Decimal
(`&#8212;`) and hexadecimal (`&#x1F600;`, `&#X1F600;`)
numeric references decode to that character. An unknown
name (`&notanentity;`) and a reference with no semicolon
stay literal. A numeric value that is zero, a surrogate,
a noncharacter, a disallowed control, or greater than
U+10FFFF becomes U+FFFD.

Inside a code span or a fenced code block, backslashes
and character references stay exactly as written. An
indented code block does too. Escaped text is painted as
text. `&lt;script&gt;` shows `<script>` and is not HTML.

Inline `\(` `\)` and own-line `$$` are recognized as TeX
before escape handling, so those delimiters keep working
and the formula source is unchanged. A footnote, a
reference link, a pipe table, a task list, a www or
http(s) autolink, the link destination preview, and
syntax highlighting keep working. An escaped `www\.`
or `\~~` does not become a link or strikethrough.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays
plain text, including the backslashes and entity text.
Copy on the message still copies markdown source. Copy on
a code block and Copy on a table still copy their source.
In-chat find still searches the reply.

No animation. No new shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the
iOS Gestures & shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

## 141. v1.41 — CommonMark hard line breaks in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` honors CommonMark
hard line breaks.

A line that ends in two or more spaces, or in an unescaped
backslash, followed by a newline inside a paragraph renders
as a visible line break. Those trailing spaces and that
backslash are not shown. Spaces before a trailing backslash
stay. Leading spaces and tabs on the next line are ignored.

A plain single newline is a soft break. It renders as a
space, so the paragraph is one wrapping block. One trailing
space, or a trailing tab, is also a soft break. This matches
CommonMark and Grok Bot's chat: blank lines already split
bubbles (v0.48), and the "real line breaks instead of stray
backslashes" behavior is this hard-break rule. A newline is
not turned into a break on its own.

At the end of a paragraph or other block there is no break.
Trailing spaces disappear. A backslash at the end of the
block stays visible. A setext underline (`---` or `===`)
closes a heading, so the marker before that underline is
not a break inside the heading text. A thematic break, a
heading, or a list that interrupts the paragraph keeps the
backslash and does not insert a break.

Inside a code span, a fenced code block, or an indented
code block, two spaces and a backslash before a newline
stay exactly as written. Inline `\(` `\)` and own-line
`$$` math are recognized first, so a hard-break marker
inside a formula stays in the formula. A pipe table that
starts on its own is still a table, and a newline inside
a cell is not a hard break. Raw HTML blocks and an inline
HTML tag that spans a newline keep the markers literal.
When a paragraph is already open, a following table line
does not interrupt it, so the hard break stays.

A backslash escape is applied first. An even run of
backslashes (`\\`) plus a newline is a literal backslash
and a soft break. An odd run leaves one backslash as the
hard-break marker. Strikethrough does not cross a hard
break. Footnotes, reference links, task lists, autolinks,
the link destination preview, and syntax highlighting
keep working.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays
plain text, including the trailing spaces and backslash.
Copy on the message still copies markdown source. Copy on
a code block and Copy on a table still copy their source.
In-chat find still searches the reply.

No animation. No new shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the
iOS Gestures & shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

autolinks stand. v1.35 link destinations stand. v1.36
ordered lists stand. v1.37 syntax highlighting stands.

## 142. v1.42 — CommonMark inline images in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second

untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` renders a
CommonMark inline image.

`![alt](https://example.com/a.png)` and
`![alt](https://example.com/a.png "title")` become an image
in the bubble. The image is sized to fit the bubble width
and keeps its aspect ratio. It is not stretched. The alt
text is the accessibility label. A title, in double quotes,
single quotes, or parentheses, is the tooltip. Angle-bracket
destinations (`<https://...>`) load the same way. An empty
alt still loads the image; the lightbox names it Image.

Reference-style `![alt][ref]` and `![alt][]` reuse the v1.39
link reference definitions. A resolved reference is rewritten
to an inline image and then rendered. An unresolved reference
stays the literal characters. Definition lines stay hidden,
the same as v1.39.

While the bytes are loading, the bubble shows a muted
placeholder. If the load fails, the alt text shows as muted
link-like text. When the alt is empty, that fallback is the
URL. There is no broken-image icon.

Clicking or tapping a rendered image opens the existing
v0.79 image lightbox (dimmed backdrop, Close, Esc, click or
tap outside, iOS swipe down). An image that is the entire
content of a link, `[![alt](img)](href)`, does not open the
lightbox. It opens the link destination, using the existing
http(s) safe-scheme rules and the existing destination
preview. On desktop, an image anywhere inside a link label
does the same: the link handles the click. On iOS, an image
that shares a link label with other words stays in that
link so the link text is preserved.

Only http and https URLs with a host load in the browser.
Existing local attachment URLs also load:
`/v1/attachments/{id}` and `/v1/images/{id}`, through the
same authenticated fetch the app already uses for bubble
attachments. javascript:, data:, file:, ftp:, and any other
scheme stay the literal marker. They are not requested.

Inside a code span, a fenced code block, an indented code
block, inline `\(` `\)` math, or a `$$` block, the marker
stays exactly as written. A code span inside a table cell
stays literal too. A table cell that is not code can show
an image. Escapes, hard breaks, footnotes, reference links,
strikethrough, task lists, autolinks, the link destination
preview, and syntax highlighting keep working.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays
plain text, including the image marker. Copy on the message
still copies markdown source. Copy on a code block and Copy
on a table still copy their source. In-chat find still
searches the reply.

No animation. No new shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the
iOS Gestures & shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

## 143. v1.43 — CommonMark angle-bracket autolinks in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` renders a
CommonMark angle-bracket autolink.

`<https://example.com/path>` becomes a link. The visible
text is the URL. The angle brackets are not shown.
`<user@example.com>` becomes a mailto link. The visible
text is the address. The destination is `mailto:` plus
that address. `foo+special@Bar.baz-bar0.com` and `foo@bar`
are emails too, matching CommonMark. A trailing period
stays outside the link.

Desktop hover and keyboard focus show that destination in
the existing v1.35 status pill. iOS long-press shows the
full destination in the existing Open / Copy Link menu.
Copy Link copies the destination, not the visible label.
A click or tap opens it through the same path as any other
link.

http and https still need a host. A mailto link opens only
when the destination is `mailto:` plus an email address,
with no query and no fragment.
`[go](mailto:user@example.com?subject=hi)` is not a link.
`<javascript:alert(1)>`, `<file:///etc/passwd>`,
`<ftp://files.example.com/a>`, `<data:text/html,hi>`, and
`<mailto:user@example.com>` (a URI whose scheme is mailto,
not an email autolink) stay the literal characters,
brackets included. `<not a link>`, a space inside the
brackets, and a one-character scheme such as `<m:abc>` stay
literal too. A backslash before the opening bracket
(`\<https://example.com>`, `\<user@example.com>`) stays
literal.

Inside a code span, a fenced code block, an indented code
block, inline `\(` `\)` math, or a `$$` block, the brackets
stay exactly as written. A code span inside a table cell
stays literal too. A table cell that is not code can show
the link. Images, hard breaks, escapes, footnotes,
reference links, strikethrough, task lists, the link
destination preview, and syntax highlighting keep working.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays

## 144. v1.44 — CommonMark setext headings in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` renders a
CommonMark setext heading with the same styling as an ATX
heading.

A paragraph followed by a line of one or more `=` is an H1.
A paragraph followed by a line of two or more `-` is an H2.
The underline may have up to three leading spaces and any
trailing spaces or tabs. Desktop draws it as the existing
16px semibold `h1` or 14px semibold `h2`. iOS draws it in
the same 16pt / 14pt semibold font. `Foo` then `===` is the
same heading as `# Foo`. `Foo` then `---` is the same
heading as `## Foo`.

Several lines with no blank line between them are one
heading. `Foo` then `Bar` then `===` is one H1 whose text
is both lines, with the line break still inside the
heading. Emphasis can span those lines (`Foo *bar` then
`baz*` then `====`).

A `---` after a blank line is a thematic break, not a
heading. A line of `=` with no paragraph above it stays
text. `---` directly under a list item, at the left margin,
ends the list and is a thematic break. The same underline
indented to the item's text (`- Foo` then `  ---`) is an
H2 inside that item. A paragraph continued under a list
item and then an underline at the left margin is a break
after the list, not a heading of the item.

A setext underline that would only match because a quote or
list was continued lazily stays the literal characters.
`> foo` then `bar` then `===` is a quote, then a paragraph
that still shows `===`. A backslash-escaped `\=` or `\-`
(`Foo` then `\===`) stays the characters `===`.

Inside a fenced code block, an indented code block, inline
`\(` `\)` math, a `$$` block, raw HTML, or a table cell,
an underline stays exactly as written. It is not a heading
and it is not a rule. ATX headings, strikethrough, thematic
breaks, task lists, quotes, images, hard breaks, escapes,
footnotes, reference links, autolinks, and syntax
highlighting keep working.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays
plain text. Copy on the message still copies markdown
source. Copy on a code block and Copy on a table still
copy their source. In-chat find still searches the reply.

No animation. No new shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the
iOS Gestures & shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

## 145. v1.45 — GFM email autolinks in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second

routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Task-list checkboxes already shipped in v1.32, including
`*` and `+` markers. Bare `www.` and `http(s)` autolinks
already shipped in v1.34 and v0.82. This cut is the
remaining GFM extended autolink.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` renders a bare
email as a link. `foo@bar.baz` shows that address and
opens `mailto:foo@bar.baz`. `mailto:foo@bar.baz` shows the
prefix as well and opens the same destination. Desktop
hover and keyboard focus show it in the existing v1.35
status pill. iOS long-press shows it in the existing
Open/Copy Link menu. A click or tap opens it through the
existing link path. The destination is `mailto:` plus the
address, with no query and no fragment.

The local part is one or more of alphanumeric, `.`, `-`,
`_`, or `+`. The domain is labels of alphanumeric, `-`, or
`_`, separated by dots, and it must contain at least one
dot. `+` after `@` stays plain, so `hello@mail+xyz.example`
is not a link and `hello+xyz@mail.example` is. A trailing
`.` is not part of the address. An address that would end
in `-` or `_` stays plain (`a.b-c_d@a.b-`,
`a.b-c_d@a.b_`). `foo@bar` has no dot, so it stays plain.
That is different from the v1.43 angle-bracket form, which
still links `<foo@bar>`.

`xmpp:foo@bar.baz` and any other scheme written against
the address stay the literal characters. An `http(s)` URL
that contains an `@` stays that URL. A `www.` autolink
stays a `www.` autolink.

Inside a code span, a fenced code block, or an indented
code block, the address stays exactly as written. A
backslash-escaped `@` (`user\@example.com`) stays the
characters and is not a link. A normal table cell can
still show the link.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble does not
turn addresses into links. User-right text, tool lines,
and cards stay as they are. Copy on the message still
copies markdown source. Copy on a code block and Copy on a
table still copy their source. In-chat find still searches
the reply.

iOS Gestures & shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

after whitespace is collapsed has none either. Leading and

## 147. v1.47 — CommonMark lazy blockquote continuation in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce

GFM footnotes already shipped in v1.38. Ordered lists that
honor a custom start number already shipped in v1.36.
Nested blockquotes (`>>`) already shipped in v1.32. This
cut is the CommonMark quote rule those slices left open.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` treats a line
that continues an open quote paragraph, and does not start
another block, as part of that quote. `> quoted` followed
by `still quoted` is one quote. The second line does not
need a `>` marker. Several such lines stay in the same
quote, and a later line that does have a marker stays in
it too. Nested quotes keep the continuation on the inside:
`> > foo` then `bar` is still nested, and `bar` is inside
the inner quote.

A blank line ends the quote. The next line without `>` is
a paragraph after it. A list marker in the first three
columns ends the quote, including `2.` and a task marker
`- [ ]`. An ATX heading (`#` through `######` followed by
a space or the end of the line) ends the quote. A fence
and a thematic break (`---`, `***`, `___`) end the quote.
An own-line `$$` ends the quote so math stays a math
block. `#bar` and `-bar` are not a heading or a list, so
they stay in the quote. A list item that already has text
keeps a following plain line on that item (`> 1. foo` then
`bar`). An empty list item does not. Indented code stays
code: a quote marker, then four spaces of code, then a
later four-space line, is a code block inside the quote
and another code block after it.

A setext underline that would only match because of this
continuation still shows its characters. `> foo` then
`bar` then `===` is one quote whose paragraph shows
`===`. It is not a heading. `---` after the quote is still
a thematic break. v1.44 stands.

Inside a fenced code block or a `$$` block, the characters
stay exactly as written. While a LEFT reply is still
streaming, the bubble stays plain text. User-right text,
tool lines, and cards stay as they are. Copy on the
message still copies markdown source. Copy on a code block
and Copy on a table still copy their source. In-chat find
still searches the reply.

## 148. v1.48 — CommonMark indented code blocks in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second

Fenced code blocks already shipped in v0.11. Copy on a
fence already shipped in v0.66. The soft-wrap toggle
already shipped in v1.29. Syntax highlighting for a
recognized info string already shipped in v1.37. This cut
is the CommonMark indented code block those slices left
open. An indented block has no info string.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` treats a run of
lines indented by four or more columns as a code block
when the run is not a paragraph continuation and not
list-item content. Four spaces count. A tab counts as
indent to the next column of four, so a leading tab is
enough. The block is drawn with the existing fence chrome:
monospace body, the Copy control that reads Copied for
1.5s, and the Wrap control beside it. Wrap still defaults
to horizontal scroll and still lasts only for the app
session. The language label stays empty. Nothing is
highlighted.

Blank lines inside the run stay in the block, including a
line that is only spaces. Trailing blank lines are not
part of the block. One indent level, four columns, is
removed from each code line. Further spaces stay. An
indented line cannot interrupt a paragraph, so `Foo` then
`    bar` stays one paragraph. A list continuation stays
with the item. A loose list continuation that the list
parser still owns stays with the list. A heading, a
thematic break, or a finished fence can be followed by
indented code. Lines inside a fence or a `$$` block stay
in that fence or block.

A blank line inside the code block does not split the
assistant bubble. A blank line outside one still does.
Streaming LEFT bubbles stay plain text. User-right text,
tool lines, and cards stay as they are. Copy on the
message still copies markdown source. Copy on the code
block copies the dedented code, not the indent. In-chat
find still searches the reply.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

## 149. v1.49 — GFM task items keep indented continuation in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Task lists already shipped in v1.32. Hard line breaks
already shipped in v1.41. Backslash escapes and HTML
character references already shipped in v1.40. Ordered-list
start numbers already shipped in v1.36. Nested blockquotes
already shipped in v1.32. This cut is the continuation a
task item still dropped.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` keeps a following
line inside a task item when that line is indented at least
to the item's text column. That column is the column after
the bullet (`-`, `*`, or `+`) and the space that follows it,
not the column after the checkbox. Two spaces under
`- [ ]` continue the item. The checkbox stays in the item
text, and the continuation is dedented, so the item body is
markdown again. A nested `- [ ]` on that indent renders as
a task list inside the item. An indented bullet renders as
a nested list. A plain indented line stays in the same
item. A single newline inside the item is still a soft
break.

A line that is not indented that far ends the item. Another
task in the first three columns is the next item of the
same list. A blank line ends the item when the next
non-blank line is not indented into it. When that next line
is another margin task, the list continues and the blank is
not part of either item. When that next line is indented
into the item, the blank stays in the item. A blank line in
the message body still splits the assistant into bubbles, so
a loose continuation across that blank is two bubbles. A
quote that kept the blank still applies this rule.

A fence or a `$$` block indented to the text column stays
in the item, and the blank lines inside that fence or block
stay with it. A fence or a `$$` block at the margin ends
the list. A task marker inside a fence or a `$$` block
stays literal. Streaming LEFT bubbles stay plain text.
User-right text, tool lines, and cards stay as they are.
Copy on the message still copies markdown source. The
glyphs stay read-only. Checked items stay muted.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

code blocks stand. A list continuation that the list parser

## 151. v1.51 — CommonMark loose and tight lists in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Ordered-list start numbers and nested lists already shipped
in v1.36. Task lists already shipped in v1.32. A task item's
indented continuation already shipped in v1.49. Indented
code blocks already shipped in v1.48. This cut is the
spacing CommonMark gives those lists.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` renders a tight
list with compact item spacing and a loose list with
paragraph spacing. A list is loose when a blank line
separates any of its items, or when any item directly
contains two block-level elements with a blank line between
them. Otherwise the list is tight, and every item of a
loose list picks up that paragraph spacing. That is
CommonMark's loose/tight rule.

Bullet lists (`-`, `*`, and `+`), ordered lists with `.` or
`)`, and GFM task lists follow the same rule. On desktop, a
bullet or ordered list already becomes CommonMark HTML, so
a loose item's paragraph keeps the 8px paragraph gap and a
tight item, which has no paragraph wrapper, stays compact.
A task row uses a 4px gap when the list is tight and the
8px paragraph gap when it is loose. iOS draws the same
lists in a stack: 4pt when tight, 8pt when loose.

A nested list keeps its own looseness. A blank line inside
the nested list does not loosen the parent. A blank line
between the parent's own blocks does not change the child.
A blank line inside a fence, a `$$` block, or an indented
code block stays inside that block and does not loosen the
list. A blank line between a paragraph and one of those
blocks does.

A blank line that belongs to a list stays in that bubble,
so the list arrives whole and can render loose. A blank
line that ends the list, and any other blank line outside
a list, still splits the reply into bubbles. A fence or a
`$$` block at the margin still ends the list. Streaming
LEFT bubbles stay plain text. User-right text, tool lines,
and cards stay as they are. Copy on the message, Copy on a
code block, and Copy on a table still copy markdown source.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

### Prior slices

v1.32 blockquotes and task lists stand. v1.36 ordered-list
start numbers and nested lists stand. v1.48 indented code
blocks stand. v1.49 task-item continuation stands. A list
continuation that the list parser owns stays with that
list. Streaming LEFT bubbles stay as they are. Copy still
copies markdown source.

## 158. v1.58 — CommonMark HTML blocks in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Indented code blocks already shipped in v1.48. Lazy
blockquote continuation already shipped in v1.47. Link
reference definitions already match case-insensitively
after collapsed whitespace (v1.39). GFM single-tilde
strikethrough already shipped in v1.50. Bullet characters
already split lists in v1.57. Lists that do not interrupt
a paragraph already shipped in v1.56. Raw inline HTML such
as `<br>`, `<kbd>`, `<sub>`, and `<sup>` already shows as
literal text on desktop and iOS, with no script execution.
Desktop react-markdown already ends a list at an HTML
block. iOS was still keeping that tag line inside the
item, so a later marker became another item. This cut is
that block.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` follows
CommonMark's HTML blocks. Types 1–6 interrupt a paragraph,
a list item, and a lazy blockquote. The tag line stays
outside the list or quote, and so do the lines the block
owns: through the closing tag for a comment, script,
style, pre, textarea, processing instruction, declaration,
or CDATA, and through the line before the next blank line
for a block tag such as `<div>` or `<hr>`. Up to three
leading spaces still count. Four or more columns, or a
tab, stay indented code. A type 7 tag (`<br>`, `<br/>`,
`<BR>`, `<span class="a">`) cannot interrupt a paragraph.
On a lazy continuation it stays in the item or quote and
ends further lazy lines; an indented continuation of a
list item can still belong to that item. A marker inside
the block, including `9.`, is not a list. After the block
ends, the paragraph is closed, so `9.` can start a list.
A blank line inside a comment, script, style, pre, or
textarea stays in that bubble. A blank line that ends a
`<div>` still splits bubbles. Tags stay literal text.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays
plain text. Copy on the message still copies markdown
source. User-right text, tool lines, and cards stay as
they are.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

## 159. v1.59 — GFM ordered task lists in completed assistant replies (locked)

untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /

Bullet task lists already shipped in v1.32. `- [ ]`,
`- [x]`, `* [X]`, and `+ [x]` render as read-only checkbox
glyphs, and a checked item is muted. A task item's indented
continuation, including a nested task, already shipped in
v1.49. Ordered-list start numbers already shipped in v1.36.
CommonMark HTML blocks already shipped in v1.58. This cut
is the task marker on an ordered item. `1. [ ]` was still
the characters `[ ]`.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` treats `[ ]`,
`[x]`, and `[X]` at the start of an ordered item as a
read-only checkbox. `1. [ ] todo` is unchecked. `2. [x] done`
and `3. [X] also` are checked, and the row is muted. The
number stays, including a start other than 1 (`9. [ ]`,
`10. [x]`). The `)` delimiter is the same list rule as
`1)`. A nested marker indented into the item stays there:
`1. [ ] parent` then an indented `- [ ] child` is a task
inside that item, and `- parent` then an indented
`1. [ ] child` is an ordered task inside the bullet. A
mixed list keeps one number sequence, so `1. [ ]`, `2. plain`,
`3. [x]` is one list and only the task rows have a glyph.

The marker is the brackets at the start of the item, after
at most three spaces, then a space, a tab, or the end of
the line. `1. [x]done` has no space, so it stays the
characters. `1. see [ ] later` is not at the start, so it
stays the characters. Four spaces before `[ ]` is indented
code, not a task. A code span that starts the item
(``1. `[x] kept` ``) stays a code span. `[x]` inside a
later code span stays in that span. A backslash before `[`
stays a literal bracket. A fenced code block stays the
source. An ordered marker that would not interrupt a
paragraph still does not: `14. [ ]` after a sentence stays
text, and `1. [ ]` still interrupts. An HTML block of
types 1–6 still ends the list before the tag line.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays
plain text. Copy on the message still copies markdown
source. Bullet task lists keep the glyphs they already had.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

Setext headings already shipped in v1.44. A paragraph
followed by `===` is h1. Followed by `---` it is h2. A
`---` after a blank line stays a thematic break, and a
list marker is not an underline. ATX closing hashes
already match CommonMark in react-markdown and in
cmark-gfm, which iOS uses: `## Title ##` and
`# Title #####` drop the trailing hashes, `# C#` and

Nested `***bold italic***` and `___bold italic___`

This cut is the mismatch those left. Desktop already

comment. `<!DOCTYPE html>` and `<!A>` are declarations.

`<3>`, `< a>`, `<__>`, and an unclosed `<` are not tags.

a comment, a processing instruction, a declaration,
CDATA, and the other block tags still end a list item
and a lazy quote. A blank line inside a script or a
comment still does not start a list. A type 7 tag on
its own line, including `<br>` after a blank line, is
a paragraph of those characters.

`<https://example.com>` and `<user@example.com>` stay
links. A `<` in a link destination, including
`[a](http://ex.com/<foo>)` and `[a](<http://ex.com>)`,
stays in that destination. A code span, a fenced code
block, and an indented code block stay literal.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays

## 161. v1.61 — CommonMark character references stay characters inside raw HTML in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Backslash escapes and HTML character references already
shipped in v1.40. Raw HTML stays literal text in v1.60.
Both use the same `§§SBE` token. This cut is what happens
when a character reference sits inside a tag or an inline
comment that the raw-HTML pass stashes.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` decodes the
common named references and numeric references to
characters. `&amp;` `&lt;` `&gt;` `&copy;` show & < > ©.
`&nbsp;` is a no-break space. `&hellip;` is …. `&#35;`
is `#`. `&#x1F600;` and `&#X1F600;` are the emoji.
`&#X22;` is a quotation mark. An unknown name `&foo;`
stays literal. A reference with no semicolon, such as
`&amp`, stays literal. Eight decimal digits
(`&#87654321;`) are not a reference and stay literal.
A bare `&` stays literal. `&#0;` and the other
disallowed numeric values are U+FFFD.

The decoded character is not markdown syntax. `&#42;foo&#42;`
shows `*foo*` and is not italic. `&#35; x` at the start
of a line is not a heading. A real `#` and a real `*`
still are. Inside a code span or a fenced code block the
reference stays the source characters.

A link destination and a link title decode the same way.
`[a](/u?x=1&amp;y=2)` has destination `/u?x=1&y=2`.
`[a](https://example.com/u?x=1&amp;y=2 "A &amp; B")`
has that URL and the title `A & B`. `&#X22;` inside a
title is a quotation mark and does not end the title.

A raw HTML tag or a mid-line comment that already holds
one of those tokens paints the character, not the token.
`<a href="?x=1&amp;y=2">` shows
`<a href="?x=1&y=2">`. `foo <!-- &amp; --> bar` shows
the comment with `&`. `<span title="&#35;">` shows `#`
in the title and is not a heading. A type 1–6 HTML block
that starts a line stays a block, and a reference in it
still decodes when that text is shown. Code, fences, and
indented code are not rewritten.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays
plain text. Copy on the message still copies markdown
source.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

v1.60 raw HTML literal text stands. v1.46 link titles
stand. v1.43 angle autolinks stand. Streaming LEFT
bubbles stay plain text. Copy on the message still
copies markdown source.

## 162. v1.62 — GFM extended autolink trailing punctuation in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Bare `www.` autolinks already shipped in v1.34. On main
they already follow cmark-gfm's extended-autolink
delimiter, on desktop and on iOS. Bare `http://` and
`https://` already shipped in v0.82, with a shorter
trimmer: `.` `,` `;` `:` `!` `?` `)` and quotes stayed
outside, and `<` already ended the link, but a trailing
`*` `_` or `~` stayed inside the link, and a trailing
entity-like `&hl;` lost only the semicolon. Angle-bracket
autolinks already shipped in v1.43. GFM email autolinks
already shipped in v1.45. v1.61 character references
inside raw HTML already shipped.

This cut is that http(s) delimiter. A completed assistant
LEFT `kind=message` keeps a trailing `?`, `!`, `.`, `,`,
`:`, `*`, `_`, or `~` outside a bare `www.` or `http(s)://`
autolink. The same character stays inside when it is not
trailing, so `https://example.com/a*b` and
`https://example.com/a?q=1` keep the mark. A trailing `)`
is excluded only when parentheses are unbalanced.
`(see https://example.com/a_(b))` links
`https://example.com/a_(b)` and the inner `)` stays.
`https://example.com/Pikachu_(Electric)` stays whole.
`https://example.com/Pikachu_(Electric))` drops the extra
`)`. A trailing entity-like `&hl;` is excluded, so
`https://example.com/search?q=commonmark&hl;` links
through `commonmark` and the `&hl;` stays text. A lone
`;` stays outside. `<` ends the link. `www.` already
did this. http(s) now calls that same delimiter on both
clients. A trailing `]`, `}`, or curly quote on an
http(s) autolink still stays outside, as v0.82 already
did. A balanced `https://example.com/a[b]` keeps the
brackets.

An explicit `[label](url)` is unchanged, so
`[go](https://example.com/a*)` keeps the `*` in the
destination. `<https://example.com/a*>` keeps the `*`
too, because the brackets delimit it. A URL inside a
code span or a fenced code block stays plain.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, a URL that still
touches the end of the buffer stays plain, including
when it ends in `*` or `&hl;`. A URL earlier in that
buffer is still a link, with this delimiter. Copy on
the message still copies markdown source. User-right
bare URLs use this same splitter. Tool lines and cards
stay plain.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

stand. v1.45 GFM email autolinks stand. v1.61 character
references inside raw HTML stand. Streaming LEFT bubbles
keep the end-of-buffer rule. Copy on the message still

## 163. v1.63 — CommonMark tight list block spacing in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Ordered-list start numbers and nested lists already shipped
in v1.36. A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` already
nests lists to any depth on both clients. Desktop
react-markdown emits nested `<ul>` and `<ol>`. iOS dedents
each item and parses the nested list again. Bullets are a
disc, then a circle, then a square. From the third bullet
on, including depth 4 and deeper, the marker stays a
square, matching the desktop rules `.assistant-md ul ul ul`
and the WebKit sample stylesheet. Ordered markers stay
decimal numbers (`3.` then `4.`), including at depth 4.
Padding on desktop stays 1.2em per list. iOS indents each
level under its parent marker. That rendering was already
on main.

Loose and tight lists already shipped in v1.51. A blank
line between items, or between two blocks of one item,
makes the list loose. A nested list keeps its own
looseness. What was still wrong is the gap in front of a
nested list. On desktop a tight item is not wrapped in
`<p>`, so the nested list sits flush with the text. A
loose item is wrapped in `<p>`, so the paragraph's 8px
margin sits in front of the nested list even when that
item's own nested list has no blank line of its own. iOS
always used the 8pt block gap, so a tight list gained
another 8pt at every level. At four levels that extra gap
stacked. This cut uses the list's looseness for that gap.
Tight is flush (0). Loose is 8px. Outside a list the block
gap stays 8px. A quote inside an item keeps that 8px for
its own blocks. A nested list's item spacing stays its
own: compact when that nested list is tight, paragraph
spacing when it is loose.

A bullet task list is a `ul` on desktop, with the checkbox
in place of a marker. The next bullet is therefore a
circle, then a square, and depth 4+ stays a square. iOS
counts that task row as one list level so the markers
match. A loose task item uses the same 8px column gap
before a nested list. A tight task item stays flush.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays
plain text. Copy on the message still copies markdown
source.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row.

`[docs](https://example.com)`, `~~gone~~`, and `~gone~`
still format. The backslash that keeps the marker is
painted away by the existing escape pass, so the cell
shows the character and not a stray `\`.

Setext lines still literalize first, so `---` and `===`
stay the characters they already were. An escaped pipe
and a pipe inside inline code still do not split the
cell. Copy on the message and Copy on the table still
copy markdown source, including the original `- item`
and `\|`. While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the

`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Pipe tables already shipped in v1.31. Column alignment
(`:---` left, `:---:` center, `---:` right) already shipped
in v1.55. v1.65 clips a body row to the header and pads a
short row, and left a pipe-less continuation out of scope.
GFM's example is `| abc | def |` then `| --- | --- |` then
`| bar | baz |` then `bar`, a blank line, then `bar`. The
pipe-less `bar` is a row. The blank line ends the table.
The last `bar` is a paragraph.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now does that,
on desktop and on iOS. The pipe-less line is one cell.
v1.65 pads the rest, so a two-column table shows `bar` and
an empty cell. The empty cell keeps the delimiter
alignment. `| L | R |` then `| :--- | ---: |` then `plain`
is left and right. Up to three leading spaces are trimmed.
`foo \| bar` stays one cell; the existing escape pass
paints the pipe. `#foo` is not an ATX heading, `===` is
not a rule, `*foo` is not a list, and `1234567890. item`
is not an ordered list, so those stay cells.
`<span>x</span>` stays a cell. The clipboard source is
still the original lines, so Copy on the table includes
the pipe-less line. Copy on the message still copies
markdown source.

A block start still ends the table and is not a cell.
That is a heading (`# heading`, up to three spaces), a
thematic break (`---`, `* * *`, `___`), a quote, a list
item (`- item`, `1. item`, `2) item`, and an empty `-`
or `1.`), a fence (`` ``` `` or `~~~`), a line indented
four columns or a tab, a raw HTML block (`<div>`,
`<span>` alone, `<!-- c -->`, and the other type 1–7
starts), and a `$$` math line. The block is rendered
after the table. A pipe-less header does not start a
table, so `foo` then `---` stays the setext heading it
already was. A `[ref]: url` line or a `[^1]: note` line
with no blank line before it stays in the cell. The same
line after a blank line is still a link reference or a
footnote. While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the
bubble stays plain text and this pass is a no-op.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row. User-right text, tool
lines, and cards stay as they are.

### Prior slices

v1.31 pipe tables stand. v1.55 column alignment and
escaped pipes stand. v1.64 inline cells stand. v1.65
clipping and padding stand. `snake_case_name` stays
literal. Streaming LEFT bubbles stay plain text. Copy on
the message still copies markdown source.

Out of scope: a pipe-less header (`foo` then `| --- |`),
VNC / full Box GUI, extra channel types, public store,
coding-agent, new surfaces, a new HTTP route, bumping
OpenAPI past 0.18.0, a second PR. `workspaceTree.ts`
(never reintroduce `computerPane.ts`) stands.

OpenAPI **0.18.0** (not bumped).

Both completed-reply parsers already follow the CommonMark
intraword `_` rule. Desktop react-markdown uses micromark.
iOS full markdown uses cmark-gfm. `snake_case_name`,
`foo_bar_baz`, and `foo__bar__baz` stay literal.
`_emphasis_` and `__strong__` at a word boundary still
render. `foo*bar*baz` still emphasizes, because `*` has
no intraword ban. That part was already shipped. It was
not covered by its own tests.

The same parsers still treat a word-boundary dunder as
strong emphasis, so `__init__` became bold `init`. This
cut stashes that identifier, the other Python data-model
names (`__name__`, `__main__`, `__file__`, and the rest
of the shared list), and any `__name__` whose name itself
contains `_` (`__init_subclass__`, `__foo_bar__`). The
underscores stay visible. `__strong__` is not on that
list and has no internal `_`, so it stays strong. A run
of three (`___bold italic___`, `___init___`) is unchanged.
`*__init__*` is explicit emphasis, so the identifier can
still be italic and the underscores stay.

Code spans, fenced code, and indented code are not
stashed. A link destination, an `http` or `www` URL, and
an email address are not stashed. While a LEFT reply is
still streaming, the bubble stays plain text and this
pass is a no-op. Copy on the message still copies
markdown source.

shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row. User-right text, tool
lines, and cards stay as they are.

## 168. v1.68 — CommonMark Unicode symbols count as emphasis punctuation in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

CommonMark reference-style links already shipped in v1.39.
GFM footnotes already shipped in v1.38. Both already resolve
on desktop and iOS, including full `[text][ref]`, collapsed
`[ref][]`, and shortcut `[ref]` links, and a footnote list
for `[^1]`. This cut is the emphasis mismatch those slices
left open.

Desktop react-markdown uses micromark. iOS full markdown
uses cmark-gfm. Micromark treats a BMP Unicode symbol
(`£`, `©`, `±`, `™`, `°`, `¥`, `×`, `→`) as punctuation
when it decides whether `*` can open or close emphasis.
cmark-gfm does not. On a completed assistant LEFT
`kind=message`, `*£*bravo`, `alpha*£*`, `*±*1`, `*™*brand`,
`a*©*b`, `**£**bravo`, and `***£***bravo` therefore stayed
literal asterisks on desktop and became emphasis on iOS.

The symbol is stashed before either parser, in the same
`§§SBE` token escapes already use. The token begins and
ends with `§`, which both parsers count as punctuation, so
the flanking rule matches. Restore paints the symbol. The
asterisks stay visible and are not emphasis.

`*£50*`, `price *£50* today`, `(*©*)`, `**©** notice`,
`*°C*`, `*foo*`, `foo*bar*baz`, and `***bold italic***`
still emphasize. A non-BMP emoji such as `*😀*bravo` is
not a BMP symbol, and both parsers already emphasize it,
so it is left alone. ASCII symbols (`~`, `|`, `$`, `` ` ``)
are not stashed either: both parsers already count them
as punctuation, and they mark strikethrough, tables,
math, and code. `~~£~~` still strikes. `__init__`
stays literal.

Code spans, fenced code, and indented code are not
stashed. A link destination, an `http` or `www` URL, and
an email address are not stashed. The link label can
still use the rule:
`[*£*bravo](https://example.com/a)` keeps the asterisks
and still opens that URL. While a LEFT reply is still
streaming, the bubble stays plain text and this pass is
a no-op. Copy on the message still copies markdown source.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row. User-right text, tool
lines, and cards stay as they are.

v1.39 reference-style links stand. v1.38 footnotes stand.
v1.67 intraword underscores stand. Streaming LEFT bubbles
stay plain text. Copy on the message still copies markdown
source.
