## 169. v1.69 — CommonMark over-long numeric character references stay literal in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

CommonMark setext headings already shipped in v1.44, and
they already match on both clients. Desktop react-markdown
uses micromark. iOS full markdown uses cmark-gfm. Both
agree on the setext and thematic-break examples: `Title`
then `===` is h1, `Title` then `---` is h2, a `---` after
a blank line is a thematic break, `- - -` and `***` are
thematic breaks, a `---` directly under a list item or on
a blockquote lazy continuation follows CommonMark, `Foo`
then `---` then `bar` is an h2 and a paragraph, and a
setext underline inside a fence or under a table stays
out of the heading. In-range character references and
ASCII backslash escapes already shipped in v1.40 and
v1.61. `&amp;`, `&copy;`, `&#169;`, and `&#x1F600;`
already decode on both. This cut is the numeric
reference whose digit run is longer than CommonMark
allows.

CommonMark accepts 1–7 decimal digits and 1–6 hex digits.
micromark leaves a longer run as the characters.
cmark-gfm consumes it: `&#87654321;` became U+FFFD, and
`&#x0001F600;` (seven hex digits) became an emoji. A
completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` stashes that
source before either parser, so both show the characters.
Eight or more decimal digits (`&#87654321;`,
`&#12345678;`) and seven or more hex digits
(`&#x0001F600;`, `&#X0001F600;`, `&#x0000000;`,
`&#x1234567;`) stay literal. The same text inside
emphasis is still emphasis of those characters, so
`*&#87654321;*` is italic `&#87654321;` and not U+FFFD.
`&#x00000023; x` is not a heading.

A reference inside the digit limit is unchanged.
`&amp;` is &, `&copy;` and `&#169;` and `&#0000169;` are
©, and `&#x1F600;` is the emoji. `&#0;` and a
noncharacter such as `&#x10FFFF;` are still U+FFFD. An
unknown name `&foo;`, a reference with no semicolon
(`&#87654321`, `&amp`), and a bare `&` stay literal and
are not stashed. Code spans, fenced code, and indented
code are not stashed. A reference inside a raw HTML tag
or a link destination is stashed with the rest, so
`<span title="&#87654321;">` and `[a](/q?&#87654321;)`
show those characters. While a LEFT reply is still
streaming, the bubble stays plain text and this pass is
a no-op. Copy on the message still copies markdown source.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row. User-right text, tool
lines, and cards stay as they are.

### Prior slices

v1.44 setext headings stand, and they already match
micromark and cmark-gfm. v1.40 backslash escapes and
in-range character references stand. v1.61 character
references inside raw HTML stand. Streaming LEFT bubbles
stay plain text. Copy on the message still copies markdown
source.

Out of scope: VNC / full Box GUI, extra channel types,
public store, coding-agent, new surfaces, a new HTTP
route, bumping OpenAPI past 0.18.0, a second PR.
`workspaceTree.ts` (never reintroduce `computerPane.ts`)
stands.

OpenAPI **0.18.0** (not bumped).

## 170. v1.70 — GFM strikethrough tilde runs in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Extended autolinks were checked on main before this cut.
Bare `http(s)://` (v0.82), `www.` (v1.34), angle autolinks
(v1.43), email (v1.45), and trailing punctuation (v1.62)
already match in `linkify.ts` and `WebLinks.swift`. They
already skip code and link text. This cut does not reopen
them.

v1.33 strikes `~~text~~`. v1.50 strikes `~text~` by
rewriting it to `~~text~~`. Desktop react-markdown has no
remark-gfm, so `~~` was an `indexOf` split: a longer run
was treated as a pair plus extras. cmark-gfm (iOS full
markdown) only arms a tilde run whose length is exactly 1
or exactly 2, and the opener and closer lengths must be
equal. A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now uses
that rule on both clients.

A delimiter is a run of exactly two tildes. `x ~~~foo~~~ y`
and `~~foo~~~` stay literal. `~~a~~~b~~` strikes `a~~~b`.
`~~a~~~~b~~` and `~~a~~~~~b~~` each strike once. The opener
is not followed by whitespace and the closer is not
preceded by it, so `~~ foo ~~`, `~~foo ~~`, `~~ foo~~`, and
`a ~~ b ~~ c` stay literal. `~~foo bar~~`, `~~foo~~`, and
`foo~~bar~~baz` still strike. `~~foo.~~` strikes `foo.`.
A soft newline may sit inside the pair, so `~~foo` then
`bar~~` strikes. A closer that is itself after a newline
(`~~foo` then `~~`) stays literal.

A hard break still ends the pair (v1.41). `~~foo` then two
spaces then `bar~~` stays `~~foo` / `bar~~` with a break
between, even though raw cmark-gfm would strike across that
break. Do not reopen that.

A single tilde that v1.50 did not promote is stashed
(`§§SBE00007E§§`) before the parser. cmark-gfm would strike
`~foo~~bar~` as one pair of length 1. Both clients keep
that text literal, which v1.50 already required. `~text~`
still promotes to `~~text~~` and strikes. `~~foo~bar~~`
strikes `foo~bar`. `~foo` then a newline then `bar~` stays
literal. A tilde in a URL, a link destination, or code is
not stashed. A run of three or more stays the characters,
so a tilde fence is still a fence.

A `~~` run inside an http(s), `www.`, or email autolink is
not a delimiter. `https://example.com/~~foo~~` stays one
address with the trailing `~~` outside the link.
`~~https://example.com~~` still strikes the address.
Inline code, fenced code, and indented code stay literal.
`~~**bold**~~` and `**~~x~~**` still nest.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays
plain text and this pass is a no-op. Copy on the message
still copies markdown source.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row. User-right text, tool
lines, and cards stay as they are.

### Prior slices

v1.33 `~~text~~` stands. v1.50 `~text~` stands, including
`~foo~~bar~` literal and no single-tilde pair across a
newline. v1.41 hard breaks stand, and a hard break still
ends a strikethrough pair. v0.82 / v1.34 / v1.43 / v1.45 /
v1.62 autolinks stand. Streaming LEFT bubbles stay plain
text. Copy on the message still copies markdown source.

Out of scope: VNC / full Box GUI, extra channel types,
public store, coding-agent, new surfaces, a new HTTP
route, bumping OpenAPI past 0.18.0, a second PR.
`workspaceTree.ts` (never reintroduce `computerPane.ts`)
stands.

OpenAPI **0.18.0** (not bumped).

## 171. v1.71 — GFM http(s) autolink path characters in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

GFM task-list items were checked on main before this cut.
They already shipped. v1.32 renders `- [ ]`, `- [x]`, and
`* [X]` as read-only checkbox glyphs on desktop and iOS.
v1.49 keeps the indented continuation in the item. v1.59
does the same for ordered lists (`1. [ ]`, `1. [x]`), and
a task inside a blockquote uses the same glyphs. A `[ ]`
mid-line or outside a list stays literal. Nested task
items already work. Those slices are not reopened.

`www.` autolinks (v1.34) already keep an apostrophe, a
quote, a backtick, or a `>` inside the path, then peel a
trailing `'` or `"` with the v1.62 delimiter. Bare
`http://` and `https://` (v0.82) stopped at those four
characters, so `https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O'Brien`
linked only through `O` on both clients. cmark-gfm's
extended autolink does not. This cut is that scan.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` reads a bare
`http://` or `https://` until whitespace or `<`. An
interior `'`, `"`, `` ` ``, or `>` stays in the link.
`https://example.com/a"b"c`, `https://example.com/a>b`,
`https://example.com/?a=1>2`, and
`https://example.com/a`b`` are one link each when the
characters are still in the text. A trailing `'` or `"`
stays outside (`https://example.com/O'Brien'` links
`https://example.com/O'Brien`). `<` still ends the link.
A `>` that closes `<https://...>` stays outside, and a
later `https://` after that closer is still its own link.
`www.` is unchanged and already matched this.

v1.62 still peels a trailing `?`, `!`, `.`, `,`, `:`,
`*`, `_`, or `~`, an unbalanced `)`, and a trailing
`&hl;`. A trailing `]`, `}`, or curly quote on http(s)
still stays outside. `ftp://`, `javascript:`, `file:`,
and `data:` stay plain. `xmpp:` stays plain. A balanced
backtick pair is still a code span, so
`` see https://example.com/a`b` now `` links
`https://example.com/a` and shows `b` as code. An
address inside a code span, a fence, or indented code
stays literal. While a LEFT reply is still streaming, a
URL that touches the end of the buffer stays plain.
Copy on the message still copies markdown source.

iOS percent-encodes `'`, `"`, `` ` ``, and `>` in the
opened URL when Foundation would otherwise reject the
string. The visible text stays the characters that were
written. Desktop opens that same visible text.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row. User-right text, tool
lines, and cards stay as they are.

http(s) links stand. v1.34 `www.` autolinks stand.
v1.43 angle-bracket autolinks stand. v1.45 email
autolinks stand. v1.62 trailing punctuation stands,
including the legacy `]`, `}`, and curly-quote peel on
http(s). Streaming LEFT bubbles stay plain text. Copy on

## 172. v1.72 — CommonMark tab stops for indented code inside list items in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Lazy blockquote continuation was checked on main before
this cut. It already shipped in v1.47. A paragraph line
inside `> ...` that is followed by a line with no leading
`>` stays in that quote. A blank line, a thematic break,
a fenced code opener, a list marker, or a heading ends
it. Desktop and iOS already agree, and that agrees with
cmark-gfm. It is not reopened.

A backtick fence whose info string contains a backtick,
and a tilde fence closed by a longer run of tildes, were
checked too. v1.52 already refuses a line of three
backticks whose info string contains a backtick, and both
clients close a fence when the closing run is at least as
long as the opener. Those rules already match. They are
not reopened.

Indented code at the margin already shipped in v1.48, and
list marker padding already shipped in v1.54. The gap is
the tab stop inside a list item. Desktop react-markdown
already matches cmark-gfm. iOS strips the marker, then
measures a remaining tab from column 0. A bullet, two
tabs, and `foo` became code `foo`. cmark-gfm's code text
is two spaces then `foo`, because the second tab still
starts at column 4.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` keeps that
stop. The code text is what cmark-gfm would keep, and the
item stores it after four spaces so the indented-code
pass can read it from column 0. A bullet and two tabs
then `foo` is code whose text is two spaces then `foo`.
A bullet and three tabs then `foo` is two spaces, a tab,
and `foo`: the tab that crosses the four-column code
indent becomes the leftover spaces, and a later tab stays
a tab. `1.` and two tabs then `foo` is code ` foo`.
`10.` and two tabs then `foo` is code `foo`. `- foo`, a
blank line, then two tabs and `bar`, a tab, and `baz` is
code `  bar`, a tab, and `baz`. A bullet, four spaces,
and a tab then `foo` is code `  foo`. `*`, `+`, and `9)`
follow the same rule. A line of only spaces is unchanged:
`- foo`, a blank line, then six spaces and `bar` is still
code `bar`.

An open paragraph is not code. `- foo` then two tabs then
`bar`, with no blank line, stays the paragraph `foo` /
`bar`. A bullet, a tab, and `follow`, then a tab and
`more`, stays `follow` / `more`. A fence indented fewer
than four columns past the item stays a fence. Two tabs
in front of a fence are indented code, not a fence, so
the fence characters are the code text. A nested `-` that
is only one tab in stays a nested list.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays
plain text and this pass is a no-op. Copy on the message
still copies markdown source. Copy on the code block
still copies the code text.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row. User-right text, tool
lines, and cards stay as they are.

### Prior slices

v1.48 indented code blocks stand. v1.54 list marker
padding stands. v1.47 lazy blockquote continuation
stands. v1.52 multi-backtick code spans stand, including
a backtick in a fence info string and a closing fence
that is longer than the opener. v1.53 indented fences
stand. Streaming LEFT bubbles stay plain text. Copy on
the message still copies markdown source.

Out of scope: a blockquote's own tab stop, which is the
quote marker and not a list item. VNC / full Box GUI,

## 173. v1.73 — CommonMark tab stops for blockquotes in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Hard line breaks were checked on main before this cut.
They already shipped in v1.41. A backslash at the end of
a line, or two or more trailing spaces, is a hard break
inside a paragraph, and that break does not apply at the
end of the paragraph. Ordered-list start numbers already
shipped in v1.36. The rule that only a list starting at
1 may interrupt a paragraph already shipped in v1.56.
Those cuts already match cmark-gfm. They are not reopened.

v1.72 matched tab stops for indented code inside a list
item and left this case out. A blockquote's own tab stop
is the quote marker. The optional column after `>` can
eat part of a tab. The leftover columns decide indented
code. Desktop and iOS both measured that tab from column
0 after stripping `>`, so `>\t\tfoo` became code `\tfoo`.
cmark-gfm's code text is two spaces then `foo`.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` keeps that
stop. `>\t\tfoo` is a blockquote whose code text is two
spaces then `foo`. `>\t\t\tfoo` is two spaces, a tab, and
`foo`: the tab that crosses the code indent becomes the
leftover spaces, and a later tab stays a tab. `>\tfoo`
and `> \tfoo` are the paragraph `foo`. A tab before `>`
is indented code, not a quote. A nested marker keeps its
own stop, so `>>\t\tfoo` is a nested quote whose code
text is one space then `foo`.

A list inside the quote uses the same columns. The list's
content column is counted after the quote marker, not
from column 0 of the whole line. `>-\t\tfoo` is a list
item whose code text is one space then `foo`. `>1. foo`,
a blank quote line, then `>\t\tbar` stays the paragraph
`bar`, because those two tabs are not four columns past
the item. A second blank line does not end a filled
item. An empty item ends on a blank line, and the next
line belongs to the quote. An open paragraph is not
code. `> foo` then `>\t\tbar` is the paragraph `foo` /
`bar`. A fence stays a fence. `>\t``` ` then a tabbed
line then the closer is a fence whose text keeps the
tab that belongs to the code, not an indented code block
of the fence characters.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays
plain text and this pass is a no-op. Copy on the message
still copies markdown source. Copy on the code block
still copies the code text.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new

An inline link is not replaced by a shortcut that uses the
same label. A backslash escapes ASCII punctuation only, so

loads. The fetched URL is the percent-encoded one. A bare
`<https://example.com/a b>` is not a link. Code spans,
fences, indented code, and math stay literal. Find still
searches the resolved markdown, which keeps the space.
The render pass percent-encodes it so the link opens.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays
plain text and this pass is a no-op. Copy on the message
still copies markdown source.

## 175. v1.75 — CommonMark safe inline HTML in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Raw HTML as literal text already shipped in v1.60, and
character references inside that literal HTML already
shipped in v1.61. Type 1–6 HTML blocks already end a list
or a quote (v1.58). Those cuts made desktop and iOS agree
on showing the characters. cmark-gfm still passes a real
inline tag through to the HTML renderer. The gap is that
pass, done with a safe allowlist so a reply cannot run a
script or an event handler.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` renders four
bare inline tags and leaves every other angle bracket as
text. A tag is bare when it has no attributes: optional
whitespace, and for a void tag one optional slash.

- `<br>`, `<br/>`, `<br />`, `<BR>`, and `<br >` are a line
  break. The same hard-break placeholder v1.41 already
  paints is reused, including inside `<kbd>`.
- `<kbd>…</kbd>`, `<sub>…</sub>`, and `<sup>…</sup>` render
  when the closer matches the current opener. Case does
  not matter (`<KBD>`, `</Sup>`). Markdown between the
  tags still parses, so `<kbd>**Ctrl**</kbd>` is a key
  whose label is bold. `<kbd/>` is an empty key.
- An opener with no closer, or a closer that is not the
  current opener, stays the original characters.
  `<sup><kbd>b</sup></kbd>` renders the key and shows the
  crossed `sup` tags as text.

Anything else stays the characters, on both clients:
`<kbd onclick="alert(1)">`, `<kbd title="x">`, `<span>`,
`<script>`, `<!-- x -->`, `a <b c`, `<3`, `<not a tag>`,
and `1 < 2 > 0`. A comment does not emphasize the text
inside it. A script is not an element and has no handler.
Attributes are never copied onto a rendered tag.

Code spans, fenced code, indented code, and math stay
literal, so `` `<br>` `` and a `\( <br> \)` do not break
the line. Angle autolinks and link destinations stay
links; `[<kbd>Enter</kbd>](https://example.com)` is a link
whose label is a key. A type 1–6 HTML block that starts a
line is still left raw so a list or quote ends there.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the bubble stays
plain text and this pass is a no-op. Copy on the message
still copies markdown source.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row. User-right text, tool
lines, and cards stay as they are.

### Prior slices

is not the allowlist above. v1.61 character references
inside that literal HTML stand. v1.58 HTML blocks stand.
v1.41 hard line breaks stand. Streaming LEFT bubbles stay
plain text. Copy on the message still copies markdown
source.

Out of scope: rendering every CommonMark raw HTML tag,
executing scripts or handlers, a second sanitizer
library. VNC / full Box GUI, extra channel types, public

## 176. v1.76 — CommonMark link text in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Reference links already shipped in v1.39, reference images
in v1.52, and link titles in v1.46. Inline HTML as literal
text shipped in v1.60 and v1.61, and safe bare tags in
v1.75. Spaced angle-bracket destinations shipped in v1.74.
Those were already on main. Checked again before this cut:
react-markdown already renders a finished inline link the
way cmark-gfm does, including `[link [foo [bar]]](/uri)`,
an escaped `\[`, and `[foo [bar](/u)](/v)`. The gap is the
reference rewriter both clients run before that parser.
It treated every balanced `[` … `]` as one label, so a
nested link was swallowed. `[foo [bar](/uri)][ref]` became
one link, and `[foo][bar]` fell back to the `foo` shortcut
when `bar` was not defined. cmark-gfm does neither.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now walks
brackets the way cmark does. The nearest `[` is the only
candidate for a `]`. A matched link, not an image, sets
`noLinkOpeners`, so an outer bracket cannot contain that
link. Balanced brackets that are not themselves links stay
in the text.

- `[link [foo [bar]]](/uri)` and
  `[link [foo [bar]]][ref]` are one link whose text is
  `link [foo [bar]]`.
- `[link [bar](/uri)` is literal `[link ` plus the link
  `bar`. `[link] bar](/uri)` is not a link.
- `[link \[bar](/uri)` is one link whose text is `link [bar`.
- `[foo [bar](/u)](/v)` is literal `[foo `, the link `bar`,
  and literal `](/v)`. `[foo [bar](/uri)][ref]` is that
  same shape plus the reference `ref`.
- `[foo *bar [baz][ref]*][ref]` is literal `[foo `,
  emphasis around the link `baz`, literal `]`, and the
  reference `ref`.
- `[foo][bar]` stays literal when `bar` is not defined,
  even if `foo` is. `[foo][bar][baz]` with only `baz`
  defined is literal `[foo` plus the link `bar`.
- `[foo]()` is an empty inline link and does not take the
  shortcut. `[foo](not a link)` still does.
- `` [foo`]`(/uri) `` and `` [foo`][ref]` `` are not links.
  The `]` is inside the code span. `[link `code`][ref]`
  is a link whose text contains the code span.
- An autolink binds tighter, so
  `[foo<https://example.com/?search=](uri)>` is the
  autolink, not a link to `uri`. A `]` inside a raw HTML
  tag does not close a link, so
  `[foo <bar attr="][ref]">` does not resolve `ref`.
- An image may contain a link:
  `![foo [bar][ref]][img]` is one image whose alt is
  `foo bar`. A link may contain an image:
  `[![moon][img]][ref]` is a link around that image.

Code spans, fenced code, indented code, and math stay
literal. Task markers stay markers. Footnotes stay
footnotes. While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the
bubble stays plain text and this pass is a no-op. Copy
on the message still copies markdown source.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row. User-right text, tool
lines, and cards stay as they are.

### Prior slices

v1.39 reference links stand. v1.42 inline images stand.
v1.46 link titles stand. v1.52 reference images stand.
v1.74 spaced angle-bracket destinations stand. v1.75
safe inline HTML stands. Streaming LEFT bubbles stay
plain text. Copy on the message still copies markdown
source.

Out of scope: flattening image alt text that the
platform parser already flattens (`![foo *bar*](/u)`),
VNC / full Box GUI, extra channel types, public store,
coding-agent, new surfaces, a new HTTP route, bumping
OpenAPI past 0.18.0, a second PR.
`workspaceTree.ts` (never reintroduce `computerPane.ts`)
stands.

## 177. v1.77 — CommonMark image alt text in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked v1.76 on main before this cut. That slice is link
text. Its out-of-scope line named image-alt flattening
because the desktop parser already turns
`![foo *bar*](/u)` into alt `foo bar`. Confirmed again:
micromark flattens emphasis, strong, code spans, nested
links and images, entities, and backslash escapes. It does
not match cmark-gfm's plain alt. A soft break stays a
newline, a hard break is dropped, a newline inside a code
span stays a newline, and `~~` stays in the alt because
strikethrough runs after the alt has collapsed to a
string. iOS was further away. `MarkdownImage.split`
decoded entities and escapes only, so the accessibility
label and the failure fallback kept the asterisks,
backticks, and nested link syntax.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now parses the
image description as inlines and uses that plain text for
the alt, the accessibility label, and the fallback when
the image fails to load. cmark-gfm's HTML writer, in plain
mode, emits one space for both a soft break and a hard
break. This cut follows that.

- `![foo *bar*](/u)` and `![foo **bar**](/u)` have alt
  `foo bar`. Unmatched marks stay: `foo * bar` stays
  `foo * bar`, and `foo ~bar~` stays `foo ~bar~`.
- `![foo ![bar](/b)](/u)` and `![foo [bar](/u)](/v)` have
  alt `foo bar`. A nested link or image contributes its
  text, not its URL. `foo [bar]` stays `foo [bar]`.
  `foo [bar [baz](/u)](/v)` is `foo [bar baz](/v)`.
- A description `a`, a code span of `code`, and `b` has
  alt `a code b`. A newline inside the span is a space.
  One leading and trailing space come off unless the span
  is all spaces. Entities and escapes inside the span stay
  literal, so a code span of `b&amp;c` stays `b&amp;c`.
- `![foo ~~bar~~ baz](/u)` is `foo bar baz`. A run of
  exactly two tildes is strikethrough. A longer run stays
  the characters. A single tilde is not strikethrough.
- `![foo\nbar](/u)`, `![foo  \nbar](/u)`, and
  `![foo\\\nbar](/u)` are `foo bar`. One trailing space
  before a newline stays, then the break adds another
  space.
- `&copy;`, `&#35;`, and `&#x22;` decode. `&amp;amp;`
  becomes `&amp;`. An unknown name stays literal. A
  numeric reference with more digits than CommonMark
  allows stays the source characters (v1.69). A backslash
  before ASCII punctuation becomes that character.
- An autolink contributes its inner text.
  `<https://example.com/a>` does not keep the brackets.
  A raw HTML tag is copied as text, so `<bar>` stays
  `<bar>`.
- Only an image that will load is rewritten. `javascript:`,
  `data:`, `file:`, and the other literal schemes stay the
  original characters. The rewritten alt is one stash
  token, so a later escape, math, or tilde pass cannot
  treat the flattened text as syntax. An empty alt stays
  empty, and the fallback is the URL.

Code spans, fenced code, indented code, and math stay
literal. Reference links are already resolved before this
pass, so `![foo *bar*][img]` flattens after it is an
inline image. While a LEFT reply is still streaming, this
pass is a no-op and the bubble stays plain text. Copy on
the message still copies markdown source.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row. User-right text, tool
lines, and cards stay as they are.

v1.42 inline images stand. v1.52 reference images stand.
v1.69 over-long numeric references stand. v1.70
strikethrough stands. v1.76 link text stands. Streaming
LEFT bubbles stay plain text. Copy on the message still
copies markdown source.

## 178. v1.78 — CommonMark link title forms in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /

Checked v1.46 and v1.77 on main before this cut. v1.46
already shows a one-line `"title"`, `'title'`, and
`(title)` as the link tooltip. v1.77 named link-title
quote forms as out of scope. The scanners still disagreed
with cmark-gfm. A reference title could not contain a line
break, so a title that spans lines failed and a definition
whose title text wraps was not a definition. The next-line
continuation stopped after three spaces. An unescaped `(`
inside a parenthesis title was accepted. A title with no
separating whitespace was accepted by the link and image
scanners. A blank line inside a title was accepted. A
backslash before a non-punctuation character was dropped.
iOS reference rewriting called `emitLink` without defining
it, so a definition title never became the inline title.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now parses link
and image titles the way cmark-gfm does. The title is the
tooltip and the accessibility hint where the platform has
one. Whitespace collapses to a single space. An empty
result has no tooltip.

- `[a](/u "title")`, `[a](/u 'title')`, and
  `[a](/u (title))` are the same link. The visible text is
  `a`. The tooltip is `title`. Images use the same three
  forms, and the tooltip is the image title.
- A title may span a line break. `[a](/u "ti` then `tle")`
  has tooltip `ti tle`. A blank line inside the title is
  not a link and not an image.
- `\"` inside a double-quoted title is a quote.
  `foo\baz` keeps the backslash, because `b` is not ASCII
  punctuation. `&#35;`, `&#x22;`, and `&amp;` decode.
  `&amp;amp;` becomes `&amp;`. An unknown name stays
  literal.
- A reference definition may put the title on the next
  line, in double quotes, single quotes, or parentheses,
  including after more than three spaces. `[a]: /u` then
  `"title"` resolves `[a]` with tooltip `title`. A title
  that itself spans a line collapses the same way. A blank
  line, or text after the title, is not a definition.
- An invalid title is not a link or an image.
  `[a](/u "title "and" title")`, `[a](/u (foo(bar)))`,
  and a blank line inside the title stay literal text.
  The opening bracket is escaped before the platform
  parser so a looser title scan cannot accept them.
  `[a](/u"title")` has no separating whitespace, so the
  quotes are part of the destination and the link has no
  title. If the label is a defined shortcut, that shortcut
  still applies and the failed `(...)` stays beside it.
- The rewritten title is one double-quoted line so desktop
  and iOS agree. `&` is written as `&#38;` so a second
  entity pass cannot turn `&amp;` into `&`.

Code spans, fenced code, indented code, and math stay
literal. While a LEFT reply is still streaming, this pass
is a no-op and the bubble stays plain text. Copy on the
message still copies markdown source.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row. User-right text, tool
lines, and cards stay as they are.

destinations stand. v1.76 link text stands. v1.77 image
alt text stands. Streaming LEFT bubbles stay plain text.
Copy on the message still copies markdown source.

## 179. v1.79 — CommonMark link and image destination entities in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked v1.78 on main before this cut. v1.78 already
rewrites link and image titles, including a line break, a
reference title on the next line, escaped punctuation, and
character references inside the title. It left destination
character references and backslash escapes out of scope.
The escape pass also ran on the destination first, with a
small named set, so `&amp;` and `&copy;` in an href did not
percent-encode the way cmark-gfm does. micromark already
resolved many of those references when the escape pass did
not see them. v1.69 still keeps an over-long numeric
reference as the source characters. v1.74 still
percent-encodes spaces inside an angle destination.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now cleans a
link or image destination the way cmark-gfm does, for an
inline destination and for a link reference definition.
Trim space, tab, LF, and CR. Decode HTML character
references (the full HTML5 named set, and numeric
references). Then drop a backslash only before ASCII
punctuation. Percent-encode the href. `&` is written back
as `&#38;` and `'` as `&#x27;`, and the escape pass skips
that destination, so the parser decodes them once.

- `[a](/f&ouml;&ouml;)` has href `/f%C3%B6%C3%B6`.
  `[a](/u&amp;v)` has href `/u&amp;v`. `[a](foo\)\:)` has
  href `foo):`. `&#35;` and `&#x26;` decode. An unknown
  name such as `&notanentity;` stays literal.
- `&#11;` is `%0B`. `&#xFFFE;` is `%FE` and `&#xFFFF;` is
  `%FF`. A null, a surrogate, or a value past U+10FFFF is
  `%EF%BF%BD`.
- Eight or more decimal digits, or seven or more hex
  digits, stay the source characters. `&#87654321;` does
  not become U+FFFD, and `&#x0001F600;` does not become an
  emoji. That is the v1.69 rule already on main.
- An angle destination trims before it encodes.
  `[a](< foo >)` is `foo`. A bare destination that would
  not survive the parentheses, such as `foo):`, is wrapped
  in angle brackets. Balanced parentheses stay bare.
- A reference definition uses the same destination.
  `[a]: /f&ouml;&ouml;` resolves `[a]` to
  `/f%C3%B6%C3%B6`. An image destination does too. A title
  on the same link still follows v1.78.
- A backslash before a non-punctuation character stays a
  backslash (`/url\bar\*baz` is `/url%5Cbar*baz`). The
  reference reader no longer drops that backslash.

Code spans, fenced code, indented code, and math stay
literal. An invalid title is not a link, so its
destination is not rewritten. A footnote label is not a
link reference definition. While a LEFT reply is still
streaming, this pass is a no-op and the bubble stays plain
text. Copy on the message still copies markdown source.
Ordinary text outside a destination still uses the smaller
named set from v1.40.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row. User-right text, tool
lines, and cards stay as they are. `javascript:` is still
dropped by the existing safe-link check, not by this
encoder.

## 180. v1.80 — CommonMark setext heading edge cases in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked v1.44 and v1.79 on main before this cut. v1.44
already renders a paragraph followed by `=` as an H1 and
by `-` as an H2, including a multi-line paragraph, and it
keeps a lazy quote or list underline literal. v1.79 left
the remaining setext edges out of scope. A solid `---`
with no paragraph above it was stored as paragraph text,
so `---` then `Foo` then `---` became a heading whose
text started with the rule. An HTML block of any `<`
plus a letter ran until a blank line, so `<a title="a lot`
then `---` was not a heading, and a comment or `<script>`
swallowed a heading that followed the closer. Indented
code was recognized only after a blank line, so `# Heading`
then a four-space line was heading text.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now matches
cmark-gfm on those edges. Desktop and iOS share the scan.

- `Foo` then `Bar` then `---` is one h2. `Foo *bar` then
  `baz*` then `====` is one h1, and the line break stays
  inside the heading.
- An underline may have up to three leading spaces and
  any trailing spaces or tabs. `Foo` then `   ----` is an
  h2. Four or more columns are not an underline, so `Foo`
  then four spaces and `---` stays a paragraph. A leading
  tab is four columns.
- `---` after an open paragraph is an h2, not a thematic
  break. The same line with no paragraph above it is a
  thematic break. `---` then `---` is two breaks. A line
  of `=` with no paragraph above it stays the characters.
- `= =` and `--- -` are not underlines. `---` with spaces
  between the marks stays a thematic break.
- An underline cannot be a lazy continuation. `> foo`
  then `bar` then `===`, and `- Foo` then `===`, stay
  the characters. `---` at the left margin after a list
  item stays a break. The same underline indented to the
  item's text stays a heading inside the item.
- `<a title="a lot` then `---` is an h2, because that
  line is not an HTML block. `<div>`, `<script>`, and
  `<!--` keep a following underline inside the block.
  A heading after `</script>` or `-->` is still a heading.
  A type 7 tag such as `<br/>` does not interrupt a
  paragraph, so `Foo` then `<br/>` then `---` is one h2.
- Indented code may follow a heading with no blank line.
  `# Heading` then four spaces of `foo` then `Heading`
  then `------` is a heading, a code block, and an h2.
  The four-space `foo` is not heading text.
- A link reference definition is still removed by the
  v1.39 pass before this scan. `[foo]: /url` then `===`
  leaves the characters `===`. `[foo]: /url` then `bar`
  then `===` is an h1 whose text is `bar`.

Code spans, fenced code, indented code, and math stay
literal. While a LEFT reply is still streaming, this pass
is a no-op and the bubble stays plain text. Copy on the
message still copies markdown source.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row. User-right text, tool

  A definition inside a fence or a `<div>` block stays
  the characters.
- Labels use the Unicode case fold. `ẞ`, `ß`, and `SS`
  match. `ς` matches `Σ`. `ﬁ` matches `fi`.
- A label may contain 1000 characters, which is the
  length cmark-gfm accepts. 1001 stays literal.

## 182. v1.82 — CommonMark thematic break edge cases in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked the CommonMark thematic-break examples on main before
this cut. `***`, `---`, and `___` already render as rules,
including spaced forms (`- - -`, `**  * ** * ** * **`,
`-     -      -`) and tabs between the marks. Up to three
leading spaces are a break. Four spaces are an indented code
block, or a paragraph continuation when a paragraph is already
open. Fewer than three marks, mixed characters (`*-*`), and
`+++` / `===` are not breaks. A break interrupts a paragraph
with no blank line. `---` directly under paragraph text is a
setext h2. When a line could be a list item or a break
(`* * *`), the break wins, and `- foo` then `***` then `- bar`
is two lists. v1.81 had left the remaining edges out of scope.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now matches
cmark-gfm on those edges. Desktop and iOS share the scan.

- A break indented to the list item's text stays inside the
  item. `- foo` then `  ***` then `  bar`, the same with
  `* * *` or `- - -`, and a loose item with a blank line
  before the break are one list whose item contains the rule.
  `- Foo` then `- * * *` is one list whose second item is the
  rule. The list renderer paints that rule; this pass does
  not pull it out as a top-level rule.
- A break in the first three columns with less indent than
  the item's text still ends the list. `- foo` then `***`
  then `- bar` is a list, a rule, and a second list.
- A line inside an HTML block is not a rule. `<div>`,
  `<script>`, and `<!--` keep a following `***`, `---`, or
  `* * *` in the block. A type 7 tag such as `<br>` at the
  start of a block does the same until a blank line. `<br>`
  that continues an open paragraph does not, so `foo` then
  `<br>` then `***` is a paragraph and a rule.
- `---` under an open paragraph stays an h2. A solid dash
  underline indented to the item's text stays a heading
  inside the item.

Code spans, fenced code, indented code, and math stay
literal. While a LEFT reply is still streaming, this pass
is a no-op and the bubble stays plain text. Copy on the
message still copies markdown source.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row. User-right text, tool
lines, and cards stay as they are.

Checked the CommonMark emphasis examples on main before this
cut. Delimiter-run flanking, intraword `_` versus `*`, the
rule of 3, nested and overlapping runs, unmatched delimiters,
`***strong emph***`, and emphasis inside code spans and links
already match cmark-gfm on both clients. v1.67 and v1.68
stand. The hard-break examples, and a setext heading that
stays inside a list item, already match via v1.41. v1.82 had
left hard line breaks beyond v1.41 out of scope.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now matches
cmark-gfm when the hard break is inside a setext heading.
Desktop and iOS share the scan. The heading text used to
drop every trailing space and then turn every remaining
newline into a hard break.

- A plain newline is a soft break. `Foo` then `bar` then
  `===` is one heading whose lines wrap. It is not a `<br>`.
- Two or more trailing spaces, or one backslash before the
  newline, is a hard break. The spaces and that backslash
  are not shown. An even run of backslashes is a literal
  backslash and a soft break. One leftover backslash after
  those pairs is the break.
- The line before the underline is the end of the block.
  Trailing spaces there disappear. A backslash there stays
  visible. Neither is a break.
- A marker inside a code span stays in the code. The newline
  becomes a space. Two spaces before it stay. A backslash
  stays a backslash.
- Leading spaces and tabs on a heading line are not part of
  the text, including a continuation line inside a code span.
  A trailing run of tabs, or a single trailing space, is not
  a break and is dropped. A tab between words stays.
- The same rules apply when the heading is the body of a
  quote. A heading indented into a list item already went
  through the paragraph hard-break pass and still does.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the setext split is a
no-op and the bubble stays plain text. Copy on the message
still copies markdown source.

## 184. v1.84 — GFM strikethrough flanking in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked cmark-gfm strikethrough (extension, single and double
tilde, no double-tilde-only option) on main before this cut.
v1.50 and v1.70 already struck `~a~` and `~~a~~`, left a run
of three or more literal, left `~~a~` and `~a~~` literal,
left a whitespace-flanked pair literal, struck an intraword
pair, let a code span win, and let `~~` cross a soft newline.
Those cases already matched. The rewrite that turned `~text~`
into `~~text~~` and stashed every other single tilde did not.
It changed the characters flanking looks at and hid matches
the extension does make.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now matches that
extension on both clients. Desktop `remarkStrikethrough`
applies the rule. iOS full markdown already did, once the
source tildes are left alone. `promote` stays in the pipeline
and returns the text unchanged.

- A delimiter is a run of one tilde or two that is
  left-flanking, right-flanking, or both. The opener and the
  closer must be the same length. A run of three or more is
  not a delimiter, so `x ~~~foo~~~ y` stays literal and
  `~~a~~~b~~` strikes `a~~~b`.
- `~~a~` and `~a~~` stay literal. `~foo~~bar~` strikes
  `foo~~bar`. `~a~~ b~` stays literal. This supersedes the
  v1.70 lock that kept `~foo~~bar~` literal.
- Punctuation uses the cmark set: ASCII punctuation, including
  `$` `+` `<` `=` `>` `|` `^`, or a Unicode punctuation
  category. `a~~.b~~`, `~~b.~~c`, `a~~!~~`, `a~~$~~b`,
  `a~~…~~b`, and `~~ a~~` stay literal. `~~!~~`, `~~.b~~`,
  `~~b.~~`, `~~$~~`, and `(~~b~~)` strike. A symbol that is
  not punctuation still strikes, so `a~~£~~b` strikes.
- A run that can open but not close does not close an earlier
  opener. `~~foo ~~bar~~` strikes `bar` only. `This ~is ~
  legit~` strikes `is ~ legit` and leaves the later tildes.
- A soft newline may sit inside a pair of either length.
  `~foo` then a newline then `bar~` strikes. This supersedes
  the v1.50 lock that refused to cross a newline.
- A hard break still ends the pair on desktop (v1.41), even
  though raw cmark-gfm would strike across `~~foo` then two
  spaces then a newline. The spaces and the backslash form
  stay visible beside the tildes.
- Inline code and fenced code are left alone. A pair may wrap
  emphasis, strong, or a link that the parser already closed
  (`~~**bold**~~`, `*~~foo~~*`, `~~[a](http://b)~~`). A
  punctuation flank still wins (`a~~*b*~~c`,
  `~~[a](http://b/c)~~d`). A tilde run inside an http(s),
  `www.`, or email autolink is not a delimiter.
- A backslash-escaped tilde is that character, not a
  delimiter. `\~~not~~` and `\~not~` stay literal.
  `~~foo\~bar~~` strikes `foo~bar`.

Image alt text is unchanged. v1.77 strikes only a run of
exactly two tildes there, so `foo ~bar~` in an alt stays
literal.

Overlapping `*` and `~` (`~~*a~~b*~~`, `a*~~b~~*c`,
`a*~b~*c`) stay on micromark's emphasis pass. cmark-gfm
pairs both marks on one stack and skips `~` when it measures
`*`. iOS full markdown follows cmark. Matching that on
desktop would mean parsing emphasis again. Not this cut.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, the strikethrough
plugin is not installed and the bubble stays plain text.
Copy on the message still copies markdown source.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row. User-right text, tool
lines, and cards stay as they are.

### Prior slices

v1.33 strikethrough and v1.41 hard line breaks stand. v1.50
single-tilde strikethrough stands except where this section
names a superseding match. v1.70 tilde-run length stands the
same way. v1.68 symbol flanking stands. v1.77 image alt
stands. Streaming LEFT bubbles stay plain text. Copy on the
message still copies markdown source.

Out of scope: hard breaks beyond v1.41, emphasis changes,
image-alt single tildes, VNC / full Box GUI, extra channel
types, public store, coding-agent, new surfaces, a new HTTP
route, bumping OpenAPI past 0.18.0, a second PR.
`workspaceTree.ts` (never reintroduce `computerPane.ts`)
stands.

## 185. v1.85 — GFM extended autolink edge cases in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked the cmark-gfm autolink extension (0.29.0.gfm.13) on
main before this cut. These already matched on both clients
and stay as they are:

- `www.` autolinks, including trailing `?` `!` `.` `,` `:`
  `*` `_` `~` `'` `"`, balanced trailing `)`, a trailing
  letter entity such as `&hl;`, and a link that ends at `<`.
- Underscores in the last two segments of a `www.` name
  (`www.xxx.yyy._zzz`, `www.xxx._yyy.zzz` stay plain;
  `www._xxx.yyy.zzz` links). More than ten dots still links.
- Angle-bracket autolinks from v1.43, including an email
  whose domain ends in a digit (`<foo@bar.com2>`).
- Code spans, fenced code, and indented code stay literal.
  Text inside an existing link or image is not scanned
  again. A type 1–6 HTML block stays the characters and
  does not gain an autolink. A blockquote or list item
  still does.
- `ftp:` and any other non-http(s) scheme stay plain, as
  v0.82 locked. `xmpp:` and `notmailto:` stay plain, as
  v1.45 locked.

The unshipped gap was the bare `http(s)` domain scan, the
entity walk, and the last character of an extended email.
A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now matches
cmark-gfm on those edges. Desktop `splitWebUrls` and iOS
`WebLinks.split` share the scan.

- A bare `http://` or `https://` uses the same domain rule
  as `www.`. An underscore in either of the last two
  segments, when there are ten dots or fewer, is not a
  link. `http://ex_ample.com`, `https://example_foo.com`,
  `http://example.com_foo`, `http://good.example_foo.com`,
  and `http://exam_ple` stay plain. `https://foo_bar.example.com`
  and `https://a.b_c.d.com` link. An underscore in the path
  stays (`http://example.com/foo_bar`). More than ten dots
  still links (`http://a.b.c.d.e.f.g.h.i.j.k._l`). A host
  with no dot still links (`http://localhost`,
  `http://example`).
- ASCII letters glued to the scheme are part of the scheme,
  so `foohttp://example.com` stays plain. A digit or
  punctuation before it does not, so `1http://example.com`
  and `foo-http://example.com` link `http://example.com`.
- The first character after `://` must be a host character.
  `http://-example.com`, `http://.example.com`,
  `http://_foo.com`, `http://[::1]/`, and `http://$foo.com`
  stay plain. `http://example$foo.com` still links. A host
  character is not a space and not punctuation: ASCII
  punctuation, or a Unicode punctuation category. `-` is
  still allowed after that first character.
- A trailing `_` at the end of the buffer is peeled
  (`http://example.com_` links `http://example.com`). The
  same `_` before more text is in the last segment, so
  `http://example.com_ more` stays plain.
- A trailing entity-like run is letters only. `&hl;` and
  `&amp;` stay outside the link. `&hl1;`, `&9;`, and
  `&#38;` keep everything except the semicolon. This
  applies to `www.` and to `http(s)`.
- An extended email must end in an ASCII letter.
  `foo@bar.com2`, `foo@bar.b2`, and `a@b.c2` stay plain.
  `foo@bar.c2m`, `a@b2.com`, and `user@example.2com` link.
  A trailing `-` or `_` still keeps the whole address
  plain. This supersedes the v1.45 wording that treated
  every alphanumeric domain label as enough on its own.
- Only the exact prefix `mailto:` is part of the visible
  link. `MAILTO:foo@bar.baz` and `Mailto:foo@bar.baz` leave
  the prefix outside and link `foo@bar.baz`. Opening a
  destination that already says `MAILTO:` still works.
  `notmailto:` and `xmpp:` stay plain.

While a LEFT reply is still streaming, a URL or address
that still touches the end of the buffer stays plain.
Copy on the message still copies markdown source.

No animation, including when Reduce Motion is off. No new
shortcut, so the shortcuts list and the iOS Gestures &
shortcuts sheet do not gain a row. User-right text, tool
lines, and cards stay as they are.

### Prior slices

v0.82 bare http(s) links stand except where this section
names a domain the extension does not link. v1.34 `www.`
autolinks stand. v1.43 angle-bracket autolinks stand.
v1.45 email autolinks stand except the final-letter rule
and the exact `mailto:` prefix named above. v1.62 trailing
punctuation stands, with the entity walk now counting
letters only. v1.71 path characters stand. Streaming LEFT
bubbles stay plain text. Copy on the message still copies
markdown source.

Out of scope: linking `ftp:` or `xmpp:`, rewriting inline
HTML tokens, VNC / full Box GUI, extra channel types,
public store, coding-agent, new surfaces, a new HTTP
route, bumping OpenAPI past 0.18.0, a second PR.
`workspaceTree.ts` (never reintroduce `computerPane.ts`)
stands.

## 186. v1.86 — GFM table edge cases in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked cmark-gfm 0.29.0.gfm.13 (table extension) on main
before this cut. These already matched on both clients and
stay as they are:

- A delimiter row is a table only when its column count
  equals the header. `:---` is left, `:---:` is center,
  `---:` is right. A space inside the marker (`: ---`) is
  not a delimiter. One hyphen is enough. Leading and
  trailing pipes are optional. Zero to three leading spaces
  still start a table. Four spaces, or a leading tab that
  reaches column 4, stay indented code.
- A short body row is padded with empty cells. A long body
  row drops the extra cells. The clipboard source stays the
  original lines. An empty cell stays empty. A header and
  delimiter with no body is still a table.
- An escaped `\|` outside code stays one cell and renders
  as `|`. An escaped `\|` inside a code span becomes `|`
  in the chip and does not split the cell. A doubled
  backslash inside code keeps one backslash.
- A blank line ends the table. A block quote, a list
  (including an empty `-` or `1.`), an ATX heading, a
  thematic break, a fence, indented code, a raw HTML block
  of types 1–6, and a type 7 tag on its own line (`<span>`,
  `<br>`) end it. `<span>x</span>` stays a cell. A
  pipe-less body row is one cell, then padded. `foo` over
  `---` stays a setext heading.
- A table at column 0 interrupts a paragraph. A fully
  quoted table stays inside the blockquote. Inline
  emphasis, strong, code, and links in a cell still
  render. A block start at the beginning of a cell stays
  the characters.
- A link reference definition such as `[ref]: url` is not
  a block start. It stays a cell. Streaming stays plain
  text. Copy on the message still copies markdown source.

The unshipped gap was four cmark-gfm edges. A completed
assistant LEFT `kind=message` now matches them. Desktop
`splitMarkdownTables` and iOS `MarkdownTable.split` share
the rules.

- An unescaped `|` inside a code span splits the row, the
  same as a pipe outside code. `` | `a|b` | c | `` over a
  two-column delimiter is not a table. In a body row the
  cells are the split pieces, and cells past the header
  width are dropped. This supersedes the v1.55 sentence
  that said an unescaped pipe inside inline code does not
  split. An escaped `\|` inside code still does not split.
- A footnote definition (`[^1]: note`, labels matching the
  v1.38 set) ends the table. The definition is a footnote.
  The first definition wins. A later duplicate stays the
  characters, the same unmatched-definition rule as v1.38.
  This supersedes the v1.66 case that kept a pipe-less
  footnote definition inside the cell. A link reference
  definition still stays in the cell.
- A table indented into a list item stays inside that
  item. It is not hoisted out as a sibling of the list.
  On desktop, a list that contains a table is drawn as a
  real list so the table can render inside the item.
  Lists that do not contain a table stay on
  react-markdown. A tight list does not wrap the item
  text in a paragraph. A loose list does.
- A lazy continuation cannot open a table. `> | a | b |`
  then a delimiter with no `>` is one paragraph inside
  the quote. A quoted delimiter is a table, and a
  following line with no `>` sits outside the quote. A
  lazy header is allowed when the delimiter itself is
  quoted. The same rule applies inside a list item: an
  indented delimiter is a table, and a later unindented
  row ends the item.

### Prior slices

v1.31 pipe tables, v1.55 alignment and escaped pipes,
v1.64 inline cells, v1.65 clipping, and v1.66 pipe-less
rows stand except where this section names a superseding
match. v1.38 footnotes stand except the definition that
now ends a table. v1.32 quotes and v1.36 lists stand.
Streaming LEFT bubbles stay plain text. Copy on the
message still copies markdown source.

Out of scope: rendering arbitrary HTML in cells, task-list
indent changes, VNC / full Box GUI, extra channel types,
public store, coding-agent, new surfaces, a new HTTP
route, bumping OpenAPI past 0.18.0, a second PR.
`workspaceTree.ts` (never reintroduce `computerPane.ts`)
stands.

OpenAPI **0.18.0** (not bumped).

## 187. v1.87 — cmark-gfm HTML block edges in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked cmark-gfm 0.29.0.gfm.13 on main before this cut.
These already matched on both clients and stay as they are:

- Types 2–5 start and end: a comment through `-->`, a
  processing instruction through `?>`, a declaration
  (`<!` plus an ASCII uppercase letter) through `>`, and
  CDATA through `]]>`. A one-line comment, declaration,
  processing instruction, or CDATA ends before the next
  paragraph. Blank lines inside types 1–5 stay in the
  block. Blank lines end types 6 and 7 and are not part
  of the block.
- Block tags that were already in the set are
  case-insensitive. Zero to three leading spaces still
  start the block. Four spaces, or a tab at column 0,
  stay indented code. `<div/>` and `</div/>` are blocks.
  A type 6 tag such as `<div>` interrupts a paragraph, a
  list, and a lazy quote. A type 7 tag does not interrupt
  a plain paragraph, so `foo` / `<br>` / `*bar*` is one
  paragraph and `*bar*` is emphasis. `<span>x</span>`
  has trailing text, so it is not type 7.
- GFM tagfilter does not change what a reader sees. Raw
  HTML is escaped. The safe inline tags from v1.75
  (`br`, `kbd`, `sub`, `sup`) are not on the tagfilter
  list. A bare `<br>` still becomes a hard break. A
  paired `<kbd>`, `<sub>`, or `<sup>` still renders.
  Streaming stays plain text. Copy on the message still
  copies markdown source.

The unshipped gap was the block boundary, not a new HTML
renderer. A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now
matches cmark-gfm 0.29.0.gfm.13 on these edges. Desktop
`markdownList.ts` and iOS `MarkdownList.swift` share the
scanner.

- `source` is a type 6 block tag. It interrupts a
  paragraph, a list, and a quote. `search` is not a block
  tag in this cmark-gfm (CommonMark added it in 0.31).
  `foo` / `<search>` / `*bar*` stays one paragraph with
  emphasis. After a blank line, `<search>` is type 7 and
  `*bar*` is not emphasis. An unfinished `<search` is
  never a block. An unfinished `<source` is a block
  through the next blank line.
- A type 1 block (`script`, `pre`, `style`, `textarea`)
  ends on a line that contains any of `</script>`,
  `</pre>`, `</style>`, or `</textarea>`, case-insensitive,
  not only the matching close tag. `<script>` / `*a*` /
  `</pre>` / `*b*` emphasizes only `b`.
- A type 6 opener needs a space character, `>`, or `/>`
  after the name. `<div/>` is a block. `<div/` and
  `<div/foo>` are not. Type 7 is one complete open or
  close tag and trailing spaces or tabs only. A quoted
  attribute may contain `>`. `=` requires a value. A
  close tag cannot take attributes or a slash, so
  `</span bar>` and `</foo/>` are not type 7.
- A type 7 tag at the list or quote margin ends that
  container. `- foo` / `<br>` / `*bar*` is a list item
  `foo` and then an HTML block. The same line with the
  `>` omitted ends a blockquote. This supersedes the
  v1.58 rule that left a lazy `<br>` inside the item or
  the quote. An indented `<br>` stays in the item as
  inline HTML, and a hard break on a later indented line
  still fires. An indented `<div>` stays inside the item
  as a type 6 block.

### Prior slices

v1.58 HTML blocks stand except the margin type 7 case
this section supersedes. v1.60 raw HTML literals, v1.75
safe `br` / `kbd` / `sub` / `sup`, and v1.41 hard breaks
stand. Streaming LEFT bubbles stay plain text. Copy on
the message still copies markdown source.

Out of scope: rendering arbitrary HTML, VNC / full Box
GUI, extra channel types, public store, coding-agent,
new surfaces, a new HTTP route, bumping OpenAPI past
0.18.0, a second PR. `workspaceTree.ts` (never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`) stands.

OpenAPI **0.18.0** (not bumped).

## 188. v1.88 — cmark-gfm list item edges in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked cmark-gfm 0.29.0.gfm.13 on main before this cut.
These already matched on both clients and stay as they are:

- Content indentation follows the W+N rule. One to four
  spaces after the marker are padding. Five or more keep
  one space as the separator and the rest as content, so
  `-     foo` is indented code inside the item. A marker
  line with no text uses padding of one. A second blank
  line before any text ends the item (`-` / blank / `  foo`
  leaves `foo` outside).
- An empty item stays in the list (`- foo` / `-` / `- bar`,
  and the same with ordered markers). An empty marker does
  not interrupt a paragraph (`foo` / `*`, `foo` / `1.`,
  `foo` / `1.` / `   text`).
- An ordered marker has at most nine digits.
  `123456789.` is a list. `1234567890.` is not. `0.` and
  `003.` do not interrupt a paragraph. `1.` and `1)` do.
  Any start number begins a list after a blank line, a
  heading, a fence, or at the start of the chunk.
- Changing the bullet (`-`, `*`, `+`) or the ordered
  delimiter (`.` versus `)`) starts a new list. A marker
  in columns 0–3 ends the item, so `- foo` / `2. bar` and
  `- foo` / `*` are two lists. `10) foo` / `   - bar` is
  two lists. `10) foo` / `    - bar` is a nested list.
- Tight versus loose follows blank lines between items or
  between an item's direct children. A nested list has its
  own looseness. A blank inside a fence, indented code, or
  block quote does not loosen the parent. `- > foo` /
  `  >` / `  > bar` is a tight list.
- An ordinary lazy line stays in the item (`- foo` / `bar`,
  and a partial indent). A heading or fence indented to the
  content column is a real block inside the item. `* * *`
  is a thematic break. `+ + +` is nested empty lists.
  Sibling items may be indented up to the parent's content
  column; one more indent is indented code outside the list.
  Streaming stays plain text. Copy on the message still
  copies markdown source.

The unshipped gap was two list-item edges, not a new list
renderer. A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now
matches cmark-gfm 0.29.0.gfm.13 on both. Desktop
`markdownList.ts` and iOS `MarkdownList.swift` share the
rule. Desktop react-markdown already matched the lazy
cases; it still leaves `01.` in a paragraph, so that
reply is drawn through the list splitter. iOS always
uses the splitter.

- The ordered start is the integer value. `Foo` / `01. bar`
  / `02. baz`, `Foo` / `01) bar`, `Foo` / `001. bar`, and
  `Foo` / `000000001. bar` are a paragraph plus a list
  that starts at 1. `- foo` / `  01. bar` is a nested
  ordered list. This supersedes only the digit-string
  part of v1.56, which kept `01.` in the paragraph.
  `9.`, `14.`, `0.`, `00.`, `003.`, and `2)` still stay
  in the paragraph. Ten digits (`0000000001.`) are not a
  marker. A list that already starts at `01.` with no
  open paragraph is unchanged. `01.` inside a fence stays
  code.
- A line whose indent is at least 4 and still short of
  the item's content column is a lazy continuation when
  the paragraph is open. It is not a new block. The
  CommonMark example `- a` / ` - b` / `  - c` / `   - d`
  / `    - e` is one list of four items, and the last
  item is `d` then `- e`. The same rule keeps a 4-space
  `#`, `***`, fence, `>`, `<div>`, `---`, `==`, or
  `01.` inside the item. The stored line escapes that
  block start (a backslash before `#`, `*`, a fence,
  `>`, `<`, `-`, or `=`, or `01\.`) so the next parse on either
  client keeps the characters in the paragraph. A type 7
  tag such as `<br>` is not escaped. A lazy `|` still
  takes the v1.86 table mark. A blank line after that
  lazy text, then a line indented to the content column,
  makes the list loose. After a blank line, a 4-space
  line that is short of the content column ends the item
  and is indented code outside it.

### Prior slices

v1.36 ordered start numbers, v1.51 tight and loose lists,
v1.54 marker padding, v1.57 bullet characters, v1.59
ordered tasks, v1.72 tabs in item code, and v1.86 lazy
table lines stand. v1.56 stands except the digit-string
interrupt this section supersedes. Streaming LEFT bubbles
stay plain text. Copy on the message still copies
markdown source.

Out of scope: hard line breaks inside block quotes, tab
stops in the rest of the block structure, rendering
arbitrary HTML, VNC / full Box GUI, extra channel types,
public store, coding-agent, new surfaces, a new HTTP
route, bumping OpenAPI past 0.18.0, a second PR.
`workspaceTree.ts` (never reintroduce `computerPane.ts`)
stands.

OpenAPI **0.18.0** (not bumped).

## 189. v1.89 — cmark-gfm tab stops when a container marker partly consumes a tab (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked cmark-gfm 0.29.0.gfm.13 on main before this cut.
CommonMark section 2.2 (tabs advance to the next column of
4) already matched on both clients and stays as it is:

- A tab at column 0 is indented code. `\tfoo\tbaz` is a
  code block whose text is `foo`, a tab, and `baz`. Two
  spaces and then a tab are the same four columns, so
  `  \tfoo\tbaz` is that same code block. A tab inside the
  code stays a tab.
- `- foo` / blank / `\tbar` is a second paragraph of the
  item. The tab is not four columns past the item text.
- `>\t\tfoo` is a block quote whose content is indented
  code. The optional column after `>` consumes one column
  of the first tab, and the code text is two spaces then
  `foo`. `-\t\tfoo` is a list item whose content is that
  same indented code.
- `#\tFoo` is an h1. `*\t*\t*\t` is a thematic break.
  A tab cannot be the indent of a heading, a break, a
  quote marker, or a fence: four columns is indented code.
- Those stops were already shipped for indented code
  (v1.48), space-indented fences (v1.53), list padding
  (v1.54), indented code inside a list item (v1.72), and
  block quotes (v1.73).

The unshipped gap was a fence whose indent is a tab that
a container marker only partly consumed. cmark-gfm's
`fence_offset` counts whitespace bytes, and each byte is
one column. Micromark counted the whole tab as one column
and dropped the leftover spaces. A completed assistant
LEFT `kind=message` now keeps those spaces.

- `>\t\`\`\`` / `>\tcode` / `>\t\`\`\`` is a quote
  whose code text is a space, then `code`. The optional
  column after `>` consumes one column of the tab. That
  tab is still one byte of fence indent, so stripping it
  leaves the one leftover column as a space. A second
  tab on a content line stays a tab after that space:
  `>\t\tline` is a space, a tab, and `line`. A tilde
  fence follows the same rule. Info strings stay info
  strings, including `\`\`\`js`.
- `>>\t\`\`\`` strips the inner marker through the rest
  of that tab, so the code text is `code` with no extra
  space. Three spaces before `>\t\`\`\`` leave two
  spaces in the code.
- `- foo` / `\t\`\`\`` / `\tcode` / `\t\`\`\`` is a
  list item whose code text is a space, then `code`.
  `1. foo` consumes three columns of the tab, and the one
  remaining column is the whole fence indent, so
  `\tcode\tX` keeps the tab and adds no space.
  `10. foo` consumes the tab completely. That fence was
  already correct and stays on the space-indent path.
- A list inside a quote owns the tabbed fence. Measured
  from the quote column, the same tab would add a space.
  `> - foo` / `>\t\`\`\`` / `>\tcode` / `>\t\`\`\``
  is code text `code`.
- A non-blank line indented less than the item ends the
  open fence and the item. `- foo` / `\t\`\`\`` /
  `code` / `\t\`\`\`` is an empty code block inside the
  item, then a paragraph `code` and the closing marker
  outside it.
- A space-only fence indent is unchanged, including
  `> \`\`\`` / `>\t\tcode` / `> \`\`\``. Four or more
  columns stay indented code, not a fence.

### Prior slices

v1.48 indented-code tabs, v1.53 indented fences, v1.54
list padding, v1.72 list-item indented-code tabs, and
v1.73 blockquote tabs stand except where a fence's byte
offset differs from the tab's column width. v1.88 list
item edges stand. Streaming
LEFT bubbles stay plain text. Copy on the message still
copies markdown source.

Out of scope: hard breaks, entities, VNC / full Box GUI,
extra channel types, public store, coding-agent, new
surfaces, a new HTTP route, bumping OpenAPI past 0.18.0,
a second PR. `workspaceTree.ts` (never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`) stands.

OpenAPI **0.18.0** (not bumped).

## 190. v1.90 — cmark-gfm entity and numeric character references in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked cmark-gfm 0.29.0.gfm.13 on main before this cut.
CommonMark section 2.5 (entity and numeric character
references) already matched on both clients for ordinary
text and stays as it is:

- Named entities decode, including `&nbsp;` `&amp;`
  `&copy;` `&AElig;` `&Dcaron;` `&frac34;`
  `&HilbertSpace;` `&DifferentialD;`
  `&ClockwiseContourIntegral;` and `&ngE;` (U+2267 plus
  U+0338). The lookup is the HTML5 set cmark-gfm ships
  (2125 names).
- Decimal and hex numeric references decode: `&#35;`
  `&#1234;` `&#992;` `&#X22;` `&#XD06;` `&#xcab;`.
  `&#0;` and an out-of-range, surrogate, or noncharacter
  code point are U+FFFD. Eight or more decimal digits
  and seven or more hex digits stay the characters
  (v1.69), so `&#87654321;` is not U+FFFD.
- An invalid reference stays literal: `&nbsp` without a
  semicolon, `&x;` `&#;` `&#x;` `&#abcdef0;`
  `&ThisIsNotDefined;` `&hi?;` and `&copy` with no
  semicolon.
- A reference is not markdown syntax. `&#42;foo&#42;`
  is the characters `*foo*`. `&#42; foo` is not a list
  item. `foo&#10;&#10;bar` stays one paragraph.
  `&#9;foo` is not indented code. `&#35;` at the start
  of a line is not a heading.
- Code spans, fenced code bodies, and indented code
  blocks stay literal, so `` `&amp;` `` and a fence
  body of `&ouml;` show the source characters.
- `[a](url &quot;tit&quot;)` is not a link. The quotes
  decode and the brackets stay text.
- Link and image destinations already decoded the full
  set (v1.79). A reference-definition title already
  decoded `&ouml;` through the platform parser.

The unshipped gap was two label paths that clipped or
rewrote a reference before the parser could finish it.

- An inline link or image title used the short name
  list. `&ouml;` was not a name, so the rewriter turned
  the ampersand into `&#38;` and the title stayed the
  characters `f&ouml;&ouml;`. A title now uses the same
  HTML5 table as a destination. `[foo](/f&ouml;&ouml;
  "f&ouml;&ouml;")` opens `/f%C3%B6%C3%B6` with title
  `föö`. `&ngE;` in a title is the two characters
  `≧̸`. An image title follows the same rule. An image
  alt does too, because that pass shares the lookup.
- A fenced code info string already decoded inside the
  parser (`class="language-föö"`), but the language
  label kept only the ASCII run, so the chip read `f`.
  The label now keeps the decoded word, including
  `föö`, `foo+bar`, and an over-long
  `foo&#87654321;`. The code body on the next lines
  stays literal. iOS decodes that info string only
  after the reply is complete: entities first, then a
  backslash before ASCII punctuation, so `foo\+bar`
  is `foo+bar` the way the desktop parser already
  rendered it. A streaming fence keeps the source
  word. `mermaid` is still the diagram language after
  that decode.

### Prior slices

v1.40 backslash escapes and the common named set, v1.61
references inside raw HTML, v1.69 over-long numeric
references, and v1.79 destination entities stand.
Streaming LEFT bubbles stay plain text. Copy on the
message still copies markdown source.

Out of scope: VNC / full Box GUI, extra channel types,
public store, coding-agent, new surfaces, a new HTTP
route, bumping OpenAPI past 0.18.0, a second PR.
`workspaceTree.ts` (never reintroduce `computerPane.ts`)
stands.

OpenAPI **0.18.0** (not bumped).

## 191. v1.91 — cmark-gfm backslash escapes in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked cmark-gfm 0.29.0.gfm.13 section 2.4 (backslash
escapes) on main before this cut. These already matched
on both clients and stay as they are:

- Every ASCII punctuation character after a backslash
  becomes that character and is not markdown syntax.
  `\(` and `\)` stay math delimiters, taken out before
  this pass (v0.46). The other thirty punctuation
  characters render as themselves.
- A backslash before anything else stays a backslash.
  `\→\A\a\ \3\φ\«` shows those backslashes.
- `\\*emphasis*` is a backslash and then emphasis.
- A backslash at the end of a line is a hard break.
  `foo\` then `bar` breaks. The same marker at the end
  of a block stays a visible backslash: `foo\`,
  `foo\` then a blank line, `### foo\`, and `foo\`
  then `---`. It is not a break inside a code span,
  a fence, or indented code.
- The same hard break already fires inside a blockquote
  and a list item, including a lazy continuation
  (`> foo\` then `bar`, `- foo\` then `  bar`). A
  backslash at the end of the quote or the item stays.
- Code spans, fenced code, and indented code keep
  `\*` and `\[\]`.
- Escapes already work in link destinations and titles.
  `[foo](/bar\* "ti\*tle")` opens `/bar*` with title
  `ti*tle`. A reference definition does the same.
  `/url\bar\*baz` with title `foo\"bar\baz` is href
  `/url%5Cbar*baz` and title `foo"bar\baz`.
- A fence info string already unescapes punctuation.
  `` ``` foo\+bar `` is `language-foo+bar`. `foo\bar`
  stays `foo\bar`.

The unshipped gap was three places that still treated a
backslash as an escape, or dropped it while matching a
label. A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now
matches cmark-gfm there.

- An angle autolink is copied whole. `<http://example.com?find=\*>`
  is a link whose href is `http://example.com?find=%5C*`
  and whose text is `http://example.com?find=\*`.
  `&amp;` inside that URL stays the characters `&amp;`.
  `\<http://example.com>` is not a link. `<user\@example.com>`
  is the characters `<user@example.com>`, not a mailto
  link, because the backslash is outside an autolink and
  `@` is punctuation.
- A backslash inside raw HTML stays. `foo <a href="\*">`
  and `<a href="/bar\/)">` show those characters, and
  they are not a live tag. `<div>` then `foo\*` then
  `</div>` keeps the backslash. A character reference
  inside the tag still decodes, so `foo <a href="&ouml;">`
  shows `ö` (v1.61). `\<br/> not a tag` is the
  characters `<br/> not a tag`. A bare `<br>` is still
  the safe line break.
- A reference label matches the source text, not the
  parsed inlines. `[bar][foo\!]` does not match
  `[foo!]`. `[foo\bar]` does not match `[foobar]`.
  `[foo\\bar]` does not match `[foo\bar]`.
  `[foo\!]` does match `[foo\!]`, and the link text
  parses the escape, so the visible word is `foo!`.
  `[FOO\bar]` matches `[foo\bar]`. `[foo][ref\[]`
  matches `[ref\[]`.

### Prior slices

v1.40 backslash escapes, v1.41 hard breaks, v1.43 angle
autolinks, v1.60 raw HTML as text, v1.61 references
inside a tag, v1.74 and v1.78 titles, v1.79 destinations,
v1.81 reference-definition edges, and v1.90 fence info
strings stand. Streaming LEFT bubbles stay plain text.
Copy on the message still copies markdown source.

Out of scope: VNC / full Box GUI, extra channel types,
public store, coding-agent, new surfaces, a new HTTP
route, bumping OpenAPI past 0.18.0, a second PR.
`workspaceTree.ts` (never reintroduce `computerPane.ts`)
stands.
