## 192. v1.92 — cmark-gfm link reference definitions in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked cmark-gfm 0.29.0.gfm.13 sections 4.7 (link
reference definitions) and 6.3 (full, collapsed, and
shortcut reference links and images) on main before this
cut. These already matched on both clients and stay as
they are:

- A definition may split the label, the destination, and
  the title onto their own lines. `[foo` then `bar]: /url`
  then `"title"` defines `foo bar`. Whitespace inside the
  label collapses, including a line break.
- A definition cannot interrupt an open paragraph. That
  includes a lazy blockquote continuation (`> foo` then
  `[bar]: /url`) and a lazy or indented list continuation
  (`- foo` then `[bar]: /url`, `- foo` then `  [bar]: /url`).
  A blank line, an ATX heading, a thematic break, a fence,
  or a setext underline closes the paragraph first, so
  `Foo` then `---` then `[bar]: /url` is a heading and a
  definition. `===` alone does not.
- The first definition for a label wins. A later one is
  removed and does not replace it.
- Labels match with the Unicode case fold and collapsed
  whitespace. `ẞ`, `ß`, and `SS` match. `ς` matches `Σ`.
- A backslash in a label is part of the label (v1.91).
  `[bar][foo\!]` does not match `[foo!]`. `[foo\!]`
  matches `[foo\!]`.
- A label of 1000 characters matches. 1001 stays literal.
  cmark-gfm uses that limit. The spec sentence says 999.
- Full `[text][ref]`, collapsed `[ref][]`, and shortcut
  `[ref]` already resolve, including images. An inline
  destination beats a shortcut. An undefined full label
  does not fall back. A title's newlines are collapsed
  to one space for the tooltip (v1.78).
- A definition on its own quote line after other quote
  text (`> See [docs][ref].` then `>` then `> [ref]:`)
  already applied to the whole message.

The unshipped gap was three edges. A completed assistant
LEFT `kind=message` now matches cmark-gfm there.

- An empty pointy destination is a real destination.
  `[foo]: <>` then `[foo]` is a link with an empty href.
  `[foo]: <> "t"` keeps the title, written back as
  `[foo](<> "t")` so the title is not read as the
  destination. `[foo]: <>` with no reference contributes
  nothing.
- A title that parses on a later line, then has more
  text on that line, ends the definition at the
  destination and keeps the title. `[foo]: /url` then
  `"title" ok` is a link titled `title`, and the line
  `"title" ok` stays. The same junk on the destination's
  line (`[foo]: /url "title" ok`) is not a definition.
  An unclosed title (`"title` with no closer) ends the
  definition at the destination and drops the title.
- A definition that is the body of a list item or a
  blockquote applies to the whole message. `- [foo]: /url`,
  `2. [foo]: /url`, `- - [foo]: /url`, `> [foo]: /url`,
  and `> - [foo]: /url` all define `foo`. The marker
  stays (`- `, `> `, `> - `) so the empty item or quote
  still renders. A destination or title may sit on the
  next indented or quoted line. A lazy continuation that
  is itself a definition is taken too (`- [foo]: /a`
  then `[bar]: /b` defines both, and the item is empty).
  A lazy continuation that is ordinary text stays in
  the item (`- [foo]: /url` then `bar`). A definition
  still cannot interrupt a paragraph that already has
  text (`- foo` then `  [bar]: /url`).

### Prior slices

## 193. v1.93 — cmark-gfm setext headings and thematic breaks in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked cmark-gfm 0.29.0.gfm.13 sections 4.1 (thematic
breaks) and 4.3 (setext headings) on main before this
cut. These already matched on both clients and stay as
they are:

- `***`, `---`, and `___` with up to three leading spaces,
  spaces or tabs between the marks (`* * *`, `*\t*\t*`),
  and trailing spaces are a rule. Four spaces (`    ***`)
  is indented code. Mixed characters (`*-*`) and a short
  run (`--`) are not a rule.
- `---` after a paragraph is an h2. `***` after a paragraph
  is a paragraph and a rule. `Foo` then `- - -` is a
  paragraph and a rule. A multi-line paragraph plus `===`
  or `---` is one heading. Trailing spaces or tabs on the
  underline are fine. Internal spaces (`= =`, `--- -`)
  are not an underline. A line of `=` with nothing above
  it stays text. A single `-` after a paragraph is an h2.
- A setext underline cannot be a lazy continuation. `> foo`
  then `bar` then `===` stays inside the quote. `> Foo`
  then `---` is a quote and a rule. `- Foo` then `---` is
  a list and a rule. `- Foo` then `--` stays text in the
  item. An underline indented to the item text is a heading
  (`- Foo` then `  ---`).
- `- - -` is a thematic break and takes precedence over a
  list item. `* Foo` then `* * *` then `* Bar` ends the
  list, draws the rule, and starts a new list. `- Foo`
  then `- * * *` is a rule inside the second item.
- An ATX heading, a block quote, a list item of value 1,
  a fence, or a thematic break in the first three columns
  interrupts a paragraph, so the following underline is
  not a heading of that paragraph. Four spaces of indent
  on `---` under an open paragraph stays paragraph text.

The unshipped gap was two edges. A completed assistant
LEFT `kind=message` now matches cmark-gfm there.

- A continuation whose indent is four columns, counting a
  tab stop, is indented code when no paragraph is open.
  `# Hi` then a space and a tab then `---` then `---` is
  an h1, a code block whose text is `---`, and a rule.
  Two spaces or three spaces before that tab are the same.
  A leading tab is the same. `    code` then a space and a
  tab then `---` then `---` is one code block `code` /
  `---` and a rule.
- The same four-column line under an open paragraph stays
  heading text. The heading is inline, so a `-`, `=`,
  `***`, `#`, `>`, list marker, or fence on that line
  stays the characters. `Foo` then four spaces then `-`
  then `---` is an h2 whose second line is `-`, not a
  nested heading. `Foo` then a tab then `***` then `===`
  is an h1 whose second line is `***`, not a rule.
  `*bar*` and `***bar***` on a heading line still
  emphasize. A newline inside a code span is a space, so
  a heading whose code span contains a line of `***`
  renders `a *** b` inside the code, not a rule. An
  indented line of three backticks, then `bar`, then
  another indented line of three backticks, is that
  code span, not a fence.

## 194. v1.94 — cmark-gfm emphasis delimiter runs in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce

untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked cmark-gfm 0.29.0.gfm.13 section 6.4 (section 6.2 in
newer CommonMark numbering) on main before this cut. These
already matched on both clients and stay as they are:

- Rule of 3. `*foo**bar**baz*` is emphasis around a strong.
  `*foo**bar*` is one emphasis and the inner `**` stays.
  `foo***bar***baz` is emphasis around a strong. A closer
  that can also open, or an opener that can also close,
  does not match when the sum of the two run lengths is a
  multiple of 3 unless both lengths are multiples of 3.
- Punctuation flanking. `a*"foo"*` stays literal.
  `foo-_(bar)_` and `*(bar)*.` and `_(bar)_.` emphasize.
  v1.68 symbol punctuation stands (`*£*bravo` stays literal,
  `*£50*` emphasizes).
- Intraword emphasis. `foo*bar*` and `5*6*78` emphasize.
  `foo_bar_`, `5_6_78`, and `пристаням_стремятся_` stay
  plain. v1.67 `__init__` identifiers stay literal.
- Process-emphasis order. `**foo*bar**` is strong with a
  literal star. `*foo _bar* baz_` emphasizes `foo _bar` and
  leaves `baz_` plain. `***foo** bar*` is emphasis around
  strong. `*_foo_*` stays emphasis inside emphasis.

The unshipped gap was two edges. A completed assistant
LEFT `kind=message` now matches cmark-gfm there.

- Whitespace that is not a cmark space does not block
  flanking. cmark-gfm's space set is Unicode Zs plus tab,
  line feed, form feed, and carriage return. U+000B,
  U+0085, U+2028, U+2029, and U+FEFF are not in that set.
  `*\u000ba\u000b*`, and the same shape with those other
  four characters, is emphasis. `a*\u2028foo*` emphasizes
  the separator and `foo`. NBSP and em space stay
  whitespace, so `*\u00a0a\u00a0*` stays literal. The same
  rule is what the image-alt scanner uses. A character
  inside code, a link destination, or a URL stays as written.
- Nested strong is one `<strong>`. `****foo****`,
  `____foo____`, and `******foo******` are one strong.
  `_____foo_____` is emphasis around one strong.
  `foo******bar*********baz` is one strong `bar` and three
  literal stars. `__foo, __bar__, baz__` is one strong.
  Emphasis inside emphasis stays nested.

## 195. v1.95 — cmark-gfm indented code blocks in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce

untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /

Checked cmark-gfm 0.29.0.gfm.13 section 4.4 (Indented code
blocks) on main before this cut, through the same prepare
path and remark plugins a completed reply uses, and the
iOS split (`MarkdownList` then `MarkdownIndentedCode`).
These already matched on both clients and stay as they are:

- Four spaces or a tab opens a code block. Further indent
  stays in the text. `    a simple` / `      indented code
  block` is that block. A first line indented more than
  four spaces keeps the extra. A tab and a space/tab mix
  that lands on column 4 are the same indent. Internal
  tabs stay tabs.
- Chunks separated by blank lines are one block. A blank
  line of four columns or fewer is an empty line. Trailing
  blank lines, including ones with spaces, are not part of
  the block. Leading blank lines are not either. Trailing
  spaces on a code line stay.
- An indented line cannot interrupt a paragraph. `Foo` /
  `    bar` is one paragraph. A paragraph may follow the
  code immediately.
- List-item content wins. `  - foo` / blank / `    bar` is
  two paragraphs, not code. `1.  foo` / blank / `    - bar`
  is a nested list. A tab continuation of a list item is
  the same rule.
- A heading, a setext heading, a thematic break, or a
  fence may sit next to indented code. `# Heading` then
  `    foo` then a setext heading then `    foo` then
  `----` is h1, code, h2, code, rule. Four spaces before
  a fence is indented code whose text is the fence, not a
  fenced block. A real fence does not swallow a following
  indented block.
- The code text is literal. `<a/>`, `*hi*`, and `- one`
  inside the block stay characters.

The unshipped gap was interior blank lines. A completed
assistant LEFT `kind=message` now matches cmark-gfm there.

- Whitespace past column 4 on an interior blank stays.
  `    chunk1` / six spaces / `      chunk2` is
  `chunk1`, two spaces, `  chunk2`. A line of five spaces
  keeps one. Four spaces or fewer become an empty line.
- A tab is a four-column stop on that blank too. Two tabs
  between code lines keep the second tab. A space, a tab,
  and a space keep the one column past the stop. Four
  spaces and a tab keep the tab.
- The same remainder is kept inside a list item. The item
  stores four spaces plus the columns past the code
  indent, so the indented-code pass (which starts at
  column 0) still matches. A blank that only separates a
  paragraph from code stays empty and is not code text.

## 196. v1.96 — cmark-gfm list items in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked cmark-gfm 0.29.0.gfm.13 section 5.2 (List items) on
main before this cut, through the same prepare path and
remark plugins a completed reply uses, and the iOS split
(`MarkdownList`, then the rest of the reply). These already
matched on both clients and stay as they are:

- A basic item holds the blocks under it: a paragraph, an
  indented code block, and a block quote. A marker needs a
  space or a tab. `-one` and `2.two` stay paragraphs.
- Indentation follows the content column. One to four
  spaces after the marker are padding. A later line that
  is short of that column ends the item. Five or more
  spaces on a line that has text keep one separator and
  the rest is content, so `1.      indented code` is code
  whose text starts with a space.
- A marker line with no text, then an indented line, is
  an item that starts with a blank line. `-` then `  foo`,
  a fence, or indented code stays in the item. A second
  blank line ends the item, so `-` then a blank then
  `  foo` is an empty item and a following paragraph.
- Empty items stay in the list. `- foo` / `-` / `- bar`,
  the same with spaces after the empty marker, and
  `1. foo` / `2.` / `3. bar` are three items. `*` alone
  and `1.` alone are empty items. An empty marker does
  not interrupt a paragraph (`foo` / `*` stays one
  paragraph).
- Nested markers parse as nested lists. `- - foo` is a
  bullet inside a bullet. `1. - 2. foo` is an ordered
  item, a bullet, then an ordered item that starts at 2.
  `- -` is that same shape with an empty inner item.
  `- - -` stays a thematic break.
- Start numbers match. Up to nine digits, including `0.`
  and `003.` (start 3). Ten digits are not a marker.
  `-1.` is not a marker. `9.` and `0.` do not interrupt
  a paragraph. `01.` still does, because the integer is 1.
- A real hard break inside an item stays a break.
  `- foo  ` then `  bar`, and a backslash before the
  newline, render as `foo<br>bar`. The break at the end
  of the item, or before a blank line, does not: the
  spaces disappear and a final backslash stays visible.
  The same rules hold in a block quote.

The unshipped gap was the hard-break pass treating marker
padding as paragraph spaces. A completed assistant LEFT
`kind=message` now matches cmark-gfm there.

- Spaces after a marker that has no other text are
  padding, not a break. `-   ` then `  foo` is a one-item
  list, including two, four, or five spaces, `*`, `+`,
  and `1.`. `10.`, `0.`, and `003.` keep their start
  numbers. The same marker inside a quote, including a
  nested quote, stays a list. A tab after `*` is the
  same item.
- `foo` then `-   ` then `  bar` is a setext heading and
  a paragraph. The single dash is the underline. It is
  not a list and not a break.
- A marker whose text is indented code keeps the trailing
  spaces in the code. `1.     code  ` then `   more` is
  code `code  ` and then `more` in the same item.
  `-       bar  ` then `  baz` is code `  bar  ` and then
  `baz`. Neither is a `<br>`.

v1.57 bullet characters, and v1.88 `01.` stand. Streaming

Checked cmark-gfm 0.29.0.gfm.13 section 5.1 (Block quotes)
on main before this cut, through the same prepare path a
completed reply uses, and the iOS quote split. These already
matched on both clients and stay as they are:

- A basic quote holds the blocks under the marker. `>` may
  be followed by a space, or not. One to three spaces may
  indent the marker. Four spaces is indented code.
- Laziness. A paragraph line with no `>` stays in the open
  quote, including a nested quote (`> > > foo` then `bar`,
  and `>>> foo` / `> bar` / `>>baz`).
- Laziness does not apply to a thematic break, a list
  marker, indented code, or a fence. A blank line splits
  one quote from the next. `>` on its own is an empty line
  of the same quote.
- A tab after `>` still follows the tab-stop rule from
  v1.73. Hard breaks inside a top-level quote, and inside
  a list item, stay breaks.

The unshipped gap was a block quote marker in the first
three columns that is still indented to an open bullet
item. The quote split treated `  > bar` under `- foo` as
the next block, so the list ended and a hard break inside
the quote landed outside the item.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now keeps that
marker in the item.

- `- foo` then `  > bar` is one bullet whose body is a
  paragraph and a quote. `*`, `+`, and three spaces of
  indent do the same. A later sibling (`- baz`) stays in
  the same list.
- The line may be lazy (`  baz`, or `baz` with no indent)
  and it stays in the quote. A blank line makes the item
  loose; the quote is still inside it.
- A hard break in that quote stays in the item. Two spaces
  or a backslash before the newline render as `<br>`,
  including when the quote is nested under another quote
  (`> - foo` / `>   > bar  ` / `>   baz`).
- A marker short of the item's content column still ends
  the list. `- foo` then `> bar`, or one space then `>`,
  is a list followed by a quote.
- A task item keeps the same line. `- [ ] foo` then
  `  > bar` stores `foo` and `> bar` as the item text.
- An ordered item already kept its quote (`1. foo` then
  `   > bar`). That is unchanged.

## 198. v1.98 — cmark-gfm fenced code that is a list item in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked cmark-gfm 0.29.0.gfm.13 on main before this cut,
through the same prepare path a completed reply uses.
These already matched on both clients and stay as they are:

- Spec 5.1 lazy continuation. A paragraph line with no `>`
  stays in the open quote. Laziness does not start a
  thematic break, a list, a fence, or a heading, and it
  does not cross a blank line. Nested quotes keep the
  lazy line in the innermost paragraph. A quote nested in
  a list item keeps the same rule.
- Spec 6.7 hard breaks. Two trailing spaces or a backslash
  before the newline are a break inside a block quote and
  inside a list item.

The unshipped gap was a fenced code block that is the text
of a list marker, when the closing fence sits in the first
three columns. Scanners that only see a fence at the
margin treated `- ``` ` as ordinary text and `  ``` ` as a
new top-level fence. The list item's code was empty, an
extra fence followed the list, escapes and hard breaks
inside the code became tokens, and a blank line after the
list did not split bubbles because that false fence never
closed. `segments()` on iOS could take the closer as its
own code segment.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now keeps that
fence inside the item.

- `- ``` ` then `  foo` then `  ``` ` is one bullet whose
  body is a code block `foo`. `*`, `+`, `1.`, `1)`, `~~~`,
  and up to three spaces of indent do the same. A later
  sibling stays in the same list.
- A blank line inside the fence stays in the code, including
  CommonMark example 288 (`- a`, then a fence holding `b`
  and two blank lines, then `- c`).
- An ordered item may continue after the fence. `1. ``` `,
  `   foo`, `   ``` `, a blank line, and `   bar` is one
  item: code `foo`, then a paragraph `bar`. The blank does
  not split the bubble.
- A line shorter than the item's content column ends the
  item and the fence. `- ``` ` then `foo` is an empty code
  block and a following paragraph. No closer is appended.
- A column-0 fence after the item is a new top-level fence.
  `- ``` `, `  foo`, ` ``` `, `bar` is the list, then a
  code block `bar`. An unclosed top-level fence still gets
  a closer. An unclosed list fence does not.
- A closing line with info text does not close. `` ``` extra ``
  stays in the code. An info string on the opener
  (` ```js`, ` ``` extra`) is the language.
- A backslash escape and two trailing spaces inside the
  fence stay literal. They are not an emphasis marker or
  a hard break.
- The same fence under a quote (`> - ``` ` / `>   foo` /
  `>   ``` `) stays in the quoted item. The quote marker
  is peeled first, so the outer line is not a fence.

A fence indented four or more columns under a nested marker
is unchanged. Streaming LEFT bubbles stay plain text. Copy
on the message still copies markdown source.

## 199. v1.99 — cmark-gfm extended autolink trailing character references in completed assistant replies (locked)

Desktop + iOS. One impl PR. Spec rides this PR. **No second
PR.** OpenAPI stays **0.18.0**. Do not bump. No new HTTP
routes. Runtime idle. Clients only. Never reintroduce
`computerPane.ts`. Leave stale PRs #23, #24, and #130
untouched. Clients never speak MCP or the model. Mac oMLX /
runtime URL unchanged.

Checked cmark-gfm 0.29.0.gfm.13 on main before this cut,
through the same prepare path a completed reply uses.
These already matched on both clients and stay as they are:

- Spec 5.3. An ordered marker interrupts a paragraph only
  when its start is 1. A nested ordered list that starts
  at another number needs a blank line and indent to the
  content column. `01.` is start 1.
- Tagfilter. Raw HTML stays escaped, so the tagfilter
  extension does not change what a reader sees.
- GFM extended-autolink trailing punctuation, parenthesis
  balancing, `www.` boundaries, and an unknown letter
  tail such as `&hl;`. `&hl1;` keeps the digits and drops
  the semicolon. A trailing `]`, `}`, or curly quote on
  http(s) stays outside the link.
- GFM tables. An escaped pipe does not split a cell. A
  row is clipped to the header width. A delimiter row
  with the wrong column count is not a table.

The unshipped gap was a real HTML character reference on
a bare http(s) or `www.` autolink. The escape pass decoded
`&copy;` before the autolink scan, and the decoded
character was not punctuation, so it stuck to the URL.
cmark-gfm runs the delimiter on the source characters.
A trailing named reference is outside the link and then
decodes. A reference in the middle of the path stays the
source characters. `&#38;` is not a letter entity: only
the semicolon is trimmed, and `&#38` stays in the link.

A completed assistant LEFT `kind=message` now follows that
order.

- `https://example.com/a&copy;` is a link to
  `https://example.com/a` and then ©. The same for `www.`,
  `&amp;` (the link, then `&`), `&reg;` before a period,
  `&nbsp;`, `&quot;`, and `&hellip;`.
- `https://example.com/a&copy;b` is one link whose text
  is the characters `&copy;`, not ©. `&amp;` in the path
  stays `&amp;`.
- `https://example.com/foo&#38;` keeps `&#38` in the link
  and leaves `;` outside. It does not become `&`.
- `(https://example.com/a&copy;)` keeps both parentheses
  outside the link, with © between the link and the
  closer.
- `http://example.com/foo\)` keeps the backslash in the
  link and the `)` outside.
- `&hl;` still peels and stays the source characters.
  `&copy; 2026` with no URL still decodes.
- An angle autolink `<https://example.com/a&amp;>` is
  still copied whole. A link destination still keeps
  `&#38;` for the parser. A code span stays literal.

The source stash inside the autolink is a `§§SBL` token.
A decoded trailing reference stays a `§§SBE` token, which
the autolink scan peels. A second escape pass does not
change the text. Streaming LEFT bubbles stay plain text.
Copy on the message still copies markdown source.

v1.62 trailing punctuation, v1.71 path characters, v1.85
domain and `&hl;` rules, and v1.91 angle autolinks stand.
